# Triya CAD 2D — Roadmap and Task Board

This file is the **work queue** for Claude Code threads (see `CLAUDE.md` → Autodrive protocol). Milestones run in order; tasks within a milestone can run in parallel unless a `blocked-by` is listed. Threads claim tasks by editing the checkbox and append the PR number on completion.

Legend: `[ ]` open · `[~] (thread: name)` claimed · `[x] (#73)` done · `[-]` dropped (reason in DECISIONS.md)

Every task has: **ID**, **title**, **acceptance** (what the test proves), optional **blocked-by**. Add new tasks you discover to the right milestone with a one-line *why*.

---

## M0 — Foundation and the acad-ts gate (go/no-go)

**Exit criteria:** monorepo builds and tests in CI; acad-ts spike report written; **owner decides go/no-go on acad-ts** (needs-human). No feature work beyond M0 starts until `M0-GO` is checked.

- [x] (#1) **M0-01** Monorepo scaffold — pnpm workspaces, turbo, TS strict, ESLint + `eslint-plugin-boundaries` with the dependency graph from `ARCHITECTURE.md` §2, Prettier, Vitest, Playwright, Husky pre-commit (lint+typecheck). **Acceptance:** `pnpm lint typecheck test` green on an empty engine package; boundary violation test fails lint.
- [x] (#4) **M0-02** CI workflow — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs lint/typecheck/unit/fidelity/perf/offline on PR and main; caches pnpm and Playwright; uploads fidelity diff images as artifacts. **Acceptance:** PR to main shows all jobs; a deliberately failing fidelity test blocks merge.
- [x] (#3) **M0-03** Vendor acad-ts as `third_party/acad-ts` git subtree at a pinned commit; build it into `packages/io` worker bundle; `PATCHES.md` created. **Acceptance:** `DwgReader` runs in Node test and in a Web Worker in a Vite dev build.
- [x] (#2) **M0-04** Corpus skeleton — `corpus/public/` with README, manifest schema (`corpus.json`: file, version, source, license, reference plot, tolerance, tags), `corpus/private/` gitignored; import the ezdxf and ACadSharp sample files that are redistributable with their licenses noted. **Acceptance:** `pnpm corpus:list` prints ≥ 40 files with versions R12–R2018.
- [x] (#5) **M0-05** Round-trip harness in `packages/testkit` — open → save (same version) → reopen → `structuralDiff(a, b)` over acad-ts object graphs (handles, types, properties, xdata, dictionaries, owners), with the allowed-exception list from `TESTING.md`. **Acceptance:** harness runs on all M0-04 files and emits a per-file diff report (failures allowed at this stage, they are the spike's data).
- [x] (#6) **M0-06** acad-ts spike per `docs/SPIKE_M0_ACAD_TS.md`: read + write every corpus file, DXF and DWG, all versions; categorise failures (parse error, entity not mapped, property loss, writer error); patch the top 5 defects; produce `docs/reports/M0-acad-ts-spike.md` with the go/no-go recommendation and a list of required patches. **Acceptance:** report committed; `needs-human` issue opened with the recommendation.
- [x] **M0-GO** Owner checks this box to release M1+. (needs-human) Checked 2026-11-01: owner accepted the M0-06 CONDITIONAL GO (#7). M1 read-only work is released; M1-05 and M2-13 stay blocked on M1-27 to M1-30.

## M1 — Fidelity core (open any drawing and display it as AutoCAD would)

**Exit criteria (DEC-047):** every public-corpus file opens in the app, renders without error and matches its reviewed Triya golden image (M1-24), DXF round-trips with diff = 0, and Triya-saved DXF passes the ezdxf audit (M1-105). No editing yet.

**Re-scope 2026-11-08 (Rule 0, DEC-047):** save is DXF only (AC1032) and no AutoCAD files are asked of the owner. Every "within tolerance of the AutoCAD reference" acceptance in M1 now means "matches its reviewed golden image" from M1-24; a `[~]` task whose only leftover is that acceptance closes when M1-24 covers its corpus files, and leftovers that waited on an owner sample are post-launch. M1-22's leftovers are M2 work (M2-01, M2-13). M1-75 takes no new parts after part 39 (#198): a missing writer is added, DXF only, by the M2+ task whose command first needs it. Tasks that waited only on AutoCAD files moved to "Post-launch: AutoCAD cross-compatibility" at the end of this file.

### Document model and I/O
- [x] (#10) **M1-01** Document model per `ARCHITECTURE.md` §4: types, immutable update helpers, handle allocator above `$HANDSEED`, `ChangeSet`, EntityId map. **Acceptance:** unit tests for allocation, change-set application/inversion, id mapping.
- [x] (#11, #12, #13) **M1-02** Geometry kernel `engine/geom`: points/vectors, lines, arcs (CCW), circles, ellipses, polylines with bulges, NURBS splines (evaluate/flatten), bounds, transforms, intersections (all pairs incl. spline via flattening), closest-point, tangents, offsets, tolerance model from extents. **Acceptance:** property-based + table tests; ≥ 95 % branch coverage.
- [x] (#14, #16) **M1-03** `AcadToTriya` mapping for tables (layers, linetypes, textstyles, dimstyles, mleaderstyles, tablestyles, appids, vports, views, ucs, blockrecords) and header vars. **Acceptance:** corpus tables compare equal to acad-ts values. *Done:* every table record, DIMVAR, multileader/table style field and header variable equals an independent DXF reader on every readable public ASCII DXF (`tables.corpus.test.ts`), and every acad-ts table entry of every readable file (DWG included) is mapped. What acad-ts itself does not read is listed in `ACAD_TS_GAPS` and moved to M1-32.
- [x] (#17, #19, #22, #25, #28, #29, #31, #34, #39, #40) **M1-04** `AcadToTriya` entities: LINE, XLINE, RAY, CIRCLE, ARC, ELLIPSE, POINT, LWPOLYLINE, POLYLINE(2D/3D-as-2D)+VERTEX, SPLINE, SOLID, TRACE, 3DFACE(as polyline), TEXT, MTEXT, ATTDEF, ATTRIB, INSERT (+MINSERT), DIMENSION (all subclasses), LEADER, MLEADER, HATCH, IMAGE, WIPEOUT, TABLE, VIEWPORT, TOLERANCE, MLINE, REGION(2D proxy graphics), PDFUNDERLAY/DWFUNDERLAY/DGNUNDERLAY, OLE2FRAME(bounds), PROXY_ENTITY(graphics). **Acceptance:** `FILE_FIDELITY.md` Read column ticked per type with a corpus file. *Done:* every listed type is mapped in plan view (DEC-039) and bounded in the spatial index; every entity of every readable corpus file is mapped or reported unmapped with a reason, and every ASCII DXF entity of a mapped type equals an independent group-code oracle (`entities.corpus.test.ts`), with acad-ts read losses ratcheted in `ACAD_TS_GAPS` and moved to M1-32. Types with no public corpus file (OLE2FRAME, ACAD_PROXY_ENTITY, DWF/DGN underlays) stay unticked: M1-40. Follow-ups: M1-36 table cell formats, M1-39 region spline edges.
- [x] (#118) **M1-05** Passthrough store and `TriyaToAcad` export starting from the retained original (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §6). **Acceptance:** round-trip diff = 0 on all corpus files (blocked-by M0-GO, M1-27, M1-28, M1-29, M1-30, M1-31, M1-33, M1-48, M1-49, M1-56). *Done (#118):* `exportDocument`/`triyaToAcad` apply the model's differences from the original's mapping by handle (update only the changed fields, delete with the owned extension dictionary, add under engine handles, `$HANDSEED` written back); `PassthroughStore` lists unmapped handles into `DocumentInit.passthrough`; the round-trip suite saves through it and gates zero differences on all 118 readable corpus files; a corpus test edits all ~14,500 writable entities and checks nothing else changes. Writers for the remaining types and non-entity changes: M1-75.
- [x] (#41) **M1-06** I/O worker + Comlink RPC; progress events; cancellation; memory release after job. **Acceptance:** `bench-100k.dwg` parses without blocking main thread (Playwright measures input responsiveness during load). *Done:* `readInWorker` runs each job in a fresh Comlink worker that is terminated when the job ends, with per-stage progress and `AbortSignal` cancellation (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §3). `tests/e2e/io/responsiveness.spec.ts` parses a generated 100k-entity DWG (1.6 MB, about 3 s in Chromium) while moving the pointer: the page's longest timer gap and input delay stay under 100 ms, against a control that shows a 600 ms main-thread block is caught. Running it on the owner's `bench-100k.dwg` moved to M1-42.
- [x] (#24) **M1-07** Spatial index (R-tree) per block record and per layout; incremental updates from `ChangeSet`. **Acceptance:** query 100k entities < 2 ms; invalidation tests.
- [x] (#203) **M1-105** DXF validity gate: a CI step (Python, `pip install ezdxf`, CI only, never shipped in the app; `ATTRIBUTION.md` line) loads every DXF the export corpus gate writes, the AC1032 save of each public corpus file unedited and edited, with `ezdxf.recover.readfile` and runs `doc.audit()`. **Acceptance:** 0 recover errors and 0 audit errors on every Triya-saved DXF, except errors ezdxf already reports on the source file itself (listed per file, each with its reason); a deliberately broken save (a dangling owner handle) fails the job. *Why:* DEC-047 replaces the owner's AutoCAD AUDIT (PRD G3, §11.4, T4) with an automated check. *Done (#203):* the required checks job runs `pnpm test` with `TRIYA_DXF_AUDIT_DIR`, so `testkit/src/dxf-audit` writes the AC1032 DXF save of each of the 106 readable corpus files unedited and `export.corpus.test.ts` with its edit, then `scripts/dxf-audit/audit.py` (ezdxf 1.4.5, pinned in `requirements.txt`, unit-tested in `test_audit.py`) loads all 212 with `recover` + `audit`: 0 findings except what ezdxf reports on the source too (`exceptions.json`: `valid_lineweights.dxf` lineweight, the R13/R14 binary DXFs' proxy objects) and one ezdxf limitation it verifies in the save (an extension dictionary on a DICTIONARY, `sample_AC1014.dwg`); a copy of a clean save with a dangling LAYOUT owner must fail first. It found two save bugs, fixed by acad-ts patches 0170 (ATTRIB/ATTDEF written with a second AcDbText, so ezdxf dropped every attribute) and 0171 (a visibility parameter read from DWG written as `92 NaN`).
- [x] (#207) **M1-107** acad-ts refuses to write a symbol table record whose name has `|` (`INamedCadObjectExtensions.isValidDxfName`), so a DXF save drops every xref-dependent `xref|symbol` record AutoCAD keeps for VISRETAIN (and a drawing that bound an xref with BIND Insert keeps none). Patch acad-ts to accept `|` in xref-dependent records (DXF 70 bit 16), with a round-trip test and the VISRETAIN layer in the M1-21 test set. *Why:* found by M1-21 part 1 (#206); a real VISRETAIN drawing loses its xref layer settings on save. *Done (#207):* acad-ts patch 0172 lets a record with the xref-dependent bit (a block record: its BLOCK's) have `|` in its name, each part still checked as a name, and its DXF reader keeps a DIMSTYLE's flags (the first 70, which it used only as a marker, so an xref-dependent dimension style read back as an ordinary one). `xref-parent.dxf` keeps a VISRETAIN `xref-child-tb|TB-LINES` layer, red where the child has it green, and the M1-21 fixture test loads the attachment with it.
- [x] (#211) **M1-108** Draw object transparency: an entity's DXF 440 (ByLayer from the layer's `AcCmTransparency` and, in a layout viewport, its M1-62 override; ByBlock from the INSERT), with TRANSPARENCYDISPLAY. **Acceptance:** a thread-authored DXF with transparent entities, layers and a viewport override matches its golden image. *Why:* found by M1-62: the renderer draws no transparency at all, so the viewport transparency overrides it maps have nothing to change. *Done (#211):* `resolveTransparency` resolves DXF 440 like colour (ByLayer from the layer's `AcCmTransparency`, or its VP override in a layout viewport; ByBlock from the INSERT, opaque outside a block; layer 0 in a block from the INSERT's layer), each primitive carries the opacity and the draw loop multiplies it into the canvas alpha with locked-layer fading, one transparent object per stroke batch so overlapping ones darken where they cross. `RenderStyle.showTransparency` false is TRANSPARENCYDISPLAY 0 (TRANSPARENCYDISPLAY is not a DXF header variable, so the app shows transparency, AutoCAD's initial 1; the TPY status-bar toggle comes with M2-04's toggles). New self-authored `transparency.dxf` (`scripts/corpus/transparency.py`) with its golden images; `viewport-overrides.dxf` Layout1 golden refreshed for its 50 % WALLS override.

### Text
- [x] (#30) **M1-08** SHX parser + stroke interpreter (shapes, unifont; bigfont minimal), glyph cache; user font store in OPFS with "Add fonts" drop. **Acceptance:** renders all glyphs of a user-dropped `romans.shx` identically to the reference (owner-provided reference PNG, private corpus) and the bundled fonts in CI. *Done (#30):* `parseShx` reads shapes 1.0/1.1, unifont and bigfont (minimal) files; the stroke interpreter covers codes 0–14 with exact arcs (bulges) and exact advances; glyphs are cached per font and character; tested on self-authored SHX files. The OPFS store, the "Add fonts" drop and the reference-render check moved to M1-37.
- [x] (#84) **M1-09** Bundled open stroke fonts: convert Hershey to Triya font JSON (LibreCAD `.lff` dropped: GPL, owner decision on #32, DEC-044); name map for common SHX names; attribution. **Acceptance:** text in corpus files with missing SHX renders with bundled fallback and the health panel lists the substitution. *Done (#84):* `pnpm fonts:build` converts the original Hershey occidental data (`assets/fonts/hershey/hershey.jhf`) into ten fonts (`oet-simplex`, `-duplex`, `-complex`, `-triplex`, `-italic`, `-script`, `-scriptc`, `-gothice`, `-gothicg`, `-gothici`) with printable ASCII, the Latin-1 accented letters, ° ± × ÷ · §, a `%%c` diameter sign and (simplex, complex) Unicode Greek; `loadStrokeFont` makes each an SHX font. `FontBook` draws a missing font with its Hershey equivalent (`SHX_EQUIVALENTS`), then FONTALT, then `oet-simplex`, and records each in `substitutions`. `text.corpus.test.ts` draws all 2,548 TEXT and 1,093 MTEXT with visible characters with the bundled fonts alone and checks every corpus style font is reported with its equivalent; `bundled-fonts.test.ts` fails when the committed fonts drift from the data. The health panel itself is M1-25, which lists `FontBook.substitutions`; the rest of the character repertoire is M1-59.
- [x] (#33) **M1-10** TTF pipeline via `opentype.js`: glyph paths, exact advance widths, style flags; system-font fallback in browser. **Acceptance:** width tests vs `opentype.js` reference. *Done (#33):* `parseTtf`/`TtfFont` (opentype.js 2.0) give glyph outlines in font units, exact advances by code point (kerning optional, off as in AutoCAD TEXT), bold/italic from OS/2, head and post, cap-height scaling; `TtfRegistry` resolves a family and style through injected `FontSource`s and fallbacks and reports substitutions. The browser's local-font source moved to M1-38.
- [x] (#36, #43) **M1-11** MTEXT formatting engine (full code set, columns, fields, `%%`, background mask), line breaking, justification. **Acceptance:** corpus MTEXT files render within tolerance; unit tests per code. *Done (#36, #43):* `parseMText` (part 1, #36) reads every AutoCAD format code, caret and `%%` codes (and TEXT contents, with `%%u`/`%%o`), stacks, `\p` paragraph properties and fields (shown by an injected evaluator, else `----`) into styled paragraphs; `layoutMText` (part 2, #43) breaks lines by width, aligns and justifies them, sets tabs, stacks, `\A`, line spacing (at least/exactly), columns and the background mask through an injected `MTextMeasure`. Tests per code, plus a corpus check that all 1,757 MTEXT-coded strings parse and lay out with no code left visible. The render-within-tolerance comparison moved to M1-41, since it needs the renderer and the fidelity harness.
- [x] (#218) **M1-110** Vertical text: draw MTEXT whose drawing direction is top to bottom (DXF 72 = 3, or 5 "by style" with the STYLE's vertical flag, 70 bit 4) and TEXT in a vertical STYLE as AutoCAD documents, only when the resolved font is an SHX font that supports vertical text (header mode 2, with the stroke `vertical` command that `shx/strokes.ts` now skips), and horizontally otherwise, as today. **Acceptance:** unit tests with a made-up mode-2 SHX font (Autodesk's are never committed) draw a vertical TEXT and MTEXT top to bottom and the same text in a Hershey or TrueType font horizontally; the spatial index bounds what is drawn; the 24 corpus MTEXTs that state 72 = 3 keep their golden images unless their font supports vertical text. *Why:* split from M1-41 (#215), whose layout draws every MTEXT horizontally. *Done (#218):* `ShxFont.supportsVertical` (font info mode 2) and `verticalGlyph` (the commands after code 14 run); `fontMeasure(…, { vertical: true })` measures down the column; the renderer lays vertical TEXT and MTEXT out with the MTEXT engine and draws it turned a quarter with each character upright from its vertical glyph: TEXT from its point (Left/Top), centred (Center/Middle) or ending there (Right/Bottom), Aligned and Fit scaled between its points; MTEXT lines as columns right to left, placed by the attachment point, not wrapped; any run in a font without vertical text, or a stack, draws the whole text horizontally. Bounds come from the same primitives (`text-vertical.test.ts`, on a made-up mode-2 font). AutoCAD documents none of the placement rules; they are our reading, to check against plots post-launch (M1-26). The corpus fonts have no vertical mode, so the 24 corpus MTEXTs with 72 = 3 keep their golden images.

### Rendering
- [x] (#44, #48, #49) **M1-12** `Renderer` interface + `Canvas2DRenderer` with scene cache, culling, LOD, transient layer, DPR handling, `RendererHost` main-thread fallback. **Acceptance:** interface tests; fallback renders identically (pixel test).
- [x] (#46, #51, #53, #215) **M1-13** Linetype engine (`.lin` parser incl. complex text/shape types; re-authored bundled set; LTSCALE/CELTSCALE/PSLTSCALE/MSLTSCALE), lineweight display, ACI/true-colour/colour-book resolution with ACI 7/0 rules, ByLayer/ByBlock, draw order. **Acceptance:** fidelity tests on linetype corpus files; colour-7 on light/dark canvas. *Part 1 (#46):* `render/src/style/`: `.lin` reader, A-type pattern fitting with text/shape placement and PLINEGEN, linetype scale (LTSCALE, object scale, PSLTSCALE, MSLTSCALE), computed ACI palette, ByLayer/ByBlock/layer-0 resolution of colour, linetype and lineweight, lineweight display width, and the bundled ISO 128 set; checked on all 473 corpus LTYPE records. *Part 2 (#51):* draw order: `mapSortEnts` and `drawOrder`, checked on the 75 corpus SORTENTSTABLEs. *Part 3 (#53):* wired into the M1-12 renderer: `createPaint` resolves colour, lineweight (LWDEFAULT) and linetype (LTSCALE × object scale); `tessellate` dashes strokes with their continuous path kept for LOD and picking; `Canvas2DRenderer` fits dashes on first display, shows model and layout lineweights the AutoCAD way, draws in SORTENTSTABLE order, and re-resolves on layer, linetype and LTSCALE changes. Left: the fidelity acceptance (needs M1-24 and the owner's `linetypes.dwg` from M1-26); complex-linetype text and shapes and XLINE/RAY linetypes are M1-45. *Closed (#215, DEC-047):* the fidelity acceptance is the M1-24 golden images (#204) of the corpus files with linetypes, and colour 7 on light and dark canvases is checked in `canvas2d.test.ts`; complex-linetype text and shapes and XLINE/RAY linetypes shipped as M1-45 (#111, #114); the owner's `linetypes.dwg` waited on M1-26, which is post-launch.
- [x] (#55, #139, #165, #215) **M1-14** Block rendering: nested inserts, scale/rotation/mirror, ByBlock resolution, attributes, `*U` dynamic blocks as static with badge metadata, MINSERT. **Acceptance:** block corpus files within tolerance. *Part 1 (#55):* `expandInsert` draws INSERT and MINSERT references through nesting, exact under rotation, mirroring and non-uniform scale, with ByBlock and layer-0 inheritance, AutoCAD's off/frozen insert-layer rules, linetypes scaled with the block, each block's own draw order, and re-drawing when a block changes. *Part 2 (#139):* attributes: an INSERT draws its ATTRIBs where they are stored (once for a MINSERT), multiline ones from their MTEXT, painted as objects inside the reference (ByBlock and layer 0 take the INSERT's); a block's ATTDEFs do not draw through a reference except a constant one, which shows its value; an ATTDEF outside a block shows its tag; ATTMODE 0/1/2 hides every attribute, shows the visible ones, or shows them all, and the spatial index bounds invisible attributes under ATTMODE 2 (re-bounding on an ATTMODE change) and a constant ATTDEF by its tag and value. `text.corpus.test.ts` checks all 100 corpus ATTRIBs draw, inside their INSERT's bounds. *Part 3 (#165):* dynamic-block badge metadata (FR-B3): `BlockDefinition.dynamic` marks a dynamic block definition (the `ACAD_ENHANCEDBLOCK` evaluation graph in its extension dictionary; a record that only keeps an AcDbDynamicBlockGUID is static) with the name AutoCAD shows (AcDbDynamicBlockTrueName, since R2000 saves dynamic blocks under `*U` names), `dynamicOf` links an anonymous `*U` state to its definition (AcDbBlockRepBTag), and engine `dynamicBlockOf` gives the dynamic block any reference draws; `dynamic-blocks.corpus.test.ts` checks every ASCII DXF against its own records (16 definitions, 56 states, 37 references) and each DWG against its DXF save. The badge itself shows in the Properties palette (M2-21). Left: sharing one block tessellation across its references if M1-44 profiling asks for it, and the fidelity acceptance (M1-24). *Closed (#215, DEC-047):* the fidelity acceptance is the M1-24 golden images (#204) of the corpus block files; sharing one block tessellation across references was not needed, since M1-44 (#122) met T3 without it (a `pnpm test:perf` regression would bring it back as its own task).
- [x] (#57, #215) **M1-15** Dimension rendering from stored `*D` blocks (no engine yet) incl. annotative representations. **Acceptance:** dim corpus files within tolerance. *Part 1 (#57):* `expandInsert` draws every DIMENSION and ARC_DIMENSION from its stored `*D` block in place, with the dimension's colour, linetype, lineweight and layer handed down as an INSERT does and arrowhead blocks nested; `blocks.corpus.test.ts` checks all 259 corpus dimensions and 479 inserts draw inside their spatial-index bounds. Left: annotative scale representations (each `ACDB_*DIMOBJECTCONTEXTDATA` holds its own block; acad-ts reads none of them and no public corpus file has one, so this needs an owner corpus file from M1-26 and the viewport scale from M1-18), dimension text (M1-41), and the fidelity acceptance (M1-24). *Closed (#215, DEC-047):* dimension text draws through the `*D` blocks since M1-41 part 1 (#67), and the fidelity acceptance is the M1-24 golden images (#204) of the corpus dimension files; annotative representations need `ACDB_*DIMOBJECTCONTEXTDATA`, which acad-ts does not read and no corpus file has, so they go with M1-63 (display) and M1-102 (save) on the post-launch list.
- [x] (#60, #63, #64, #215) **M1-16** Hatch rendering: `.pat` engine, bundled re-authored patterns, solid, gradient, user `.pat` drop, LOD thinning, hatch draw order. **Acceptance:** hatch corpus files within tolerance; ANSI31 at 1:1 pixel-matches reference. *Part 1 (#60):* `@triya/hatch` `hatchPattern` clips the pattern lines a HATCH stores to its boundary loops by island style (normal, outer, ignore), dashed from each line's own origin, with HPMAXLINES; the renderer draws them as one stroke and fills a too-dense pattern's area; `hatch.corpus.test.ts` checks all 140 corpus pattern hatches (133 draw lines; the other 7 have line spacing wider than their boundary) with every dash inside the hatched area by an independent ray cast. *Part 2 (#63):* gradient fills (`gradientRamp`: the nine ramps fitted to the boundary, angle and shift, one-colour tint) drawn as canvas gradients, and the LOD tint (a pattern family whose lines are under a pixel apart fills the area at its average ink); `hatch.corpus.test.ts` checks every corpus gradient's ramp spans its boundary. *Part 3 (#64):* `parsePat` reads AutoCAD's documented `.pat` format; `patternLines` places a pattern at a hatch's angle, scale and origin as AutoCAD stores it (checked against an AutoCAD-saved corpus ANSI31), `userDefinedLines` makes user-defined hatches, and `PatternLibrary` holds the bundled `assets/patterns/triya-iso.pat` (re-authored: ISO 128-50 section lining, grids, triangles, dots, dashes, running-bond brick, the ISO 128-20 line types as hatches) with user files winning by name; the renderer draws a hatch stored without pattern lines from it. Left: the user `.pat` drop in the app (M1-50, needs the M1-22 shell) and the fidelity acceptance (M1-24 with M1-26 references). *Closed (#215, DEC-047):* the user `.pat` drop shipped as M1-50 (#95); the fidelity acceptance, ANSI31 included, is the M1-24 golden images (#204) of the corpus hatch files; a comparison with AutoCAD plots is post-launch (M1-26).
- [x] (#95) **M1-50** User `.pat` drop: drop or pick `.pat` files in the app; their patterns join the renderer's `PatternLibrary` ahead of the bundled set (redrawing hatches stored without lines), persist in IndexedDB, and show in the health report's missing-patterns list when absent. **Acceptance:** Playwright drops a `.pat` and a hatch stored without lines redraws from it. *Why:* M1-16 part 3 built the reader and library, but the drop needs the app shell (M1-22). **Done (#95):** drop on the window or Settings > Hatch patterns (pick, list, remove), kept in IndexedDB, every open drawing redrawn; the missing-patterns list is the health report's (M1-25), which can compare hatch names against `patternLibrary(sources)`.
- [x] (#66, #215) **M1-17** Table rendering from stored cell geometry. **Acceptance:** table corpus files within tolerance. *Part 1 (#66):* the renderer draws each table's stored `*T` block at its insertion point along its direction (mirrored on a −Z plane), handing down colour and layer as an INSERT does, and the spatial index bounds a table by that block's extents and re-bounds it when the block changes; `blocks.corpus.test.ts` checks all 36 corpus tables draw inside their bounds. Left: cell text, which the block holds as MTEXT (M1-41), and the fidelity acceptance (M1-24 with M1-26 references). Tables without a `*T` block (ezdxf-written ones, which acad-ts drops anyway, M1-32) draw nothing until the table engine (M4-05) generates their graphics. *Closed (#215, DEC-047):* cell text draws from the block's MTEXT since M1-41 part 1 (#67), and the fidelity acceptance is the M1-24 golden images (#204) of the corpus table files. Tables without a `*T` block stay with M4-05.
- [x] (#82, #86, #88, #215) **M1-18** Layouts and viewports rendering: paper, printable area, viewports with scale/clip/VP layer overrides, annotative scale selection per viewport, `CANNOSCALE` in model. **Acceptance:** layout corpus files within tolerance; multi-viewport annotative test. *Part 1 (#82):* layouts carry their page setup (`mapLayouts`); `layoutSheet` places the paper and printable area (the sheet equals AutoCAD's stored limits on 70 corpus layouts), `viewportTransform` maps model space onto the sheet (view centre, height, target, twist, view direction), and the renderer draws a layout on its sheet with each on viewport's view of model space clipped to its frame, lineweights in the layout's paper units. *Part 2 (#86):* layers frozen in a viewport (341) are hidden in it, block parts and nested references included, and PSLTSCALE 1 draws viewport linetypes in paper units. Left, each waiting on a sample file or the fidelity harness: VP layer overrides and non-rectangular clips (M1-61 sample, then M1-62), annotative scale selection per viewport and `CANNOSCALE` in model (M1-63, needs `annotative-sheet.dwg` from M1-26), and the fidelity acceptance (M1-24, M1-26 layout plots). *Closed (#215, DEC-047):* VP layer overrides and non-rectangular clips shipped as M1-62 (#209, from a self-authored DXF); the fidelity acceptance is the M1-24 golden images (#204) of every corpus layout; annotative scale selection per viewport, `CANNOSCALE` representations in model and the multi-viewport annotative test are M1-63, on the post-launch list (its MSLTSCALE part, #128, shipped).
- [x] (#91, #94, #210) **M1-19** Images: PNG/JPEG/BMP/TIFF decode (TIFF via `utif`), clip, fade/contrast/transparency, frame. **Acceptance:** image corpus file within tolerance. *Part 1 (#91):* IMAGEDEF, RASTERVARIABLES and WIPEOUTVARIABLES mapped (`mapImageObjects`); IMAGE drawn from the app's decoded bitmap (`images` renderer option) with rectangular, polygon and inverted clips, IMAGEFRAME, brightness, contrast, fade and transparency (bitonal foreground in the image colour), outlined while no bitmap is loaded; WIPEOUT drawn as a background mask with WIPEOUTFRAME; corpus check over 85 images and 23 wipeouts. *Part 2 (#94):* `decodeRaster` reads BMP (1/4/8/16/24/32 bits, bit-field masks, bottom-up or top-down) and TIFF (UTIF.js; Deflate strips through an fflate-backed `pako` stand-in) itself and PNG, JPEG and GIF through the browser's codecs without colour conversion or premultiplying; one-bit BMP, TIFF and PNG are marked bitonal. `RendererHost.setImage` hands a bitmap to the render worker (buffer transferred) or the main-thread session; IMAGEQUALITY draft draws bitmaps unsmoothed. *Part 3 (#210):* image files dropped on the window (BMP, JPEG, PNG, TIFF, GIF) are matched to IMAGEDEFs by file name without folder or case (`apps/web/src/images.ts`), decoded once and handed to every canvas showing them, before or after the drawing opens, again after REGEN and when an edit brings definitions in; a definition saved unloaded shows its frame only, as in AutoCAD. Acceptance (Rule 0, in place of the owner sample M1-64): the thread-authored AC1032 drawing `packages/testkit/fixtures/raster/` (generator `fixture.ts`, `pnpm fixtures:raster`: PNG with alpha, 24-bit BMP and bitonal TIFF written byte by byte; transparency, a turned image with brightness, contrast and a polygon clip, fade with an inverted clip, a bitonal image in red, a missing file and an unloaded definition) is checked by `raster-images.spec.ts` and matches its golden image (`tests/e2e/fidelity/fixtures.spec.ts`). Finding files on disk and the References palette stay with M1-21; the missing file's name inside the frame is M1-109.
- [x] (#212) **M1-109** Missing image name: an IMAGE whose bitmap the app has not supplied (file not found, unreadable or unloaded) shows its file name inside its frame, as AutoCAD does, in the frame's colour and sized to the frame. **Acceptance:** image E of `packages/testkit/fixtures/raster/` shows `site-photo.jpg` in its frame and image A no text once its file is dropped; its golden image updated. *Why:* left from M1-19, which needs text in the renderer's image path, decided per frame since bitmaps arrive after tessellation (found while closing M1-19, #210). *Done (#212):* a loaded IMAGE without its bitmap (never supplied, unreadable, or not dropped yet) shows its file name without the folder inside its frame, in the frame's colour and the bundled default font, centred along U and as tall as 80 % of the frame's width and a quarter of its height allow, shrunk to fit inside a clip polygon; the glyphs are made at tessellation and skipped each frame the app has the bitmap. An unloaded definition shows its frame alone. `raster-images.spec.ts` checks A's and E's names and their going once checker.png is dropped; the raster fixture golden and the 21 corpus goldens with images were refreshed.
- [x] (#205, #213) **M1-20** PDF underlay via pdf.js (lazy): page render at viewport resolution, clip, fade; path extraction for osnap where available. **Acceptance:** a thread-authored AC1032 DXF attaching a PDF the test generates itself (two pages: vectors, fills, text, one raster; page 2 scaled and rotated; a polygonal clip) renders and matches its golden image; page cached. PDFs dropped on the window are matched to definitions by file name (finding files is M1-21). Osnap path extraction moved to M2-22.
- [x] (#206, #216) **M1-21** Xref and reference resolution: "Missing references" panel, file/folder drop, cached handles (IndexedDB), silent re-resolve, nested xrefs, overlay/attach, XCLIP, `name|layer` layers. **Acceptance:** a thread-authored AC1032 DXF xref set (`xref-parent.dxf` attaching and overlaying `xref-child-tb.dxf`, one nested child, an XCLIP, a PNG image) resolves and matches its golden images. The set gained an XCLIP (on the overlay), a PNG image in the title block and a grandchild placed by its INSBASE; its golden image is `packages/testkit/fixtures/xref/golden/model.png` (`tests/e2e/fidelity/fixtures.spec.ts`), app specs `xref.spec.ts` and `references.spec.ts`. RELOAD, UNLOAD, DETACH, BIND and repathing stay with the XREF manager (M5-04).
- [x] (#219) **M1-111** MULTILEADER leftovers from M1-52: draw block content with its attribute values (the MULTILEADER's own 330/177/44/302 records, not ATTRIBs), per-leader-line overrides (LEADER_LINE DXF 93 flags with their colour, linetype, lineweight, arrowhead and arrowhead size), and the text underline and overline attachments (5, 6, 8 and 10, see `engine/src/annotation/multileader.ts`), bounded in the spatial index by what they draw. **Acceptance:** a self-authored AC1032 DXF (a committed ezdxf script, with groups written by hand from the DXF reference where ezdxf cannot set them) holding each case maps, draws, round-trips with diff 0 and matches its reviewed golden image. *Why:* split from M1-52 (#215); no public corpus file has any of them, and Rule 0 lets a thread author the sample instead of waiting for one. *Done (#219):* the self-authored `corpus/public/self-authored/multileaders.dxf` (`scripts/corpus/multileaders.py`, ezdxf 1.4.5, the LEADER_LINE groups 170/92/340/171/40/341/93 written by hand) holds two balloon multileaders with attribute values (one turned 15° at 1.5×), three leader lines on one branch (as the multileader; overriding colour, linetype, lineweight, arrowhead and size; overriding path type and colour while storing unflagged values), underline attachments 5, 6 and 8 from both sides and the vertical overline and underline (10). acad-ts patch 0173 reads the attribute values and line properties from DXF and writes the line properties; 0174 reads the multileader's block and text rotations as radians (AutoCAD's unit; acad-ts took degrees, so the turned balloon came in at 0.26°). The mapping keeps the values and the flagged overrides, a DXF save writes them back, and `multiLeaderParts` draws them (attribute values as ATTRIBs of the content INSERT; underline and overline through the injected `TextMeasure`, as ezdxf's renderer draws them since AutoCAD documents neither). ### App shell (viewer-grade)
- [x] (#83, #92, #162, #171, #172, #217) **M1-22** Vite + React shell: open file (picker, drag-drop, file handler), document tabs, status bar, zoom/pan (wheel, middle-drag, Z/P transparent), ZOOM E/W/P/A/S/O, PAN, REGEN, VIEW save/restore; light chrome + dark canvas theme per `UI_SPEC.md`; OET home + Report issue buttons. **Acceptance:** Playwright opens corpus files via each route; pan/zoom perf ≥ 60 fps on the generated `bench-ga` drawing (DEC-045). *Part 1 (#83):* `apps/web` is a Vite + React 19 app. A drawing opens from the Open button or Ctrl+O (file picker), from a drop anywhere on the window, or from the installed app's file handler (`launchQueue`, manifest `file_handlers`), through one I/O worker job, `openInWorker` (`ioApi.open`: parse, then `documentInit`, model-space handle and extents), and shows on the dark model canvas through `RendererHost` in its own document tab (close shows the right neighbour, else the left). The canvas controller owns its canvas and input outside React: the wheel zooms at the cursor (1.25 per notch, ZOOMWHEEL 0 direction), a middle-button drag pans, a middle-button double click zooms to the extents (XLINE/RAY and text without bounds left out, header extents as the fallback; text counts by its glyphs since M1-35 part 2). The title bar has the OET home link, version badge and Report issue link; the status bar shows the cursor's X, Y, Z at `$LUPREC` and MODEL. Playwright opens a DXF and a DWG through each route and checks zoom, pan and extents through the status bar; the offline suite checks the app requests nothing outside its origin. *Part 2 (#92):* layout tabs (Model, then the layouts by tab order) each show their space through their own renderer, a layout as its white sheet on the `#8A8F98` surround, opening with the whole sheet in view, with PAPER in the status bar; `ioApi.open` returns extents per space. A navigation bar on the canvas gives PAN (left button until Esc), ZOOM Extents, Window (dragged or two clicks, rubber band on the transient layer), Previous (the last ten views per space; one wheel roll or drag is one step), All (limits ∪ extents), In and Out (Scale 2X / 0.5X), REGEN (a new renderer from the document, same view) and VIEW: the drawing's plan views restore (model-space views on Model, paper-space views in a layout; 3D or twisted views are listed but disabled), and views saved in the session restore and replace by name. *Part 3 (#162):* each space opens in the view saved with the drawing: Model at the first `*Active` VPORT's centre (plus the target, the centre being in display coordinates) with its height filling the window and the width following the window, as VIEWSIZE is the view's height; a layout at its paper-space viewport's (id 1) view centre and height (DXF 12 / 45, or the frame's 10 / 41 when no view height is stated, as acad-ts reads none from an R14 DWG). A drawing without a VPORT table, a 3D or twisted view, or a layout never shown (no paper-space viewport yet) opens at the extents or the whole sheet as before. The generated bench drawings save their `*Active` VPORT zoomed to the extents in the app's 1366 × 768 canvas, so the M1-60 gestures still start with every entity in view. *Part 4 (#171):* a drawing saved with tiled model-space viewports (several `*Active` VPORTs) opens with one canvas per viewport, placed by its DXF 10/11 corners (fractions of the drawing area from the lower left) and opened in its own saved view; the first is current (framed in the accent colour, the one the navigation bar, Esc and ZOOM Previous act on), and a press or a wheel roll in another makes it current, the left-button click that does so doing nothing else, as in AutoCAD. A viewport with no area or outside the window is left out; when the current one is, or one is left, it fills the window alone. Each viewport is its own renderer, so edits reach all of them. *Part 5 (#172):* document tabs move along the row by a drag (pointer events; HTML drag and drop stays with dropped files) or Ctrl+Shift+Page Up/Down on the focused tab, and resting the mouse on a tab for 400 ms shows AutoCAD's file tab preview: Model and the layouts in tab order, each pictured as last drawn by a thumbnail the renderer copies from its own canvas (`RendererHost.thumbnail`, a transferred `ImageBitmap` from the render worker), a space not yet shown in this session having a blank stand-in; clicking one shows that drawing and space. *Part 6 (#217):* the tab preview pictures the layouts not shown yet as they would open, each by a canvas made only for the picture (`apps/web/src/canvas/picture.ts`: the same controller and renderer, drawn once off screen at the drawing area's size, copied and thrown away), one at a time after the spaces already drawn; resting the mouse on a thumbnail shows that drawing and space in the drawing area, and leaving the preview brings back the drawing and space that showed before, unless a thumbnail was clicked (`tests/e2e/ui/doc-tabs.spec.ts`). *Closed (#217):* both acceptance halves are met (every open route in part 1's specs; 60 fps on `bench-ga` is M1-60, #124). What is left is M2 work the re-scope above names: ZOOM Center/Dynamic/Object/nXP, transparent Z/P and the ZOOM/PAN/VIEW/REGEN commands go with the command line (M2-01), saving views into the drawing and the dirty dot with save (M2-13).
- [x] (#102) **M1-23** Layers palette read-only + on/off/freeze/lock toggles with VP overrides display. **Acceptance:** toggles update canvas < 16 ms. *Done:* a Layers palette docked right (Palettes rail, `apps/web/src/LayersPalette.tsx`) lists the layers by name with Status, On, Freeze, Lock, VP Freeze (in a layout: how many of its viewports freeze the layer), Plot, Color, Linetype and Lineweight, and a search box. On, Freeze and Lock are buttons that send a layer edit to the drawing's engine worker (M1-67); every canvas of the drawing redraws from the returned change. Freezing the current layer is refused as AutoCAD does. `tests/e2e/ui/layers.spec.ts` measures toggle to drawn canvas: median of 5 under 16 ms (6 to 14 ms locally). Locked-layer fading is M1-69; editing the other columns is M2.
- [x] (#99) **M1-67** Engine worker (ARCHITECTURE.md §3): the authoritative `DocumentModel` of each open drawing lives in an engine worker built from the same `DocumentInit` as the render replicas; the app sends it edits and forwards the `ChangeSet` it returns to every renderer of that drawing; a main-thread fallback runs the same session. **Acceptance:** a Node test drives the session and a render replica side by side and they stay in step; Playwright sees each opened drawing start its own engine worker and closing its tab end it (edits reach the canvas through M1-23's toggles). *Why:* M1-23's layer toggles are document edits, and no engine worker existed yet; M2 commands run in it too.
- [x] (#104) **M1-69** Locked-layer fading: draw objects on locked layers faded by LAYLOCKFADECTL (default 50, negative = off) as AutoCAD does, in model space and through viewports. **Acceptance:** a renderer test shows a locked layer's strokes at the LAYLOCKFADECTL alpha against an unlocked one, redrawn when Lock toggles in the Layers palette. *Why:* M1-23 toggles Lock but the renderer draws locked layers like any other. *Done:* `RenderStyle.layLockFade` (LAYLOCKFADECTL, default 50, at most 90, zero or negative off) draws every primitive of an entity on a locked layer at 1 − fade/100 alpha, including the parts of a block reference on a locked layer or on a locked layer of their own, and model space through viewports; the locked set is found again on a layer change, so a Lock toggle needs no re-tessellation. `canvas2d-locked.test.ts` (6) and the Layers palette spec (the locked hatch keeps its pixels at about half strength). Setting the value in the app waits for the OPTIONS dialog (M2).

Tests: `packages/render/test/session-fonts.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/fonts.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/ui/text.spec.ts` (fails without the fonts). User SHX and TrueType fonts stay M1-37 and M1-38.

- [x] (#117 #121 #126 #129) **M1-32** acad-ts table and header read gaps found by the M1-03 oracle test (`packages/testkit/src/mapping/tables.corpus.test.ts` `ACAD_TS_GAPS`): `LAYER` 347 material never assigned on DXF read, nameless R12 shape `STYLE` records renamed `unnamedN`, R12 `DIMBLK` names without their underscore (`BOXBLANK` for `_BOXBLANK`) left unresolved (these three fixed in part 3, patches 0112–0114), multileader colours (91/93/94) left as raw integers on DXF read, `doc.mLeaderStyles`/`doc.tableStyles` never filled on read (Triya reads the dictionaries instead), entity linetype `BYBLOCK` in R12 files read as ByLayer, `TRACE` read as `SOLID`, `ACAD_TABLE` entities of ezdxf files dropped from their block (fixed in M1-33 part 2, patch 0099), caret codes other than `^J`/`^M`/`^I` left encoded, a rotated `DIMENSION` whose `AcDbRotatedDimension` subclass is empty (50 omitted) read as aligned, `HATCH` style (75) skipped, `HATCH` seed points dropped, `HATCH` ellipse-edge major axis (11/21) read with the centre's coordinate (these three fixed in part 1, patch 0108), the `IMAGE`/`WIPEOUT` clip boundary type (71) skipped on DXF read (so a save would write polygonal clips as rectangular), `ACAD_TABLE` in R14/R2000 DWG read as an unknown entity, no reader for `DWFUNDERLAY`/`DGNUNDERLAY`, the DXF `VIEWPORT` status (68) skipped and clip boundary (340) never read, a viewport ID of 0 replaced by the viewport's index (fixed in M1-58, patch 0089), the R12 viewport view (MVIEW xdata) not read, R12 `ATTRIB`s without a `7` left on a detached default style (the mapper resolves it by name), and 32 header variables missing from the acad-ts header map (`$ATTDIA`, `$ATTREQ`, `$CMATERIAL`, `$PUCS*`, `$SURFTAB*`, `$LINMIN`/`$LINMAX`, …; fixed in part 2, patch 0111). Each as a patch with a failing-first test. **Acceptance:** `ACAD_TS_GAPS` empty. *Why:* the round-trip harness compares acad-ts with itself, so values acad-ts never reads are invisible to it; only the independent DXF oracle sees them. *Part 1 (#117):* acad-ts patch 0108 reads the DXF `HATCH` style (75), keeps the group after the last pattern line (the seed-point count 98, or the pixel size 47) and builds an ellipse edge's major axis from its own 11/21; `HATCH 75`, `HATCH 98` and `HATCH 11` are off the entity oracle's gap list. *Part 2 (#121):* acad-ts patch 0111 adds the 30 AutoCAD header variables missing from the header map, so DXF reads and writes them, and keeps them through DWG (`$SURFV` read into `$SURFU`, the R2004+ `$INTERSECTIONCOLOR` read into `$INTERFERECOLOR`, R13/R14 values and the material, orthographic-UCS and visual-style pointers read and dropped); `$CMATERIAL`, `$INTERFEREOBJVS`, `$INTERFEREVPVS` are kept as raw handles. `$LINMIN`/`$LINMAX` are not AutoCAD variables (ezdxf's hand-written `Minimal_DXF_AC1006.dxf`, most likely `$LIMMIN`/`$LIMMAX` misspelt), so the oracle skips them as AutoCAD does. The header part of the tables gap list is empty. *Part 3 (#126):* acad-ts patch 0112 assigns a layer's material on read (DXF 347, DWG R2007+) and writes it back in both formats; 0113 keeps a shape file's nameless `STYLE` record nameless (it was renamed `unnamedN`, and a DWG style control listing it twice made two entries); 0114 reads the R12 `DIMSTYLE` 5 as the block name it is (5 is a handle code, so `BOXBLANK` was parsed as hex 11), resolves a built-in arrowhead named without its underscore to its block, and writes 5/6/7 in R12 as AutoCAD does. The tables gap list is empty; the entity gaps are left. *Part 4 (#129):* acad-ts patch 0115 reads an R12 entity's `BYBLOCK` linetype as ByBlock (R12 has no BYBLOCK record, so it fell back to ByLayer); 0116 decodes every caret code `^@`–`^_` to its control character and writes every control character back in caret notation; 0117 reads a rotated dimension whose AcDbRotatedDimension subclass is empty as rotated; 0118 reads the DXF viewport status (68) and clip boundary (340); 0119 reads an R12 viewport's view, snap, grid and view-mode bits from its ACAD MVIEW xdata. The entity gap list is empty too, so the acceptance is met. The listed acad-ts losses neither oracle sees (TRACE read as SOLID, multileader colours, image/wipeout clip type 71, R14/R2000 DWG tables, DWF/DGN underlay readers, R12 ATTRIBs without 7, DWG `\U+` escapes before R2007) moved to M1-79.

They reach every renderer as bytes beside the bundled fonts (`FontSources.files`), so a style naming one by file name draws with it; adding or removing one redraws and re-bounds open drawings (`RendererHost.setFonts`). `tests/e2e/ui/user-fonts.spec.ts`: a dropped `Arial.TTF` draws the corpus MTEXT whose style names `arial.ttf`, still after a reload, and removing it brings the bundled stand-in back. *Part 2 (#110):* fonts installed on this computer through the Local Font Access API (`apps/web/src/local-fonts.ts`): the permission is read without asking, and `queryLocalFonts` runs only once it is granted or from Settings > Fonts > "Use installed fonts…"; the TrueType fonts an opened drawing names (style files and MTEXT `\f` families) are looked up through a `TtfRegistry` source that matches AutoCAD's Windows file names, PostScript and full names, and families by bold and italic, then read and handed on after the user's files. A refusal keeps the bundled stand-ins without an error. `tests/e2e/ui/installed-fonts.spec.ts` (the API stood in): allowing draws the corpus `arial.ttf` MTEXT with the installed Arial, again after a reload without asking; denying keeps the stand-in with no console errors. *Part 3 (#115):* the Health palette's report asks a main-thread book of the same `FontSources` the renderers get (`appFonts(sources)`, bundled, user files and installed faces), so a font they serve is not listed as missing; a "Font errors" card lists user files that no longer parse (the session's `fontWarnings`, found again on the main thread) or could not be read back from OPFS, and installed faces that could not be read or parsed (`TtfRegistry.warnings`, now structured `{ source, font, problem }`, and a source whose read rejects is skipped with a warning instead of failing the lookup), each with the drawing's styles naming it; a "Fonts from this computer" card lists the installed face each of the drawing's fonts draws with. Tests: `packages/text/test/ttf/registry.test.ts`, `packages/io/test/health.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/{fonts,user-fonts,local-fonts}.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/ui/health-fonts.spec.ts`.

- [x] (#90) **M1-42** Generated benchmark drawings (owner decision 2026-11-02, DEC-045: benchmarks use synthetic data, not owner-provided `bench-*.dwg`): a seeded generator for `bench-100k` (100k entities, 50 blocks, 5 layouts) and `bench-ga` (25k entities, 30 blocks, 2 layouts), and `tests/e2e/io/responsiveness.spec.ts` parsing `bench-100k` in place of M1-06's line-and-circle drawing, keeping the 100 ms in-page budget. **Acceptance:** the spec passes on the generated `bench-100k` in CI. *Done (#90):* `packages/testkit/src/bench/drawing.ts` (`benchDocument`, `benchDwg`, `BENCH_100K`, `BENCH_GA`) builds the drawings with acad-ts from a seeded PRNG: 40 % LINE, 15 % LWPOLYLINE, 10 % each CIRCLE, ARC, INSERT (of 12-entity symbol blocks) and TEXT, 5 % MTEXT, over 20 layers, plus one sheet border and title per layout; `drawing.test.ts` reads both back and checks the counts by type, blocks and layouts, and that the geometry repeats. `bench-100k` is 5.2 MB as DWG; the responsiveness spec parses it in about 4.5–5 s in the worker with the page's longest timer gap about 20 ms and input delay under 30 ms. - [x] (#120) **M1-43** acad-ts ACI palette: 31 of the 255 entries of `Color._indexRgb` are mistyped (digits such as 38 written 88 or 28, 76 written 126, ACI 192 with red and blue swapped, the greys 250–254), and `Color.approxIndex` sums signed channel differences and returns at the first negative one, so a true or colour-book colour saved through `getApproxIndex` (DXF 62 next to 420 and 430, the `$CECOLOR` header, R12 DXF, DWG colours) gets an unrelated index, usually 0 (ByBlock) or 1. Patch both in `third_party/acad-ts` (table from the computed palette, nearest by squared distance over 1–255) with a unit test. **Acceptance:** `style.corpus.test.ts` `ACAD_TS_PALETTE_GAPS` is empty and a true-colour entity saved to DXF carries its nearest ACI in 62. *Why:* found by the M1-13 palette oracle test. *Note (M1-55):* `@triya/io` no longer calls `getApproxIndex`; it uses `aciOfTrueColor`, whose palette (value rows 1, 0.8, 0.6, 0.5, 0.3) reproduces the ACI AutoCAD wrote for every corpus true colour, while nearest-in-the-computed-palette gets 7 of 21 wrong: patch `approxIndex` to the same lookup so DXF 62 written by acad-ts matches AutoCAD (see M1-57 for the display palette). *Done (#120, acad-ts patch 0109):* the 31 entries equal `aciRgb`; `approxIndex` is nearest by squared distance over 1–255 in AutoCAD's lookup palette (rows 1, 0.8, 0.6, 0.5, 0.3), the same as `aciOfTrueColor`, and reproduces all 3,532 corpus 62/420 pairs (`true-color.corpus.test.ts`); the DXF writer writes that 62 before a true-colour entity's 420. The display table stays on rows 1, 0.65, 0.5, 0.3, 0.15 until M1-57.
- [x] (#122) **M1-44** Interactive render speed: pan/zoom at 60 fps (T3) with 100k entities in view. The M1-12 numbers on the synthetic 100k drawing in headless Chromium are 3–4 fps in the worker and 5 fps on the main thread, with a 1.7 s first frame; 10k entities run at 48–54 fps. Frame CPU is about 25 ms, so the rest is rasterising 100k small strokes. Candidates: draw the cached scene bitmap transformed during continuous pan/zoom and redraw in full when idle; a coarser LOD that draws 1–3 px entities as dots or boxes; per-colour `Path2D` reuse between frames. Then enforce T3 in `tests/e2e/perf/render.spec.ts`, which reports it today, and re-measure on the generated `bench-100k` drawing (M1-42, DEC-045) before any WebGL decision (DEC-006). **Acceptance:** the perf spec asserts ≥ 60 fps (−20 % runner margin) on the 100k synthetic drawing. *Why:* M1-12 delivered the renderer with the cheap frame-loop wins; T3 needs a rendering-strategy change worth its own PR, and M1-22's bench-ga 60 fps acceptance depends on it. *Done (#122):* `Canvas2DRenderer` draws the last scene bitmap moved and scaled to the new view while only the view changes on a heavy scene (a scene draw over 16 ms of CPU or over 8000 entities) and redraws it in full once the view has stayed put for 100 ms; any other change redraws at once. `tests/e2e/perf/render.spec.ts` paces pan/zoom by animation frames as the app does and asserts ≥ 48 fps on the synthetic 100k drawing (3.7 → 59.7 fps locally) and on the generated `bench-100k` mapped through `documentInit` (13.3 → 60.0 fps). No WebGL trigger (DEC-006).
- [x] (#111, #114) **M1-45** Complex linetypes and infinite lines: draw the text and shapes that `dashPath` places (`EmbedPlacement`) through the text engine (M1-41) and SHX shapes (M1-37), with the style's fixed height and the upright rule; and dash XLINE/RAY at draw time from a fixed origin so dashes stay put while panning. **Acceptance:** a corpus linetype with text (GAS_LINE style) and one with shapes render their text and shapes; an XLINE with DASHED shows dashes. *Why:* M1-13 part 3 leaves those gaps empty and infinite lines continuous. (blocked-by M1-41, M1-37) *Part 1 (#111):* text and shapes draw in their gaps (`linetypeEmbedPrimitives`): text in its STYLE at `S=` × the style's fixed height (or `S=` alone when it is 0), shapes from the STYLE's shape file at `S=`, both times the linetype scale, with the dashes only; acad-ts patch 0105 reads the DXF segment style (340) it used to skip. `packages/render/test/linetype-embeds.test.ts`; `packages/testkit/src/render/linetypes.corpus.test.ts`: every corpus LINE with GAS_LINE or a shape linetype (acadsharp samples, with a made-up `ltypeshp.shx`, as Autodesk's is never committed) draws them near the line. *Part 2 (#114):* XLINE and RAY carry their scaled pattern (`dashes` on the `infinite` primitive), laid out at draw time from the base point over the part on screen, so dashes stay put while panning; continuous under a pixel per repetition. `canvas2d-style.test.ts`: an XLINE with DASHED draws its dashes from the base point and keeps them in place across pans; a RAY only from its base on. Shapes draw once the user adds the shape file (M1-37's store); text and shapes on XLINE/RAY are not drawn.

Left: the within-tolerance check against a reference plot (M1-24, M1-26); join and mitre rules are undocumented by AutoCAD and wait on those plots. *Closed (#215, DEC-047):* the within-tolerance check is the M1-24 golden images (#204) of the corpus files with wide polylines; checking the undocumented join and mitre rules against AutoCAD plots is post-launch (M1-26).
- [x] **M1-47** Fit-point splines: interpolate the control points of a SPLINE stored only as fit points (R2013+ DWGs can hold one; `sample_AC1027.dwg`, `sample_AC1032.dwg` and `sample_base.dwg` do) the way AutoCAD does, from the fit points, end tangents, fit tolerance and knot parameterization (chord, square root, uniform), in the M1-02 kernel, so the renderer draws them and the spatial index bounds them. **Acceptance:** the three corpus fit-only splines draw through their fit points and are bounded; kernel tests per parameterization. *Why:* found by M1-15's corpus check, where `SpatialIndex.build` threw on them; until then they are unknown to the index and not drawn. *Done (#97):* `fitSpline` (`engine/src/geom/fit.ts`) makes the cubic through the fit points with knots at their chord, square-root or uniform parameters and natural ends or the given end tangents (closed splines C2 through the seam); it reproduces the control points and knots AutoCAD saved beside its fit points in `uncommon.dxf` to 1e-9. `splineCurve` feeds it to the spatial index, the renderer, hatch spline edges and spline leaders, and io maps the DWG knot parameterization. `splines.corpus.test.ts` draws and bounds the three corpus fit-only splines through their fit points. Tangent scaling, closed splines, √chord/uniform knots and fit tolerance > 0 are undocumented and unsampled: M1-65.
- [x] **M1-48** (#73) DWG AEC raw data through a save: `AEC_WALL`, `AEC_WALL_STYLE`, `AEC_CLEANUP_GROUP_DEF` and `BINRECORD` keep the bytes after their known fields as raw data. The reader takes everything up to the end of the object, including bits past the data stream, so each save adds 6–20 bytes. There are 4 differences in acadsharp `AecObjects.dwg`. **Acceptance:** raw data keeps its length through a save; 0 differences in `AecObjects.dwg`. *Why:* the last DWG writer difference outside M1-33 after M1-29. *Done (#73):* the raw data ends at the end of the object's data stream (patch 0073); round trip 6,125 → 6,121.
- [x] **M1-49** (#76) R12 DXF block records and owners through a save. R12 has no `BLOCK_RECORD` table, so acad-ts makes one record per block on read, with handles taken from `$HANDSEED`. A save writes the raised `$HANDSEED`, so reopening the saved file makes the records under different handles, and every entity, `BLOCK`, `ENDBLK` and `INSERT` owner or block link differs. This accounts for nearly all of the 6,188 differences left in the 8 R12 corpus files (2,941 in each acadsharp `sample_AC1009` file). **Acceptance:** a saved R12 file reopens with the same record handles, or the harness compares made-up R12 records by name (which needs a `DECISIONS.md` entry); those roots at 0 in every R12 corpus file. *Why:* found in M1-29; it is neither a writer gap nor a dropped object. *Done (#76):* the DXF reader records which handles it made up and which objects it added as defaults (LTYPE ByLayer, a missing `*PAPER_SPACE` or `$MODEL_SPACE`, and so on); an R12 save leaves those handles and defaults out and writes `$HANDSEED` as read, so reopening makes up the same handles (patch 0076). No harness exception or DEC entry was needed. Round trip 6,121 → 48; `ASCII_R12.dxf`, `AC1003_LINE_Example.dxf` and `Minimal_DXF_AC1006.dxf` now round-trip with no difference. What is left in R12 files is M1-56.
- [x] (#71, #74, #75, #77, #215) **M1-52** Leaders and feature control frames on screen: MULTILEADER (leader lines straight or spline, landing and dogleg, arrowheads closed filled or from their block at the drawn arrowhead size, MTEXT content with its frame, block content with its attributes), LEADER (path, hook line, arrowhead from the DIMSTYLE with the entity's `DSTYLE` overrides) and TOLERANCE (frame boxes, GD&T symbols, compartments split by `%%v`), split into parts the renderer draws and the spatial index bounds the same way. **Acceptance:** every corpus MULTILEADER, LEADER and TOLERANCE draws and is bounded by what it draws (closes M1-35 for these kinds); reference-plot check with M1-24/M1-26. *Why:* no M1 task drew them, so the 302 corpus multileaders were invisible and unbounded. *Part 1 (#71):* `multiLeaderParts` (engine) splits a MULTILEADER into leader LWPOLYLINEs, one landing, closed filled SOLID or block INSERT arrowheads at the context's drawn arrowhead size (new `drawnArrowheadSize`, DXF context 140) and its MTEXT with the frame; the renderer draws the parts like a block reference, inside blocks too, and the spatial index bounds the MULTILEADER by their union and follows its arrowhead blocks. `leaders.corpus.test.ts` draws all 302 corpus multileaders, each bounded by exactly what it draws. *Part 2 (#74):* `leaderParts` draws a LEADER's path (on under its MTEXT by DIMGAP × DIMSCALE + the text width when DIMTAD puts the text above) and its DIMLDRBLK or closed filled arrowhead at DIMASZ × DIMSCALE in DIMCLRD/DIMLTYPE/DIMLWD, from its DIMSTYLE with the entity's `DSTYLE` xdata overrides (`dimVars`); a multileader's block content draws as an INSERT; the corpus test covers all 20 LEADERs. *Part 3 (#75):* acad-ts patch 0074 reads a DXF LEADER without group 71 with its arrowhead on, as AutoCAD writes it and as its DWG twin has it, so all 20 corpus LEADERs draw their arrowhead; the mapping oracle expects the same. *Part 4 (#77):* `toleranceParts` draws a TOLERANCE as one rectangle per non-empty compartment (text width + DIMGAP either side, rows DIMTXT + 2 × DIMGAP high, from the left edge half way up the first row) in DIMCLRD/DIMLWD and each compartment's text as MTEXT in DIMTXSTY/DIMCLRT, with `DSTYLE` overrides; the widths come from an injected `TextMeasure` (`textMeasure` in `@triya/render`), so a TOLERANCE stays unknown until its font loads; the corpus test covers all 60 TOLERANCEs. AutoCAD does not document the frame geometry: the rules are the common drafter expectation, to check against M1-26 plots. GD&T symbols (`{\Fgdt;x}`) draw in whatever font serves `gdt` until a re-authored GD&T font ships (M1-54). Left: block-content attributes, per-line overrides and underline/overline attachments (no public corpus file has them); spline leaders draw and bound as the cubic through their vertices since M1-47 (#97). *Closed (#215, DEC-047):* the reference-plot check is the M1-24 golden images (#204) of the corpus leader and tolerance files, and checking the TOLERANCE frame geometry against AutoCAD plots is post-launch (M1-26); block-content attributes, per-line overrides and the underline and overline attachments moved to M1-111, which authors its own sample under Rule 0.
- [x] (#78) **M1-53** DXF DIMSTYLE groups left out of a file read as AutoCAD's defaults. A DXF DIMSTYLE omits every DIMVAR at its default, and acad-ts fills the gaps with its `DimensionStyle` class defaults, several of them ISO values (DIMGAP 0.625, DIMEXO 0.625, DIMEXE 1.25, DIMDLI 3.75, DIMDEC 2) where AutoCAD means 0.09, 0.0625, 0.18, 0.38 and 4: the acadsharp `Standard` style reads DIMGAP 0.625 from DXF and 0.09 from its DWG twin, so DXF feature control frames draw with gaps seven times too wide. Fix as an acad-ts patch that starts DXF-read styles at AutoCAD's documented defaults, with the M1-03 oracle checking omitted groups too. **Acceptance:** every corpus DXF DIMSTYLE equals its DWG twin's DIMVARs. *Why:* found by M1-52 part 4; the oracle only compares groups a file has. *Done:* acad-ts patch 0075 starts every DXF-read DIMSTYLE at AutoCAD's initial values for the 16 DIMVARs whose class defaults differ (DIMALTD, DIMALTTD, DIMDEC, DIMDLI, DIMEXE, DIMEXO, DIMFIT, DIMGAP, DIMSAH, DIMTAD, DIMTDEC, DIMTIH, DIMTOH, DIMTOLJ, DIMTZIN, DIMZIN); `tables.corpus.test.ts` checks every DIMSTYLE of the six acadsharp DXF/DWG pairs (ASCII and binary) has its twin's DIMVARs, except the R2004+ colours (M1-55).

Symbol sizes are ours (AutoCAD publishes none): the comparison with reference plots stays with M1-24/M1-26.
- [x] (#79) **M1-55** R2004+ DWG DIMSTYLE colours: DIMCLRD, DIMCLRE, DIMCLRT and DIMTFILLCLR of the acadsharp `custom_dim_style` read as ACI 1, 2, 7 and 1 from the R2004–R2018 DWGs but 86, 183, 151 and 97 from their DXF twins and from the R2000 DWG. Find which reader is wrong (the R2004+ DWG CMC colour, or a true colour behind it) and patch acad-ts. **Acceptance:** the M1-53 twin test runs without its colour exception. *Why:* found by M1-53; the oracle compares DXF with DXF only. *Done (#79):* neither reader is wrong. The R2004+ DWGs hold the four colours as true colours (AcCmColor 0xC2: 44,136,27; 128,61,194; 80,179,255; 54,117,66), and DIMSTYLE groups 176–178 and R2000 files can only hold an ACI, so AutoCAD writes its ACI for each. `AcadToTriya` turned a true colour into an ACI with acad-ts's broken `getApproxIndex` (M1-43), giving 1, 2, 7. It now uses `aciOfTrueColor`, AutoCAD's lookup (nearest by squared RGB distance in a palette with value rows 1, 0.8, 0.6, 0.5, 0.3), which reproduces all 21 distinct true colours AutoCAD wrote with an ACI in the corpus (`true-color.corpus.test.ts`, 3,532 pairs), for DIMVARs and header colours; `DimStyleRecord.trueColors` keeps the true colours, and LEADER and TOLERANCE draw in them unless a `DSTYLE` override sets an ACI. No acad-ts patch was needed. Follow-up: M1-57.

The ASCII-mapped Greek and symbol fonts moved to M1-78.
