No public corpus file has a wide polyline with a linetype (`widths.corpus.test.ts` counts them, 0 today); how AutoCAD draws a dot on a wide segment is undocumented and waits on the M1-26 reference plots with M1-46's joins.
- [x] **M1-68** (#113) `structuralDiff` compares the objects acad-ts keeps inside their owner (attributes, vertices, `SEQEND`s, polyface faces, child fields, their extension dictionaries), not only their handles, and acad-ts resolves those children's table references as it does for registered entities. **Acceptance:** a change inside an attribute or vertex is reported (test in `diff.test.ts`); round trip back to 0 differences. *Why:* found in M1-33 part 3: indexing held children (`documentObjects`) shows 391 differences in 19 files, all reference resolution on held children (layer `0` omitted on save reads back as no layer, unresolved `STYLE`/`LTYPE` on the first read); a real loss there (the field date, 0100) went unseen. *Done:* the diff indexes held children through `documentObjects`; acad-ts patch 0106 assigns the document to attributes, vertices, faces and `SEQEND`s, so they take the table entries; corpus round trip 388 → 0 with held children compared.
- [x] (#105) **M1-70** Health report in the app: a Health palette (or Drawing Utilities dialog until M7-03's palette stack) showing `healthReport` for the active drawing as cards (version and units, missing fonts with their stand-ins, references, missing patterns, unknown objects, unmapped entities), rerun when the user adds fonts or `.pat` files. **Acceptance:** Playwright opens a corpus file and the palette lists its missing fonts and unknown objects; keyboard operable. *Why:* M1-25 (#101) built and tested the report; the palette is a separate UI task with its own Playwright test, next to the Layers palette (M1-23, #102). **Done (#105):** a Health button on the Palettes rail docks the Health palette (`apps/web/src/HealthPalette.tsx`; one palette at a time until M7-03) with folding cards for the drawing on screen: Drawing (release and code, units, measurement), Missing fonts (font, what draws it or "Not drawn", text styles), References ("Not checked" until M1-21), Missing hatch patterns (against the bundled set and the user's `.pat` files, redone when they change), Unknown objects and Entities not drawn, with counts. The report asks a main-thread `FontBook` of the same bundled fonts the renderers draw with (`appFonts()` in `apps/web/src/fonts.ts`, M1-72) through the new side-effect-free `FontBook.resolve`/`hasShx`, so one drawing's fonts never show in another's report. `tests/e2e/ui/health.spec.ts` covers the content on sample_AC1018.dwg, following the active tab, and keyboard folding and closing.
- [x] **M1-73** `packages/testkit/src/io/open.corpus.test.ts` "returns the summary and a clonable document for every readable file" opens the whole public corpus in one test and runs close to the 30 s `testTimeout`: 30.7 s in a full local `pnpm test` (2026-11-02, passes alone and in CI). Give it headroom without hiding a slowdown: split it per file (`it.each`) or give it a measured budget of its own. **Acceptance:** the suite passes under full-suite load with margin; any per-file slowdown still fails. *Why:* a corpus walk at the edge of the global timeout fails at random as the corpus grows. *Done (#116):* the walk is one test per readable file (`it.each`, 108 files, failing by file name), each with a 5 s budget of its own: the slowest file takes 0.6 s alone, and a file slowed by 6 s fails.
- [x] (#214) **M1-75** `TriyaToAcad` writers beyond M1-05 (found while building it): geometry writers for the mapped types that have none (POLYLINE, SPLINE, SOLID/TRACE, 3DFACE, ATTDEF, INSERT with its ATTRIBs, the dimensions, LEADER, TOLERANCE, MULTILEADER, ACAD_TABLE, IMAGE/WIPEOUT, PDFUNDERLAY, MLINE, VIEWPORT, HATCH), and the non-entity changes `exportDocument` refuses today with `UnsupportedChangeError`: header variables, table records, objects, layouts and block definitions, moving an entity to another block, reordering a block's entities, xdata / reactors / extension dictionary / plot style / annotative edits, and colour-book colours. **Acceptance:** each writer gets a case in `packages/io/test/export.test.ts` and an edit in `packages/testkit/src/export/export.corpus.test.ts` (edited handles map back; everything else diff-free); M2-13 (Save) needs the types its markup commands create. *Part 1 (#130):* SOLID, 3DFACE, SPLINE and POLYLINE (2D and 3D, with its spline frame) are written, new or edited: a point Triya maps in plan keeps the Z it had (a 3DFACE corner, a SPLINE control or fit point, a 3D vertex), a polyline's VERTEX entities are edited in place under their own handles, and the vertices and SEQEND a polyline gains are numbered above every handle the engine handed out, with `$HANDSEED` raised past them. *Part 2 (#132):* ATTDEF and INSERT (and MINSERT) with its ATTRIBs are written, new or edited: an ATTRIB is written by handle onto the INSERT's own, with its own entity properties, one it gains is created under the engine's handle (refused if the file already uses it), one it loses is dropped, and one Triya does not map keeps its slot; the SEQEND is numbered like a polyline's when the first ATTRIB arrives and goes with the last. A multiline attribute's embedded MTEXT is edited in place; turning a single-line attribute multiline, or back, is refused, because DXF before R2018 keeps that text in an XRECORD. *Part 3 (#135):* every DIMENSION type, ARC_DIMENSION and LARGE_RADIAL_DIMENSION, LEADER and TOLERANCE are written, new or edited: a dimension's WCS points (10, 13–15) keep their Z when moved in plan and its OCS ones (11, 16) and angles fold −Z back; its type cannot change (each is its own acad-ts class and DWG object) and it must name a block, because AutoCAD draws a dimension from its `*D` block and does not regenerate a missing one (the block is not regenerated on an edit either: that is the dimension engine's, M3); a LARGE_RADIAL_DIMENSION is refused on a DWG save, which acad-ts cannot write (M1-74). A LEADER keeps its vertices' Z, and its hook line must be the one acad-ts writes (DXF 75 is derived from the vertex count, annotation type and horizontal direction); an annotation the same save adds is refused for now. A TOLERANCE runs upside down exactly on −Z, as MTEXT. Line spacing and the oblique angle are written but lost by acad-ts's DXF reader and writer (M1-82). *Part 4 (#137):* HATCH, IMAGE, WIPEOUT, PDFUNDERLAY and MLINE are written, new or edited. A HATCH's loops are rewritten in place and an unchanged loop stays as read; a loop keeps the boundary objects it was read with, because they point back at the hatch through reactors Triya does not write yet; the stored pattern lines are written as they are (acad-ts's angle and scale setters would rotate and scale them again); a solid fill has no stored angle or scale (DXF 52 and 41 are pattern-fill only). An IMAGE must name an IMAGEDEF and takes its IMAGEDEF_REACTOR along to another one (the writer makes a new image's); its normal must be U × V, a rectangular clip states two corners, and brightness, contrast and fade stay within 0–100. A PDFUNDERLAY is upside down exactly on −Z, and its normal and clip boundary are refused on a DXF save, which acad-ts writes without them (M1-83). An MLINE must name an MLINESTYLE whose element count each vertex matches; its vertices keep their Z. *Part 5 (#140):* MULTILEADER and VIEWPORT are written, new or edited. A MULTILEADER needs its MLEADERSTYLE, an mtext one its text and a block one its block; its points keep their Z, its plane is ±Z with the text upside down exactly on −Z, and what AutoCAD states both on the entity and in the context data it draws from (text style, colour and attachment, block, scale, rotation and colour, the attachment sides and alignment) is written to both; a branch or leader line keeps what Triya does not map (breaks, a line's own overrides) by its place in the list; turning annotative on or off is refused, as its scale representations are not written. A VIEWPORT must sit in a layout's paper space, and its clip boundary entity cannot change (its reactors); DWG does not store the DXF 68 status, acad-ts writes no DWG style sheet or visual style (M1-85, refused on a DWG save), and an R12 viewport's view and frozen layers live in its MVIEW xdata (M1-86, refused). acad-ts's DXF reader drops a multileader's arrowhead, content block and leader line indexes, which its writer keeps (M1-84). *Part 6 (#144):* header variables and LAYER records are written. A changed header variable is written by the DXF values its reader takes, and an existing DICTIONARYVAR by its text; refused before anything changes are the variables the save sets (`$ACADVER`, `$HANDSEED`, the code page, the save's clock, program and handle settings), the UCS name variables (acad-ts would rename the UCS record), a name variable naming no record, a value the variable cannot hold, a removed variable or a new DICTIONARYVAR, a `CANNOSCALEVALUE` that is not its CANNOSCALE's ratio, and what the format saved does not store (the R12 DXF list; per DWG version the R13–R14-only and later variables; acad-ts writer gaps, M1-87), which `export-header.corpus.test.ts` checks for every variable on every corpus format and version. A layer is written new or edited (name, on, frozen, locked, colour, linetype, lineweight, plot, plot style, material), with `frozen` and `locked` setting DXF 70; a new entity and `$CLAYER` may name a layer the same save adds; removing a layer, renaming layer 0 or an xref-dependent layer, a transparency change (AutoCAD keeps it as AcCmTransparency xdata), an R12 layer's lineweight, plot flag, plot style or material, and an edit of the layer 0 acad-ts makes up for an R12 file that has none are refused. *Part 7 (#146):* draw order: SORTENTSTABLE objects are written edited, new or removed, so DRAWORDER, TEXTTOFRONT and HATCHTOBACK (M2-08) can save. Entries are written in the model's order and may name an entity the same save adds or one Triya does not map; an entity the save removes loses its entry (acad-ts wrote it as handle 0). A new table goes into its block record's extension dictionary: the existing one, or one the save creates (hard owner, as AutoCAD makes it) when the model gives the record an `extDict` under the table's owner, which is then the record's only accepted change. Refused: an entry naming an entity outside the block, two entries for one entity, a sort handle that is not one, a table changing block or owner, a second table for a block, xdata or reactor changes, and a new table before R14, which introduced draw order. *Part 8 (#148):* STYLE and LTYPE records are written, new or edited, for the STYLE and LINETYPE commands (M4). A text style's name, flags, font, big font, height, width factor, oblique angle, last height, generation and TrueType bold and italic are written; when its font or bold and italic change, its `ACAD` xdata face name and flags are corrected (AutoCAD draws the face the xdata names): the face follows a new TrueType font, the bold and italic bits follow the style, and an SHX font loses the pair. A linetype's name, flags, description and elements are written (text and shape elements naming a STYLE record, which may be one the same save adds); its pattern length must be the elements' total, which acad-ts writes as DXF 40. Entities, layers, linetypes and `$TEXTSTYLE`, `$DIMTXSTY` and `$CELTYPE` may name a record the same save adds. Refused, as AutoCAD refuses them or acad-ts cannot keep them: removing a record, renaming Standard, ByLayer, ByBlock or Continuous, editing ByLayer or ByBlock or giving Continuous a pattern, a duplicate or invalid name, a nameless style that is not a shape file's, a style turning into a shape file or back, xref-dependent flags, an empty font, a negative height, a width factor of 0 or less, an oblique angle past ±85°, generation flags other than backward and upside down, bold or italic on an SHX font or in R12, an alignment other than A, more than 12 elements, an element both text and shape, a text element without text or on a shape file's style, a shape on a text style, text or shapes in an R12 linetype, xdata, reactor and extension dictionary changes, and an edit of a record acad-ts makes up for an R12 file. *Part 9 (#150):* DIMSTYLE records are written, new or edited, for the DIMSTYLE manager (M4-03): the name, flags and every DIMVAR `dimStyle()` maps, by the acad-ts property that carries it (`DIMVARS` now names the property instead of a getter); DIMTXSTY and the dimension linetypes may name a record the same save adds, and the arrowheads must be blocks the drawing has; a DIMVAR colour an R2004+ DWG holds as a true colour is written as one, with the ACI AutoCAD derives for it (`aciOfTrueColor`) beside it, and an ACI of its own goes with dropping the true colour; `$DIMSTYLE` and dimensions may name a style the same save adds. A new style's DIMVARs are checked against the drawing's Standard, which AutoCAD starts a new style from. Refused: removing a style, renaming Standard, a duplicate or invalid name, xref-dependent flags, an unknown DIMVAR, a value missing or of the wrong type, a whole-number group holding a fraction, a DIMDSEP of more than one character, a colour outside 0–256, a value acad-ts's setters refuse (DIMSCALE or DIMASZ below 0, DIMTXT 0 or less, DIMJOGANG outside 5°–90°), DIMTXSTY naming nothing, a reference naming no record of its table, a DIMVAR the drawing's version has no group for (the R13, R2000, R2007 and R2010 additions), DIMUNIT and DIMFIT from R2000 (obsolete: DIMLUNIT/DIMFRAC and DIMATFIT/DIMTMOVE replace them), a true colour outside an R2004+ DWG, xdata, reactor and extension dictionary changes, and what acad-ts loses (M1-92). *Part 10 (#152):* APPID, UCS, VIEW and VPORT records are written, new or edited, for the UCS and VIEW commands, VPORTS and the last view a save keeps in `*Active`: an application's name; a UCS's origin, axes, elevation and orthographic type; a view's or viewport's centre, height, direction, target, lens, clip distances, twist, view mode and render mode, a view's width and saved UCS, and a viewport's corners, aspect ratio, snap, grid, circle zoom, UCS icon and UCS. Viewports may share a name (every tiled one is `*Active`). Refused: removing a record, renaming ACAD or `*Active`, a duplicate or invalid name, xref-dependent flags, a UCS whose axes are not unit vectors at right angles, a size, lens or aspect ratio of 0 or less, no view direction, corners outside 0–1, negative snap or grid spacing, bits AutoCAD does not define, a UCS's elevation and orthographic type and a view's or viewport's UCS and render mode before R2000 and a viewport's grid flags and major lines before R2007 (the format has no place for them), what acad-ts reads back wrong (M1-93), and xdata, reactor and extension dictionary changes. *Part 11 (#155):* MLEADERSTYLE objects are written, new or edited, for MLEADER with a new current style (M2-09) and the multileader style manager (M4-04): every field `mleaderStyle()` maps (content type, draw orders, leader points and angles, path type, leader colour, linetype and lineweight, landing and dogleg, arrowhead and its size, default text, text style, attachments, angle, alignment, colour, height and frame, block content with its colour, scale, rotation and connection, scale, overwrite, annotative and break gap); a new style joins the ACAD_MLEADERSTYLE dictionary (made when the drawing has none) under the engine's handle, may name a text style or linetype the same save adds, and may be named by the `CMLEADERSTYLE` DICTIONARYVAR and by a new MULTILEADER. Refused: removing a style, renaming Standard, a duplicate or invalid name, flags (an object has none), a new style before R2007 (AutoCAD 2008 introduced multileaders), the text attachment direction and the top and bottom attachments in a DWG before R2010 (its record has no place for them), values AutoCAD's dialog does not take (enumerations out of range, fewer than 2 leader points, a text height of 0 or less, negative lengths, a zero block scale, a lineweight AutoCAD does not offer), colour-book colours, no text style or linetype, a reference naming nothing, `CMLEADERSTYLE` naming no style, xdata, reactor and extension dictionary changes, and what acad-ts loses: renaming a style (M1-95) and the arrowhead and content block on a DXF save (M1-94). *Part 12 (#157):* TABLESTYLE objects are written, new or edited, for the table style dialog (M4) and TABLE with a new current style, in files before R2010: the description, flow direction, flags, margins and title and header suppression, and each of the data, title and header row styles' text style, height, alignment, text and fill colours, fill, data and unit types and six borders' lineweight, visibility and colour; a new style joins the ACAD_TABLESTYLE dictionary (made when the drawing has none) under the engine's handle, its row styles may name a text style the same save adds, and it may be named by the `CTABLESTYLE` DICTIONARYVAR. acad-ts kept a DWG row style's text colour and a DXF one's data and unit types in other fields than the other format (patch 0142). Refused: any change from R2010, where a style is its named cell styles (M1-96), removing a style, renaming one (M1-95) or Standard, a duplicate or invalid name, flags, a new style before R2004 (AutoCAD 2005 introduced tables), the data and unit types before R2007, a true colour in DXF (ACI codes only) and in a DWG before R2004, colour-book colours, a row style or border count other than 3 and 6, values the dialog does not take (flow direction other than down or up, negative margins, a text height of 0 or less, alignment outside 1–9, lineweights AutoCAD does not offer), no text style or one that does not exist, `CTABLESTYLE` naming no style, and xdata, reactor and extension dictionary changes. *Part 13 (#159):* IMAGEDEF, RASTERVARIABLES and WIPEOUTVARIABLES objects are written, new or edited, for IMAGEATTACH, the External References palette (repath, Unload and Reload), IMAGEFRAME, IMAGEQUALITY and WIPEOUTFRAME: a definition's file name, size, pixel size, loaded flag and resolution units, the image and wipeout frames, the image quality and insertion units. Triya now maps a definition's name (its ACAD_IMAGE_DICT entry, the image name AutoCAD shows); a new definition joins that dictionary under it (the save makes the dictionary, under the handle the new definitions name as their owner, when the drawing has none) and a new IMAGE of the same save may show it, with the IMAGEDEF_REACTOR acad-ts gives it; a new RASTERVARIABLES or WIPEOUTVARIABLES joins the root dictionary as ACAD_IMAGE_VARS or ACAD_WIPEOUT_VARS. Refused: removing one (IMAGE Detach also erases the images whose reactors tie them to it), renaming a definition (M1-95), a duplicate, empty or invalid image name, a new one before R14, a second RASTERVARIABLES or WIPEOUTVARIABLES, one outside its dictionary, an empty file name, a size or pixel size of 0 or less, resolution units other than none, centimetres and inches, insertion units outside 0–8, and xdata, reactor and extension dictionary changes. *Part 14 (#161):* ACAD_TABLE is written, edited, for the table grips and cell editing (M4-05): the position and plane, the horizontal direction, the table style, the `*T` block, row heights, column widths, each cell's text and block, and cell rotation. Typed text becomes a string value whatever the cell held (a number or date typed over keeps what was typed: AutoCAD would parse it by the cell's data format, which Triya does not map), with its formatted text when it had one; a DWG R2010+ cell that was never typed in gets a value content. A block cell's block is written to the cell and to its block content. The `*T` block is written as it is: AutoCAD draws the table from it, and regenerating it from the new content is the table engine's (M4-05), as for dimensions. Refused: a new table (TABLE and COPY make a `*T` block of their own, block definitions M5), rows or columns added or removed, merges, cell formats and table-wide overrides (alongside M1-81), a change between text and block cells, text in a block cell or a block in a text cell, no table style or block, a table style that does not exist or a block that cannot be inserted, a row height or column width of 0 or less, no horizontal direction, a thickness, a tilted plane, typing over a cell that holds a field (M4-10), several contents, locked content or a data link, a new block for a cell whose block has attribute values, and a cell rotation in a DWG from R2010, which keeps it in the content format. *Part 15 (#163):* a LEADER's annotation and the reactors that point back at it, for the LEADER and QLEADER commands and for erasing or swapping an annotation: a LEADER may name an MTEXT, TOLERANCE or INSERT the same save adds (the LEADER command makes the leader first), every entity the save adds now exists before any is written, and an entity's reactors are written in the model's order. AutoCAD links the pair both ways (the annotation lists its leader in its reactors, as every LEADER of the AutoCAD-saved corpus samples shows), so the reactors may change only to keep that link. Refused: an annotation that is not MTEXT, a TOLERANCE or a block reference, sits in another block or does not list the leader among its reactors, an old annotation that still lists it, a reactor added that is not a leader annotating the entity, a reactor dropped that is not a leader's or whose leader still annotates the entity, and an ATTRIB's reactor changes. Left: a table's grid and formats (rows and columns, merges, cell formats, overrides) and new tables, a HATCH's boundary objects (with their reactors), a VIEWPORT's clip boundary, annotative MULTILEADERs, and the other non-entity changes above (block records and the other table records and TABLESTYLE objects, removing a record or an image object, objects other than draw order tables, multileader styles and the image objects, layouts, block definitions (the engine's change sets do not carry them yet: BLOCK, M5), moving entities and reordering a block's own entity list, xdata / reactors other than a leader's / extension dictionaries). *Part 16 (#166):* layer transparency, for the LAYER palette's Transparency column and LAYER TR: AutoCAD keeps it as `AcCmTransparency` xdata on the layer (one 1071, 0x02000000 | alpha, and 0 for an opaque layer, as every layer of the AutoCAD-saved sample_AC1027 but its 70 % one has it), so the save rewrites that value from the layer's transparency and registers the application, above every handle the engine handed out, when the drawing has none (it uses the drawing's own, whatever its case, or one the same save adds). Refused: ByLayer or ByBlock, an alpha that is not a whole number from 0 to 255, more than 90 % transparent (alpha below 25, the LAYER dialog's limit) and any transparency in R12. *Part 17 (#167):* an associative HATCH's boundary objects and the reactors that point back at it, for HATCH with associativity, HATCHEDIT (Associative off) and swapping a loop's boundary: each loop lists the objects it keeps to (DXF 97 and 330, DWG soft pointers), and each lists the hatch in its reactors, as every associative hatch of the corpus shows; a new hatch may keep to a boundary the same save adds (BOUNDARY and HATCH Pick points make it first). Refused: boundary objects on a hatch that is not associative, an associative hatch without any, an object listed twice, one that is not an entity of the drawing or sits in another block, one that is not a line, arc, circle, ellipse, spline, 2D polyline or region (text, TEXT or MTEXT, only in a text box loop, and only text there), one that does not list the hatch among its reactors, an old one that still does, a reactor added that is neither a leader annotating the entity nor a hatch it bounds, and a reactor dropped whose hatch still names the entity. Left after part 17: a table's grid and formats, new tables, a VIEWPORT's clip boundary, annotative MULTILEADERs, the other non-entity changes listed in part 15's Left, and reactors other than a leader's or a hatch's (groups, associative dimensions). *Part 18 (#169):* a VIEWPORT's clip boundary and the reactor that points back at it, for VPCLIP (Select Object, Polygonal and Delete) and MVIEW Object and Polygonal: the viewport names the entity that clips it (DXF 340, DWG hard pointer) and sets non-rectangular clipping (DXF 90 bit 65536), and the entity lists the viewport in its reactors, as every clipped viewport of the corpus shows (text_in_viewports.dxf); a viewport may be clipped by a polygon the same save adds, and a clip object may go with its clip. Refused: a clip boundary without the 65536 bit or the bit without one, an object that is not an entity of the drawing or sits in another block, one that is not a closed polyline (2D), circle, full ellipse, closed spline or region (AutoCAD 2024 VPCLIP), one that does not list the viewport among its reactors or already clips another viewport, an old one that still does, a reactor added that is not a leader annotating the entity, a hatch it bounds or a viewport it clips, and a reactor dropped whose viewport is still clipped by the entity. Left after part 18: a table's grid and formats, new tables, annotative MULTILEADERs, the other non-entity changes listed in part 15's Left, and reactors other than a leader's, a hatch's or a viewport's (groups, associative dimensions). *Part 19 (#170):* an entity's and an ATTRIB's xdata, new or edited, for COPY, MIRROR and ARRAY (the new entities take the source's xdata), HYPERLINK (PE_URL) and DIMOVERRIDE (the dimension's ACAD DSTYLE xdata): the applications are written in the model's order, an unchanged one keeping the records it was read with, and every group code AutoCAD defines (1000, 1002–1005, 1010–1013, 1040–1042, 1070, 1071) is written as given, a 1005 naming no object included, as AutoCAD keeps one to an erased object. acad-ts lost entity xdata in three places, now patched: its DWG writer wrote such a 1005 as 0 (0149), and its DXF reader gave an R2018 MTEXT's xdata to its column data (0148) and a multiline attribute's to its embedded MTEXT (0150). Refused: an application the drawing does not register (one the same save adds may be named) or listed twice, an application without values (AutoCAD drops it), a value of the wrong kind or out of range, a string over 255 characters or with a line break, a binary chunk over 127 bytes, braces (1002) that do not pair up, more than 16 KB, `AcadAnnotative` xdata changes (the annotative property, with its context data, is not written yet), an R12 viewport's ACAD xdata (its view, which the save writes from the model) and any xdata before Release 11 (AC1009), which introduced it. Left after part 19: a table's grid and formats, new tables, annotative entities and MULTILEADERs, plot style names, extension dictionaries, the other non-entity changes listed in part 15's Left, and reactors other than a leader's, a hatch's or a viewport's (groups, associative dimensions). *Part 20 (#173):* LAYOUT objects are written, edited, for LAYOUT Rename, dragging a layout tab, LIMITS in a layout, PSLTSCALE and PAGESETUP: a layout's name (its DXF 1 and its ACAD_LAYOUT dictionary entry, renamed in place, so two layouts may trade names), tab order, PSLTSCALE and LIMCHECK flags, limits and page setup (paper size and its width and height, margins, paper units, rotation, plot offset, custom scale, plot area and plot flags). Layouts join the engine's change sets as updates (`ChangeSetBuilder.updateLayout`, `ChangeSet.layouts`; `DocumentModel.layouts` stays in tab order); adding or removing one is LAYOUT New and Delete (M6), which bring its block record. AutoCAD states the current layouts' limits and flags in the header too ($LIMMIN/$LIMMAX and $LIMCHECK for Model; $PLIMMIN/$PLIMMAX, $PLIMCHECK and $PSLTSCALE for the layout on `*Paper_Space`), so where the header agreed with the layout, a change must change both. Refused: adding or removing a layout, a layout moving to another block record, any layout change before R2000 (which introduced layouts), renaming Model, naming a layout Model, a duplicate, empty, over-255-character name or one with < > / \ " : ; ? * | , = or a backquote, a Model tab order other than 0 or layout tab orders other than 1 to N, layout flags other than 1 and 2, limits whose upper right corner is not above and right of the lower left, a paper size of 0 or less, negative margins, units, rotation or plot area outside DXF 72, 73 and 74, plot area View or Window (the view name and window are not mapped), Layout on Model, plot flags AutoCAD does not define, a ModelType flag change, a standard scale (flag 16 on: DXF 75 is not mapped) and a custom scale change while flag 16 stays on, and xdata, reactor and extension dictionary changes. Left after part 20: a table's grid and formats, new tables, annotative entities and MULTILEADERs, plot style names, extension dictionaries, new or removed layouts, block records and definitions, moving and reordering entities, objects other than those written so far, and reactors other than a leader's, a hatch's or a viewport's (groups, associative dimensions). *Part 21 (#176):* an entity's (and an ATTRIB's) plot style name and material, new or edited, for the Properties palette's Plot style and Material rows and COPY. A plot style name is a hard pointer to an ACDBPLACEHOLDER in ACAD_PLOTSTYLENAME (DXF 390; in a DWG the plot style flags 11 and the pointer), which acad-ts read nowhere and wrote as ByLayer (patch 0154); a material (DXF 347, DWG R2007+) was read and written by neither (0152), so the five corpus entities with one lost it on every read, and a DWG layer's plot style name was dropped on read and written as 0 (0153). A plot style named ByLayer, ByBlock or the dictionary default (DWG flags only: DXF has no group for them) maps as none. Refused: a plot style name before R2000 or in a colour-dependent drawing ($PSTYLEMODE 1, as the save leaves it), one that is not an ACAD_PLOTSTYLENAME entry or its default, and a material before R2007. DWG and DXF give rows and cells no identity, so the edited table states where each row and column came from (`TableGeometry.rowOrigins` / `columnOrigins`: the row's index in the table as read, or null for a new one; DEC-046), and the save (`export/table-grid.ts`) moves each acad-ts row, column and cell to its new place with everything Triya does not map, then writes the table against what each cell was. A new row or column takes the cell style of the one it follows, or of the next one when it comes first (AutoCAD formats an inserted row or column like its neighbour; which side is not documented), its new cells override nothing of their own, and the merged ranges (DWG R2010+) and the break range holding every row (every R2010+ DWG table has one) follow the rows. A new one joins its dimension's dictionary, which the save makes (hard owner, keep existing, as AutoCAD) when the dimension has none and the model gives it one; the dimension and each point's geometry list the DIMASSOC in their reactors, and geometry no point keeps to lets go of it (reactors.ts). AutoCAD does not document what ERASE of associated geometry does to the DIMASSOC, so dropping that point or the whole DIMASSOC are both accepted; one still naming erased geometry or an erased dimension is refused. acad-ts patch 0166 exports the DIMASSOC object class (`DimensionAssociationObject`; the name `DimensionAssociation` is the DIMASSOC variable's enum), and 0167 keeps each point's geometry through a DXF read (the reader dropped it, so every DXF DIMASSOC of the corpus had lost its geometry and was saved with `331 0`) and writes the xref paths and an intersection's second object. A cell style that stated nothing is begun as AutoCAD writes one overriding nothing (content flowing, background None, scale 1), and an edge shown again keeps its visibility bit with the edge visible, so it shows whatever its row says. New materials and new or edited visual styles are left out: PRD non-goal 13 excludes rendering, visual styles and materials (needs-human issue #195). *Part 38 (#196):* table-wide overrides of edited and new tables, as the Properties palette sets them with the whole table selected, and cell rotations in a DWG from R2010 (`export/table-overrides.ts`). DXF and DWG before R2010 take the changed margins and row-type values (alignment, text style and height, content colour, fill or no fill) into the flag word and values; acad-ts writes a DXF table's override codes as kept (DEC-043), so they are written again from the flag word as AutoCAD lists them (93–96, then title / header suppression and flow direction as read, 40 / 41, the row values by code in data, title, header order). A DWG from R2010 states the margins in the table's own cell style and a row type's values in the cell style of each row of the type (the row's style id names `_TITLE`, `_HEADER` or `_DATA`), so a changed value goes into every row of the type and a value taken off leaves them all; the cells fold their rows in, so each cell is then written against what it maps as on its new row, and checked the same way on copies first. A DWG from R2010 cell's rotation is a content property folded content format over cell over row over column like the formats: the mapper now reads it from there (it read the per-cell field, always 0 in such a file), and the save writes it into the content format when that rotates the content already, the cell's own cell style otherwise, and takes it off both when the row or column gives the cell that rotation. Refused: margins whose left and right or top and bottom differ outside a DWG from R2010, true colours where the format holds ACI, an override change in a DXF table stating border overrides (94–96, whose values acad-ts cannot place, M1-81), and in a DWG from R2010 an override for a row type the table has no row of. No corpus table rotates a cell; the corpus gate (`export-table-overrides.corpus.test.ts`) changes every table's margins, title text height and data alignment, takes off the header's overrides and turns a cell. Left after part 38: extension dictionaries other than a block's draw order table's, a dimension's DIMASSOC's and an annotative entity's context data, objects other than those written so far, and reactors other than a leader's, a hatch's, a viewport's, a group's or a DIMASSOC's (new materials and visual styles are out of scope, issue #195). *Part 39 (#198):* REGIONs moved, turned and scaled, as MOVE, ROTATE and SCALE change them (`export/region.ts`, `acis/transform.ts`). Triya maps a REGION as the plan-view boundary of its ACIS body and cannot make or reshape a body, so the save recovers the motion (a turn about +Z, a uniform scale and a move, and a new elevation) from the edited boundary, edge for edge, checks it to the boundary's DEC-036 tolerance, and composes it into the body's transform record (a 3 × 3 matrix on row vectors, translation, scale and the rotate / reflect / shear flags), edited in place in SAT text (keywords, or 1 / 0 in R14's save version 106) or SAB binary, so every face, edge and curve, with its parameter range, stays byte for byte as read; a DWG REGION's point moves with it. Every corpus REGION, R14 to R2018, has a transform record. Refused: a new REGION (COPY would need the source's ACIS body), a boundary that is not the original moved, turned or scaled, a mirrored one (a reflecting transform reverses the sense of the body's faces and curves, which no corpus file shows AutoCAD saving), a change of plane direction, a body without a transform record and a DWG REGION with isolines or silhouettes. The corpus gate (`export-region.corpus.test.ts`) turns, scales and moves the 20 corpus REGIONs and refuses a mirror; `export.corpus.test.ts` moves them. *Part 40 (#201):* XCLIP clips (SPATIAL_FILTER objects, `export/xclips.ts`), as XCLIP ON / OFF, Clipdepth, New boundary, Delete and a first clip change them, and as COPY and ERASE of a clipped reference carry them: an edited filter is written by handle, field for field; a new one becomes the `SPATIAL` entry of the `ACAD_FILTER` dictionary in its reference's extension dictionary, the save making either dictionary under the engine's handle when missing (the reference's new `extDict`, the filter's `owner`), as AutoCAD makes them (hard owner, keep existing; the filter lists the ACAD_FILTER dictionary in its reactors, that dictionary the extension dictionary); a removed one leaves its ACAD_FILTER dictionary in place (AutoCAD does not document whether Delete removes it; an empty one is valid, and a later clip goes back in it). Refused: a clip before R14 (Release 14 introduced XCLIP), on a MINSERT or anything but a block reference, a second clip of one reference, one outside its reference's ACAD_FILTER dictionary, one left behind by its erased reference, a changed INSERT or owner, a new boundary on a clip with an extension dictionary (an inverted clip keeps its inner boundary there in an ACAD_XREC_ROUNDTRIP record the save does not map), a boundary that encloses nothing, matrices that are not 12 finite numbers, a singular inverse, and xdata, reactor and extension dictionary changes. The corpus gate (`export-xclips.corpus.test.ts`) turns off, deepens, reshapes and deletes the clips of the 21 clipped corpus files and clips a reference in every R14+ file that has one. Left after part 40: extension dictionaries other than a block's draw order table's, a dimension's DIMASSOC's, an XCLIPped reference's and an annotative entity's context data, objects other than those written so far, reactors other than a leader's, a hatch's, a viewport's, a group's or a DIMASSOC's, and geometry writers for OLE2FRAME and ACAD_PROXY_ENTITY (no corpus file, M1-40) and for new or mirrored REGIONs. *Close-out (#214):* the acceptance is checked as a test: `export.corpus.test.ts` fails when a type with a geometry writer (`WRITABLE_TYPES`) is edited on no corpus file. TRACE was the one left (written since M1-79, unit cases only), so the thread-authored `self-authored/traces.dxf` (`scripts/corpus/traces.py`, ezdxf 1.4.5) adds mitred wide-line segments, an elevated and thick TRACE, one on a −Z extrusion, a triangle and TRACEs in a block, and the gate moves them. Every type a markup command of M2-06–M2-12 creates (LINE, LWPOLYLINE, CIRCLE, ARC, TEXT, MTEXT, WIPEOUT, HATCH, MULTILEADER, the dimensions, INSERT with ATTRIBs) is written new or edited, as M2-13 needs. The leftovers listed after part 40 take no new part here (re-scope 2026-11-08): each is added, DXF only, by the M2+ task whose command first needs it; M2-06 carries the REGION ones (COPY and MIRROR), and OLE2FRAME and ACAD_PROXY_ENTITY writers wait on M1-40's corpus files (post-launch).
- [x] **M1-76** acad-ts: keep the DWG colour method None (CMC/ENC 0xC8). `readCmColor` reads it as true-colour black. **Acceptance:** a None colour reads as its own value and writes back as 0xC8, and a true black stays black. *Why:* found in M1-36: a cell or table style background of None reads as black (0,0,0) RGB. The mapper takes black as no fill for table backgrounds, so a real black fill is mapped as none until this lands. *Done (#131, acad-ts patch 0120):* method 0xC8 reads as ACI 257 (`kACInone`, what the DXF saves state) in the CMC readers and the ENC complex colour, and the CMC writers write 257 back as 0xC8; the table mapper reads 257 as no fill and keeps black.
- [x] (#134) **M1-77** `AcadToTriya` table-wide overrides: margins, per-row-type text style, height, alignment and colours, and border overrides. DXF states them in 93–96 and the values after them, which acad-ts keeps raw (DEC-043). The R2004/R2007 DWG reader reads them and throws them away. R2010+ states them as the table content's cell style. Also the DXF cell edge visibility codes (289/285/286/288), which acad-ts does not read and whose value convention no corpus file shows. **Acceptance:** they compare equal in `entities.corpus.test.ts`, and DWG matches its DXF save. *Why:* M1-36 maps per-cell formats only. Only a table-wide override changes the margins of a DXF table. *Done (#134):* acad-ts patch 0126 reads 93 and its values into `TableEntity.tableOverrides` (DXF, and DWG R2004/R2007, which had dropped them); `TableGeometry.overrides` holds the margins and each row type's text style, height, alignment, content colour and fill; R2010+ takes the margins from the table cell style and a row type's values from what every row of that type overrides alike. DXF repeats a per-row-type value in AcDb::RowType order (data, title, header), DWG in bit order (title, header, data). The corpus states only margins and text heights (table 528); the rest follow the ODA specification. Border overrides and cell edge visibility moved to M1-81.
- [x] (#133) **M1-79** acad-ts losses M1-32 listed that neither mapping oracle reports, each checked against the corpus first and fixed as a patch with a failing-first test where it still happens: `TRACE` read as `SOLID` (so a save writes SOLID), MULTILEADER colours 91/93/94 kept as raw integers on DXF read, the `IMAGE`/`WIPEOUT` clip boundary type (71) skipped on DXF read (the mapper infers it), `ACAD_TABLE` in R14/R2000 DWG read as an unknown entity, no reader for `DWFUNDERLAY`/`DGNUNDERLAY` (needs the M1-40 corpus file), R12 `ATTRIB`s without a `7` left on a detached default style, and `\U+` escapes kept as text in DWG strings before R2007. **Acceptance:** each item fixed with a patch and test, or shown not to happen on the corpus, or blocked on a named missing file. *Why:* M1-32's acceptance (both `ACAD_TS_GAPS` lists empty) is met by part 4 (#129), but these losses are outside what the oracles compare, so they need their own task rather than being dropped. *Done (#133):* acad-ts patches 0121 (TRACE kept as TRACE; no corpus file has one, tested in memory), 0122 (MULTILEADER/MLEADERSTYLE AcCmEntityColor groups decoded and written with their method byte), 0123 (IMAGE/WIPEOUT 71), 0124 (R14/R2000 DWG ACAD_TABLE, maps like the DXF save) and 0125 (pre-R2007 DWG `\U+` escapes). R12 ATTRIBs without a 7 already take the table's STANDARD style on the corpus (guard test, no patch). DWF/DGN underlay readers stay blocked on M1-40's corpus file.
- [x] **M1-80** acad-ts: write DXF `POLYLINE` 75 (curve and smooth surface type) for 2D and 3D polylines, not only for polygon meshes. The DXF reader keeps it, so a quadratic (5) or Bezier (8) spline-fit polyline saved to DXF reads back as cubic. **Acceptance:** a DXF save of a quadratic and a Bezier spline-fit POLYLINE maps back to its fit type (`packages/io/test/export.test.ts`), and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* found by M1-75 part 1; no corpus file has a spline-fit polyline, so the round-trip suite cannot see it. *Done (#136):* acad-ts patch 0127 writes 75 for every non-mesh POLYLINE with a smooth type, so quadratic, cubic and Bezier fits read back as saved; checked by `export.test.ts` (edited and new, 2D and 3D) and the patch test.
- [x] (#138) **M1-82** acad-ts: read DXF `DIMENSION` 41 (text line spacing factor), which the reader skips for every dimension (and, on an ARC_DIMENSION, takes for the arc's end angle only after `AcDbArcDimension`), and write 52 (the extension lines' oblique angle) for aligned and rotated dimensions, which the writer leaves out though the reader keeps it. **Acceptance:** a DXF save of a dimension with line spacing 1.5 and an oblique angle maps back to both (`packages/io/test/export-annotations.test.ts`), and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* found by M1-75 part 3: the writers set both, and the DWG reader and writer keep them, but a DXF save loses them; the round-trip suite compares acad-ts with itself, so it cannot see either loss. *Done (#138):* acad-ts patch 0128 reads 41 outside `AcDbArcDimension` as the line spacing factor, writes 52 when set, and reads and writes 52 in degrees (it was taken as radians), also where ezdxf writes it (in `AcDbDimension`).
- [x] (#141) **M1-83** acad-ts: write DXF `PDFUNDERLAY` (and DWF/DGN underlay) 210 (the normal), 91/11 (the clip boundary) and 290 (clip inverted), and check the DXF reader reads them back; the DXF writer writes only 340, 10, 41–43, 50 and 280–282, though the DWG writer keeps all of them. **Acceptance:** a DXF save of a −Z underlay with a polygonal clip maps back (`packages/io/test/export-fills.test.ts`, which refuses both on a DXF save until then), and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* found by M1-75 part 4; no corpus underlay has either, so the round-trip suite cannot see it. *Done (#141):* acad-ts patch 0129 writes 210 after the rotation when the normal is not +Z and the clip boundary as repeated 11/21 pairs after the fade, and the DXF reader reads the pairs back; the export no longer refuses an underlay's normal or clip on a DXF save. The DXF reference (UNDERLAY) has no 91 count and no 290 for underlays (those are IMAGE codes): an inverted clip is bit 16 ("clip is inside mode") of the 280 flags, which already round-tripped. acad-ts has no DWF/DGN underlay entity (M1-32 notes no reader), so there is nothing to write for them.
- [x] (#142) **M1-84** acad-ts: read DXF `MULTILEADER` 342 (the arrowhead block), 344 (the content block) and, in the context data, 341 (the content block) and each `LEADER_LINE{`'s 91 (its index); the DXF reader skips all four, though the writer writes them and the DWG reader keeps them. **Acceptance:** a DXF save of a block multileader with an arrowhead block and two leader lines maps back (`packages/io/test/export-mleader-viewport.test.ts`, whose `readBack` drops the four for DXF until then), and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* found by M1-75 part 5; every corpus multileader is mtext with the default arrowhead and one line, so neither the round-trip suite nor the mapping oracle sees it. *Done (#142):* acad-ts patch 0130 reads 342 and 344 into the multileader template's arrowhead and content block handles, context 341 into the context's block record handle and LEADER_LINE 91 into the line index; `export-mleader-viewport.test.ts` no longer drops them for DXF and saves a block multileader with an arrowhead block, a changed content block and a second leader line to DXF and DWG.
- [x] (#143) **M1-85** acad-ts: write the DWG `VIEWPORT` style sheet (DXF 1) and visual style (348, with the background and shade plot handles), which the DWG writer writes as empty and null though its reader keeps them. **Acceptance:** a DWG save of a viewport with a style sheet and visual style maps back (`packages/io/test/export-mleader-viewport.test.ts`, which refuses both on a DWG save until then), and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* found by M1-75 part 5; the round-trip suite compares acad-ts with itself, so it cannot see the loss, which every DWG save of a viewport with a plot style table makes today. *Done (#143, acad-ts patch 0131):* the DWG writer writes the style sheet (R2000+) and the visual style (R2007+), and the DWG reader keeps the visual style it used to throw away (33 of the 51 corpus DWG viewports name one); the background and shade plot handles stay null, as `Viewport` has no field for them and the DXF reader drops them too. Export refuses only what the target version cannot store (a style sheet before R2000, a visual style before R2007, in DWG and DXF alike) and a DWG visual style on a DXF save (M1-88); the DXF writer no longer names a visual style it does not write.
- [x] (#145) **M1-86** Write an R12 `VIEWPORT`'s ACAD `MVIEW` xdata (view target, direction, twist, height and centre, lens, clip planes, view-mode flags and frozen layers) from its Triya geometry, so an R12 save keeps a changed view; patch 0119 reads it, but the xdata is written back as read. **Acceptance:** an R12 DXF save of an edited view maps back (`packages/testkit/src/export/export.corpus.test.ts`, which refuses it until then), and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* found by M1-75 part 5; Triya does not write xdata yet. *Done (#145):* a save that changes an R12 viewport's view rewrites the MVIEW xdata from the viewport (`packages/io/src/export/r12-viewport.ts`, the inverse of patch 0119): target, direction, twist, view height and centre, lens, clip planes, VIEWMODE and the fast zoom, UCS icon, snap, grid, isometric snap, isopair and hidden-in-plot 1070s; the version, circle zoom, snap and grid settings are written as read and the nested frozen-layer group stays as read. Refused: frozen layers (M1-90), a new R12 viewport (M1-89), the R2000 plot style table, render mode and visual style, flags R12 cannot state, both isometric planes, and an off flag that disagrees with status 0.
- [x] **M1-87** acad-ts: write the DWG header variables its DWG writer drops (`$DIMLTYPE`, `$DIMLTEX1`, `$DIMLTEX2` and `$DIMLDRBLK`, written as null handles; `$DIMMZF`, `$REALWORLDSCALE`, `$SAVEIMAGES` and `$STEPSPERSEC`) and the DXF `$DIMTFILLCLR`, which its DXF writer skips (marked Ignored); check each against the DWG header layout of its version. **Acceptance:** each comes off `DWG_LOSSES` / the Ignored refusal in `packages/io/src/export/header.ts` and `export-header.corpus.test.ts` shows it maps back on every corpus DWG and DXF version that stores it; the round trip stays clean. *Why:* found by M1-75 part 6: a changed value of any of them is refused on that save today, and the round-trip suite cannot see the loss, since an unchanged value never differs. *Done (#147, patch 0132):* the DWG writer writes DIMLDRBLK (R2000+) and DIMLTYPE, DIMLTEX1, DIMLTEX2 (R2007+) by record, DIMMZF in the R2010+ slot acad-ts had read and written as DIMFIT, and REALWORLDSCALE in the R2007+ bit it wrote as 0; the DWG reader reads them back (DIMLDRBLK no longer overwrites DIMBLK); the DXF writer writes `$DIMTFILLCLR`. The R2007+ corpus DWGs now read the dimension linetypes their DXF saves state. What DWG does not store per the ODA header layout moved to the version lists (DIMLDRBLK before R2000; DIMLTYPE, DIMLTEX1/2, REALWORLDSCALE, STEPSPERSEC before R2007; DIMMZF before R2010; DIMFIT from R2000; SAVEIMAGES in every DWG), and STEPSPERSEC outside AutoCAD's 1–30, which the DWG reader drops, is refused.
- [x] (#149) **M1-88** acad-ts: write DXF `VISUALSTYLE` objects read from DWG (or made in memory), which the DXF writer writes only as the raw codes it read from DXF (patch 0021); a DWG saved as DXF leaves every visual style out, and since patch 0131 a viewport naming one leaves 348 out with it. **Acceptance:** a DWG viewport's visual style maps back through a DXF save (`packages/io/test/export-mleader-viewport.test.ts`, which refuses it until then), and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* found by M1-85, whose DWG reader fix made DWG visual styles reach the model. *Done:* acad-ts patch 0133 writes VISUALSTYLE from its fields in AutoCAD's R2007, R2010 and R2013+ layouts (code for code as AutoCAD's DXF saves of the four sample DWGs, except one value AutoCAD stored differently in the R2007 DWG), and the viewport writer names any visual style in 348; export no longer refuses a DWG visual style on a DXF save. Its reader puts the entities an R12 DXF marks 67 = 1 into `*Model_Space`, which has no layout, and its writer writes them without 67, so an R12 save moves every paper-space entity (the sample's two viewports and two more) into model space. **Acceptance:** they read into `*Paper_Space` with the layout acad-ts makes for it (or keep 67), an R12 save of `sample_AC1009_ascii.dxf` states 67 = 1 on the same four entities as the original, a new R12 viewport saves (`export.corpus.test.ts`, which refuses it until then), and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* found by M1-86; the round-trip suite compares acad-ts with itself, so it cannot see the loss. *Done (#151, acad-ts patch 0134):* an R12 entity that states 67 = 1 reads into `*Paper_Space`, which gets a made-up Layout1 (tab 1, header paper-space limits, extents and PSLTSCALE), so the app shows it as a layout tab and a save writes 67 = 1 on the sample's BLOCK, ENDBLK and two viewports again; a new R12 viewport saves in paper space with MVIEW xdata of its own (version 16, empty frozen-layer group). `structuralDiff` takes `handleOfB` to compare a save that moved R12's made-up handles.
- [x] (#153) **M1-90** acad-ts: read an R12 viewport's frozen layers, the 1003 layer names in the nested group of its MVIEW xdata, into `Viewport.frozenLayers` (patch 0119 stops at that group), and write them there from Triya's geometry. **Acceptance:** an R12 DXF save of a viewport freezing and thawing layers maps back (`export.corpus.test.ts`, which refuses it until then); no corpus R12 viewport freezes a layer, so the test authors one. *Why:* found by M1-86: an R12 viewport's frozen layers read as none today. *Done (#153, acad-ts patch 0135):* the template reads the nested 1003 names into `frozenLayers`, the R12 DXF writer writes no 331, and `writeR12View` writes the group from the viewport's frozen layers, for edited and new viewports.
- [x] **M1-91** acad-ts: read DXF `VISUALSTYLE` objects into their fields (the R2007, R2010 and R2013+ layouts patch 0133 writes), so a DXF saved as DWG keeps its visual styles; the DXF reader assigns no field (0021 keeps only the codes), so the DWG writer writes each one with the class defaults. **Acceptance:** every corpus DXF's styles read as the same drawing's DWG does (the four acadsharp samples), and a DXF → DWG save keeps them. *Why:* found by M1-88; DXF → DWG saves of the corpus fail on other objects acad-ts cannot write to DWG today (TABLECONTENT, CELLSTYLEMAP), so this loss is not yet reachable from Triya. *Done (#154, acad-ts patch 0136):* the DXF reader reads all three layouts into the DWG reader's fields, operations, extended properties and extended data; the four acadsharp DXF pairs (ASCII and binary) match their DWGs field for field (bar AutoCAD's own Realistic R2007 lighting quality), R2000/R2004 DXF match the R2007 DWG, and R2007, R2010 and R2018 DXFs saved as DWG keep every field.
- [x] (#156) **M1-92** acad-ts DIMSTYLE save gaps found by M1-75 part 9: its DXF writer leaves DIMARCSYM (90) out when it is 1 (above), so it reads back 0; writes no DIMTXTDIRECTION (the DXF reference gives 294, acad-ts's reader takes 295); and writes DIMTFILLCLR (the second 70) only when DIMTFILL is not 0; its DXF reader reads DIMUNIT (270) into DIMLUNIT; its R2000+ DWG reader reads DIMATFIT, the record's last BS before the lineweights (ODA spec §20.4.53), into DIMFIT and its writer writes 3 there, so every R2000+ DWG reads DIMATFIT 3. **Acceptance:** an acad-ts patch per gap with a test failing on the unpatched code; `export-dimstyles.test.ts` saves each one where it now refuses it, and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* `export.ts` refuses these changes until then (`acadTsLosses` in `dim-styles.ts`), and a DWG's DIMATFIT maps wrong on reading. *Done (#156, acad-ts patches 0137–0141):* the DXF writer leaves out only DIMARCSYM 0, reads and writes DIMTXTDIRECTION as 294 (R2010+), always writes DIMTFILLCLR, reads 270 into DIMUNIT and writes it to R13/R14; R2000+ DWG reads and writes DIMATFIT in its own place (DIMFIT then reads 3, as from a DXF without 287); `export.ts` no longer refuses any of them.
- [x] **M1-93** acad-ts VPORT read losses found by M1-75 part 10: its DWG reader sets the view mode bits it reads onto VPort's default (16, front clip not at eye), so every DWG viewport reads with bit 16, and assigns rather than adds the UCS icon's on and at-origin bits, so an icon that is off reads on at the origin; its DXF reader keeps only the first VPORT of a name (failsafe duplicate check), so a drawing's second tiled `*Active` viewport, or a saved configuration's second tile, is dropped. **Acceptance:** an acad-ts patch per loss with a test failing on the unpatched code; `export-view-records.test.ts` saves each where it now refuses it (`dwgVportLosses` and the DXF duplicate-name check in `view-records.ts`), and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* a DWG's viewport view mode and UCS icon map wrong on reading, and a DXF with tiled viewports loses all but one. *Done (#158):* acad-ts patches 0143 (DWG view mode from 0), 0144 (DWG UCS icon bits added up on reading and written apart) and 0145 (DXF keeps repeated VPORT names); the save no longer refuses these edits.
- [x] **M1-94** acad-ts: read MLEADERSTYLE DXF 341 (arrowhead) and 343 (content block): its DXF reader has no case for them, so the template's `arrowheadHandle` and `blockContentHandle` stay unset and every DXF style reads with the default arrowhead and no block (its DWG reader and both writers keep them). **Acceptance:** an acad-ts patch with a test failing on the unpatched code; `export-mleader-styles.test.ts` saves both to DXF where it now refuses them (`DXF_LOST` in `mleader-styles.ts`), and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* found by M1-75 part 11; no corpus style names an arrowhead block or content block, so the mapping oracle could not see it. *Done (#160):* acad-ts patch 0146 reads both into the template; DXF saves of an edited or new style keep them, and the refusal is gone.
- [x] **M1-95** Rename dictionary-named objects on save: acad-ts's `CadDictionary` keys an entry by the name it was added under, and renaming the object leaves the key (the DXF entry name) as it was; removing and re-adding it unassigns it from the document and moves it to the end. Add a minimal acad-ts `rename` that keeps the entry's place, handle and links, then let `mleader-styles.ts` write a renamed MLEADERSTYLE (and `table-styles.ts` a TABLESTYLE, `image-objects.ts` an IMAGEDEF, the image name the External References palette renames, and MLINESTYLE when its writer comes). **Acceptance:** a renamed MLEADERSTYLE saves to DXF and DWG with the entry in place, `CMLEADERSTYLE` and the MULTILEADERs naming it follow, and the round trip stays clean. *Why:* the multileader style manager (M4-04) renames styles; part 11 refuses it. *Done (#164):* acad-ts patch 0147 adds `CadDictionary.rename`, which swaps an entry's key where it stands; MLEADERSTYLE, TABLESTYLE (before R2010, M1-96) and IMAGEDEF renames save to DXF and DWG in place, styles may trade names and a new one may take a freed name, and `CMLEADERSTYLE`/`CTABLESTYLE` must follow the style they name (looked up without regard to case). The corpus edit renames every style but Standard and every image definition on 106 files with a clean diff. MLINESTYLE waits for its writer.
- [x] **M1-96** R2010+ table styles: map the named cell styles a TABLESTYLE holds from R2010 (DWG: the table cell style with the margins, flow direction and suppression flags, then `_TITLE`, `_HEADER`, `_DATA` and user cell styles, each with its content format and six grid formats; DXF: the same in the CELLSTYLEMAP its extension dictionary names under `ACAD_ROUNDTRIP_2008_TABLESTYLE_CELLSTYLEMAP`) so that `tableStyle()` gives an R2010+ DWG the values its DXF save gives (today alignment 0, text heights in title/header/data order and the row-style defaults for margins and flow), and write them, keeping the DXF row styles and CELLSTYLEMAP in step. **Acceptance:** the acadsharp R2010–R2018 DWG and DXF samples map their Standard table style alike, and the M1-75 part 12 edits save and map back on every R2010+ corpus file. *Why:* found by M1-75 part 12, which refuses table style changes from R2010 because a change written to the row styles alone could be undone by the cell styles AutoCAD reads back. *Part 1 (#168):* an R2010+ DWG's table styles map as their DXF saves state them: the data, title and header rows are taken from the `_DATA`, `_TITLE` and `_HEADER` cell styles by id (acad-ts lists them as stored), a row's alignment from its content format, its fill from its background colour (None, ByBlock or ByLayer is fill off with AutoCAD's DXF colour 7), and the flow direction and margins from the style's "Table" cell style (flow up is its 0x10000 flag, per the ODA spec's cell style merge flags; no corpus style flows up), with no suppress flags; `tables.corpus.test.ts` compares every acadsharp DWG's table styles with its ASCII and binary DXF saves (AC1015–AC1032). Left: user cell styles and each cell style's own margins and merge flags in the record (DWG, and the DXF CELLSTYLEMAP, which acad-ts reads into a separate object), and writing R2010+ styles with the DXF row styles and CELLSTYLEMAP in step (acad-ts's DXF writer would write a DWG-read R2010+ style's rows with alignment 0 and fill colour 257 today). *Part 2 (#174):* table style edits save from R2010 on: an R2010+ DWG's are written into its named cell styles (a row's alignment into the content format, its fill as the background colour, None when unfilled; the flow direction as the Table cell style's merge flag; the margins into the Table cell style and every named cell style that had the table's), a new R2010+ DWG style is made with AutoCAD's `_TITLE`/`_HEADER`/`_DATA`/Table cell styles, and the CELLSTYLEMAP AutoCAD keeps beside the rows in every DXF (all versions) is kept in step with them (acad-ts patch 0151 reads and writes its text height at 144 and background at 62); refused from R2010 in a DWG: the style flags, the suppress settings and a fill colour on an unfilled row, which have no place there. `export.corpus.test.ts` saves the M1-75 part 12 edits (and the M1-95 renames) on every corpus file of every version, and `tables.corpus.test.ts` checks every corpus CELLSTYLEMAP says what its rows say. Left: user cell styles (M1-99) and the CELLSTYLEMAP gaps (M1-98).
- [x] **M1-97** Follow layout change sets on screen: the app's layout tabs (name and order) and the renderer's sheet (paper size, margins, rotation, offset, scale and limits) update when a change set carries `layouts` (M1-75 part 20 added them; no command emits them yet). **Acceptance:** a Playwright test renames and reorders tabs and changes a layout's page setup through a change set, and the tabs and sheet follow without reopening the drawing; undo restores them. *Why:* found by M1-75 part 20, which made layouts editable in the model and the save; the app and renderer still read layouts once, on open (needed by LAYOUT Rename and PAGESETUP, M6). *Done (#175):* the layout tabs and the file tab preview list the layouts with the engine's layout updates applied (`layoutsOf`), the renderer re-reads the sheet, paper units and PSLTSCALE of the layout it shows, the canvas controller its sheet, name and the header limits for ZOOM Extents and All, and the render replica rebases a cloned layout change set. `EngineSession` takes layout edits (name, tab position renumbering the tabs 1 to N, page setup), the front end LAYOUT Rename, tab dragging and PAGESETUP will use (M6); until then the e2e test sends them through the dev-server-only `window.triyaDev` hook, and undoes them with the inverse edits (the U command is M2-05; the inverse change set is checked in Node).
- [x] **M1-98** CELLSTYLEMAP fidelity left by M1-96 part 2: acad-ts's DXF TABLEFORMAT reader and writer misplace 170 (the data flag, read as the cell alignment), 91 (the override flags, written back as the style class, which the CELLSTYLE_BEGIN 91 then overwrites), 93 (the content layout flags, not written) and GRIDFORMAT 90/91 (override flags and border type, swapped against the DWG), and acad-ts keeps a DWG's roundtrip CELLSTYLEMAP before R2010 unread (`UnknownNonGraphicalObject`), so a table style edit saved to an R2004 or R2007 DWG leaves it as read. **Acceptance:** a DXF save of every corpus CELLSTYLEMAP states the same TABLEFORMAT and GRIDFORMAT values the file did; the R2004/R2007 DWG maps are read and kept in step by the part 12 edits on `sample_AC1018.dwg` and `sample_AC1021.dwg`. *Why:* found by M1-96 part 2; AutoCAD's handling of a stale roundtrip map (whether it reads the map or the rows from an older DWG) is undocumented, so the safe save keeps both alike. *Done (#177, acad-ts patches 0155 and 0156):* the DXF TABLEFORMAT reads and writes 170, 91 and 93 and GRIDFORMAT 90 and 91 as AutoCAD states them, with the borders in edge flag order and 340 = 0 for no linetype, so a DXF save of every corpus CELLSTYLEMAP states the groups the file did; acad-ts reads and writes a DWG's CELLSTYLEMAP (R14 to R2007), which reads like the DXF save of the same drawing, and a table style edit keeps it in step on `sample_AC1014`–`AC1021.dwg`.
- [x] **M1-100** Intersection property flake: `packages/engine/test/geom/intersect.test.ts` "a secant through two points of any curve finds both points" failed once in a full `pnpm test` (fast-check seed -1611953239) on a degree-2 SPLINE with a doubled interior knot, `{"kind":"SPLINE","degree":2,"knots":[0,0,0,0.05,0.05,0.05],"controlPoints":[{"x":0,"y":0},{"x":7.479930489092619,"y":0},{"x":0,"y":618.8233084289079}]}` with parameters 0.03642963634131514 and 0.02, and passed on rerun. **Acceptance:** that counterexample as a fixed unit test, passing, and the property green for 20 seeded runs. *Why:* found by M1-75 part 24 (#180), which does not touch the engine; a property that fails on some seeds makes `pnpm test` red at random. *Done (#182):* the miss was in `seedPair` (`geom/intersect.ts`): it shrank the seed band by four on each halving, assuming halving a chord quarters its deviation, but across that parabola's tip the half chord turns with the curve and keeps about half (0.30 against a band of 0.155), so the pair holding the second crossing was skipped. Each half pair's band is now its measured chord deviations (sampled at ¼, ½, ¾ like `flatten`), never less than the old quarter, so no pair the old search tried is skipped. The counterexample is a unit test, and the property passed 400 runs on each of seeds 1–200 and the failing seed (the old code fails only that one of the 201).
- [x] (#199) **M1-103** XCLIP on block references: map each INSERT's `SPATIAL_FILTER` (its `ACAD_FILTER`/`SPATIAL` extension dictionary entry: boundary, normal and origin, clipping on/off, front/back planes, the inverse insert and clip transforms), draw the reference clipped to it (nested clips intersect, a two-point boundary is its rectangle, the polygon AutoCAD stores for an inverted clip drawn as stored) with the frame shown per `XCLIPFRAME`, pick only what shows, and keep the filter through saves. **Acceptance:** every corpus SPATIAL_FILTER maps alike from DWG and DXF saves; `xclip.dxf` and the ACadSharp samples draw only the clipped part of each reference; round trip stays clean. *Why:* 21 public corpus files hold XCLIPped references that nothing maps or draws, so they show whole; M1-21 lists XCLIP but waits on the owner's xref set, while clips on plain block references are in the corpus now. *Done (#199):* `mapSpatialFilters` maps every filter reached through an INSERT's extension dictionary as engine `SpatialFilter` (DXF 71 is the XCLIP ON/OFF flag, which acad-ts calls `displayBoundary`), and engine `clipBoundary` takes its boundary into the block through the inverse insert transform (a two-point boundary made the rectangle first, so a turned reference keeps the rectangle the drafter drew). acad-ts patch 0169: the DXF reader overwrote the inverse insert transform with the second matrix, so every DXF filter (and a DXF save of it) had lost where its clip sits. The renderer (`insertClips`, `RenderPrimitive.clip`) draws each part inside every clip around it with one canvas clip per run of parts, strokes the frame with the reference's own properties unless XCLIPFRAME is 0, leaves ATTRIBs unclipped (AutoCAD's help is silent; the drafter's expectation), and a pick on a clipped-away part misses. `xclip.corpus.test.ts` maps 21 files (each ACadSharp DXF save alike to its DWG) and checks the 28 clipped references draw inside the boundary composed from their DXF placement. Left: the spatial index still bounds a clipped reference by its whole block (culling stays correct; whether AutoCAD's ZOOM Extents counts only the clipped part is undocumented, and no corpus extents tell), the front and back clipping planes (3D only, no corpus file sets them), the boundary's own plane and transform (the defaults in every sample), and the XCLIP command (M5-04), whose new, edited or removed filters the save refuses until then.
- [x] (#200) **M1-104** Layer descriptions: map the Description AutoCAD's Layer Properties Manager shows (the second 1000 string of the layer's `AcAecLayerStandard` xdata) into `LayerRecord.description`, show it in the Layers palette's Description column, and save an edited or new layer's description (registering the application when the drawing has none). **Acceptance:** every ACadSharp sample's `Layer_desc` maps its description alike from DWG and DXF, the Layers palette shows it, and a description set, changed and cleared on every corpus layer saves to DWG and DXF and reads back. *Why:* found while choosing the next M1-75 part: a layer's xdata changes are refused, and the description is the layer xdata a drafter edits. *Done (#200):* `LayerRecord.description` is mapped from the second 1000 string of the layer's `AcAecLayerStandard` entry (the application matched without regard to case; a lone first string, as AecObjects.dwg's layer 0 has, is no description) and shown in the Layers palette's Description column. A save (`export/layers.ts`) rewrites that string, keeping the first and the layer's other applications, keeps the entry with an empty string when the description is cleared (as AutoCAD's layers without one have it), and adds `""`, then the text, to a layer without one, registering the application unless the drawing or the same save has it. Refused: a description longer than xdata's 255 characters, and any in R12, whose AutoCAD saves drop every layer's entry (ACadSharp's sample_AC1009 keeps only the APPID). Corpus: `layer-description.corpus.test.ts` maps Layer_desc alike from every sample's DWG and DXF saves; `export.corpus.test.ts` gives or clears a description on every corpus layer.

- [x] **M1-GO** Owner checks this box to release M2+. (needs-human) Checked 2026-11-08: the owner approved M1 complete (95 done, 15 moved post-launch by DEC-047) and released M2 in autodrive.

---

## M2 — Markup workflow end-to-end (open · red-line · save · print)

**Exit criteria:** an engineer can open a vendor DXF (or a DWG, best effort), add clouds/notes/leaders/dims, edit title-block attributes, save as DXF (round-trip clean, ezdxf audit clean; DEC-047), and print a CTB-correct PDF — entirely offline.

- [x] (#220) **M2-01** Command engine: `PromptBus`, generator contract, parser (coordinates, keywords, direct distance, `FROM`/`M2P`, filters, expressions), alias table from `COMMANDS.md`, repeat/cancel/default/transparent, history, F2 window. **Acceptance:** scripted command tests; parser table tests. *Done (#220, DEC-048):* `packages/engine/src/commands`: `CommandDef`/`CommandContext` as ARCHITECTURE.md §5.1 gives them (`ctx.prompt.*` builders, `ctx.edit` applying each change set at once, `ctx.preview`, `ctx.message`); `CommandRegistry` finds a command by name, by the acad.pgp `ALIASES` table (`tests/docs/commands-aliases.test.ts` holds it to COMMANDS.md both ways), by its own aliases or as `-NAME`; `PromptBus` takes typed text (`submit`), direct answers (`answer`), Esc (`cancel`) and button starts (`invoke`, cancelling a running command unless the new one is transparent) and emits prompts, history lines, previews and one `CommandRecord` per command (its change sets in order, for M2-05). The bus does Enter-repeats, defaults, keyword capitals, transparent `'NAME` with `>>` prompts and "Resuming X command.", FROM, M2P, `.X/.Y/.XY` filters, direct distance towards `pointer()`, two-point distances and angles, ANGBASE/ANGDIR and LUPREC, and AutoCAD's refusal messages; `parse.ts` reads numbers with `+ - * / ( )`, angles (degrees, DMS, `r`, `g`) and coordinates (`x,y`, `d<a`, `@`, `#`, `*`). Scripted command tests: `packages/engine/test/commands/bus.test.ts`; parser tables: `parse.test.ts`; the command engine has a 90 % coverage gate. Moved to M2-02, which first gives the bus a front-end: the F2 history window (it shows `PromptBus.history`) and the M1-22 view leftovers (ZOOM/PAN/REGEN/VIEW commands, ZOOM Center/Dynamic/Object/nXP, transparent Z/P), which act on the canvas view the engine does not hold. Left for their own tasks: osnap overrides typed at a point prompt (M2-04), selection modes typed at Select objects (M2-03), feet-and-inches input under LUNITS 3/4 and the UCS (both M3-era: typed input is world coordinates until then).
- [x] (#222, #224) **M2-02** Command line UI + Dynamic Input (cursor prompt, numeric fields, Tab) + Ctrl+K palette. **Acceptance:** Playwright: `L ↵ 0,0 ↵ @100<45 ↵ ↵` draws the line; DYN fields accept input. *From M2-01:* the F2 history window (`PromptBus.history`) and the M1-22 view leftovers that need a command line: the ZOOM/PAN/REGEN/VIEW commands on the bus (ZOOM Center/Dynamic/Object/nXP, transparent `'Z`/`'P`), with whatever view host the engine worker needs to reach the canvas controller. *Part 1 (#222):* the command line (`apps/web/src/CommandLine.tsx`, `command-line.ts`): history lines, the prompt with its options as chips, completion, Up/Down recall, the F2 history window, keys typed outside a field going to it; each drawing's `PromptBus` runs in its `EngineSession` (engine worker) behind `CommandInput`/`CommandOutput` messages, a command's ChangeSets taking the edit path to the canvases and the change log; the canvas picks points and draws the rubber band (`CanvasController.setPrompt`, `onPick`); commands draw in the space shown (`ctx.space`); LINE (`packages/engine/src/commands/draw/line.ts`) with Undo, Close and Enter-continue; the Playwright acceptance script passes (`tests/e2e/ui/command-line.spec.ts`). *Left for part 2:* Dynamic Input (cursor prompt, numeric fields with Tab and lock, DYNMODE), the Ctrl+K palette, and the ZOOM/PAN/REGEN/VIEW commands with the view host; also `ctx.preview` geometry on the transient layer (no command previews yet; LINE needs only the rubber band), and a click made before the prompt reaches the canvas is dropped (the prompt arrives from the worker within a frame). *Done (part 2, #224):* the view commands on the bus (`packages/engine/src/commands/view`): ZOOM (window corner, n/nX/nXP, All, Center, Dynamic as a window as this table allows, Extents, Previous, Scale, Window, `<real time>` dragging up to zoom in), PAN (real time) and -PAN, REGEN, REGENALL, VIEW/-VIEW (?, Restore, Save, Window; session views as the navigation bar's), ZOOM and PAN transparent (`'Z`, `'P`); they reach the canvas through a view host (`ctx.view`: the session sends a `view` request out and awaits the app's reply, `apps/web/src/canvas/view-host.ts`), and point and distance prompts take arbitrary text (`2x`) and show a `defaultLabel` (`<real time>`). `ctx.preview` geometry is tessellated onto the transient layer under the rubber band, which draws a rectangle for a window's corners. Dynamic Input (`DynamicInput.tsx`, `dynamic-input.ts`): the prompt and its fields at the cursor while a command waits over a drawing, X/Y pointer input (first point absolute, then relative, `#` absolute), Distance/Angle dimension input (`<` or Tab to the angle, a distance alone is direct distance), Tab locks a field, anything else typed is sent as typed, ↓ opens the options; F12 and the status bar's DYN toggle DYNMODE. The Ctrl+K palette (`CommandPalette.tsx`, `palette.ts`): fuzzy search over the commands with their aliases, the command history (F2), DYN (F12) and Settings; Enter runs (`invoke`). Tests: `packages/engine/test/commands/view.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/{view-host,dynamic-input,palette}.test.ts`, Playwright `tests/e2e/ui/command-line-dyn.spec.ts`. Moved on: ZOOM Object and a click made before a prompt reaches the canvas (M2-03, which gives "Select objects" and clicks at `Command:` their front-end), DYNMODE's settings and pointer/dimension input toggles (M3-06's DRAFTINGSETTINGS), recent files in the palette (M2-14), keeping DYNMODE between sessions (M2-20).
- [x] (#225) **M2-03** Selection core: pick, window/crossing, implied windowing, Shift-remove, Ctrl+A, Previous/Last/All, selection highlight, `PICKFIRST` pre-selection. **Acceptance:** rule tests mirror AutoCAD semantics. *From M2-02 (#224):* ZOOM Object (the option, prompting "Select objects", and the bounds of the chosen objects, text included, which needs the session's spatial index this task brings), and a click made at `Command:` or before a command's first prompt reaches the canvas (dropped now; AutoCAD starts a selection window there). *Done (#225):* the `PromptBus` builds every "Select objects" prompt (`SelectionLoop`, `packages/engine/src/selection`): picks within PICKBOX 3, implied windowing clicked or dragged (left to right a window, right to left a crossing window), Shift and Remove/Add, W, C, BOX, ALL (not frozen or locked layers; off ones in), Last (newest visible in the space), Previous (minus erased objects), Undo, AUto, SIngle, AutoCAD's "n found (d duplicate), m total" lines, and locked-layer refusal for prompts that edit. The same loop at `Command:` is the PICKFIRST pre-selection (taken by a command's first selection prompt, kept by view commands, cleared by others, Esc and a space change); Ctrl+A selects all; SELECT and ZOOM Object ship.
