- [x] (#226, #228) **M2-04** Precision input v1: running osnaps (END/MID/CEN/INT/PER/TAN/NEA/NOD/QUA/INS/APP/EXT/PAR/GEOCEN), AutoSnap markers, Shift+right-click overrides, ORTHO, POLAR, OTRACK, GRID/SNAP, status-bar toggles (LWT and TPY too: `RenderStyle.showLineweights` and `showTransparency` are ready, M1-108), F-keys. **Acceptance:** osnap response < 50 ms on the generated `bench-100k` drawing; per-mode tests. *Part 1 (#226):* running object snaps in `packages/engine/src/input/osnap.ts` (`osnap`, OSMODE bits, AutoCAD's initial 4133): END, MID, CEN, GCE, NOD, QUA, INT, APP (extended intersection in plan), INS, PER and TAN (from the rubber band's base point; deferred PER/TAN not yet) and NEA, on lines, arcs, circles, ellipses, polylines (per segment), splines, SOLID/TRACE/3DFACE edges, leaders, viewport frames, points, text and block references (their contents too, MINSERT cells and nested blocks, transformed) and dimensions (their definition points and *D block); hatches are skipped (OSNAPHATCH 0). An object counts when the aperture touches it; its points may lie anywhere on it; ranking is a point inside the aperture, then NEA, then the rest by distance, ties in Drafting Settings order (END before INT). The render worker answers (`RendererHost.snap`, where text has glyph bounds); on `bench-100k` 1000 queries with every mode on take p95 7 ms (T3 perf spec, budget 50 ms), the nearest 24 objects offering points and the nearest 12 paired for INT/APP. ORTHO, POLAR (POLARANG 90°, additional angles in the engine) and SNAP (the viewport's SNAPUNIT/SNAPBASE) in `input/constrain.ts`; the canvas (`CanvasController`, `canvas/autosnap.ts`) shows AutoSnap markers (per-mode shapes, 10 px aperture) with the mode's name, polar tracking's dashed path with `Polar: d < a°`, and a click at a point prompt sends the marked or constrained point (input typed after a click waits for it, `Engines.command`); typed points are never changed. Status bar: SNAP (F9), ORTHO (F8), POLAR (F10), OSNAP (F3) with a menu of the 14 modes; ORTHO and POLAR turn each other off; the Ctrl+K palette has them too. Tests: `packages/engine/test/input/{osnap,constrain}.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/drafting.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/ui/osnap.spec.ts`. *Part 2 (#228):* object snap tracking (`input/track.ts`: `trackCursor`, `orthoTrack`, `trackLabel`; OTRACK F11, on by default): pausing 400 ms on a snap point acquires it (a small cross; pausing again lets it go; at most 7; a command's end clears them) and the cursor runs on the horizontal and vertical paths from it (POLARMODE bit 2: the polar angles), onto the crossing of two paths, of a path with polar tracking from the base point, or with ORTHO's axis, with AutoCAD's `Endpoint: 30.0000 < 270°` / `Endpoint: < 90°, Polar: < 0°` tooltip; EXTension (a line's end, on along it) and PARallel (a line, from the base point both ways) are acquired the same way from `track` hits `osnap` now lists after the snap points. Typed one-shot overrides at any point prompt (`parseOsnapOverride`: `END`, `ENDP`, `END,MID`, `NONe`…, three letters or more) ask `of` with the rubber band kept, the canvas snaps that point with the override's modes, and a click it finds nothing at is refused with "Invalid point." while `of` stays (AutoCAD's help does not say what follows a miss; drafter expectation); Shift+right-click opens the same overrides as a menu (From, Mid Between 2 Points, the 14 modes, None). In a layout, model space is snapped to through each viewport the cursor is inside, in paper coordinates and within the frame, skipping layers frozen in it. Tab steps AutoSnap through the snap points under the aperture. GRID (F7) draws GRIDUNIT lines with every GRIDMAJOR-th stronger, adaptive (coarsened by GRIDMAJOR below 8 px), under the drawing (`RenderStyle.grid`, `render/grid.ts`); it starts off so drawings open as drawn. LWT shows the drawing's LWDISPLAY until turned, TPY is TRANSPARENCYDISPLAY; neither is written back to the file. Tests: `packages/engine/test/input/{track,osnap}.test.ts`, `packages/engine/test/commands/{bus,parse}.test.ts`, `packages/render/test/grid.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/drafting.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/ui/tracking.spec.ts`. *Left for later tasks:* deferred PER/TAN (no last point yet) and EXT along arcs (M3-06 with the Drafting Settings dialog); `TT` temporary track points and POLARMODE/APERTURE settings (M3-06); keeping the toggles between sessions (M2-20); non-rectangular viewport clips are snapped as their frames.

A `string` prompt may carry `editor` (`TextEditorRequest`): the app opens TEXT's one-line editor or MTEXT's editor on the canvas (`apps/web/src/TextEditor.tsx`, spans edited by `text-editor.ts`) with the Text Formatting toolbar: Bold, Italic (off for SHX fonts), Underline, Overline, Strikethrough, Height, Color, Stack (`/`, `#`, `^`), OK; Ctrl+Enter or a click outside keeps the text, Esc drops it. `@triya/text` `writeMText` writes the editor's spans as format codes and `spansOf` reads parsed contents back (1000-run property test); `export-text-commands.corpus.test.ts` places TEXT and MTEXT with every format on each readable corpus file, saves AC1032 DXF and reads the contents back exactly, plus 200 random editor contents. *Part 2 (#232):* TEXTEDIT (DDEDIT, ED; `textEditCommand` in `draw/text.ts`) with AutoCAD's "Select an annotation object or [Undo/Mode]", Mode Single/Multiple (Multiple by default, remembered), Undo of the command's last edit; a TEXT opens its in-place line holding its value, an MTEXT its editor holding its contents (`TextEditorRequest.value`), and a text left empty is erased. A double click on a TEXT or MTEXT runs it on that object (`PromptBus.doubleClick`, `DOUBLE_CLICK` as DBLCLKEDIT); a text now picks anywhere in its runs' boxes. The editor keeps what it has no control for: `contentOf` reads each paragraph's `\p` values as written (`MTextParagraph.codes`, carried values included, written back as what each paragraph adds, braced where a later one drops them), `\W`, `\T`, `\Q`, `\A` as run formats, font charset and pitch, `\N` and `\X` breaks; a close with no change answers the contents as they were. The editor's Columns menu (No Columns, Dynamic Columns, Static Columns 2–6, Insert Column Break, Column Settings…), the Column Settings dialog, Alt+Enter column breaks, the box shown as CSS columns; a dynamic MTEXT's count is laid out with the renderers' fonts (`dynamicColumnCount` in `@triya/text`), or counted from its `\N` breaks on the command line. The ruler above the box: first-line and paragraph indents and tab stops of the paragraphs the selection touches, written as one `\p` value (`withRuler`, replacing its own last value), the paragraphs indented by them in the editor. A `\M+` double-byte character is kept as an `mbcs` span and written back as read. `export-mtext-columns.corpus.test.ts` places static and dynamic columns with ruler values, a column break and a double-byte character on every corpus file, saves AC1032 DXF and reads columns, height and contents back exactly. Leftovers: M2-26. Fields from the editor (Insert Field, FIELD objects in ACAD_FIELD) moved to M4-10, which owns the FIELD dialog and evaluation.
- [x] (#231) **M2-08** REVCLOUD (rect/poly/freehand, arc length, style), WIPEOUT, DRAWORDER/TEXTTOFRONT/HATCHTOBACK. **Acceptance:** entity output matches AutoCAD structure (polyline with bulges / WIPEOUT). *Done (#231):* `packages/engine/src/commands/draw/{revcloud,wipeout}.ts`, `commands/modify/draw-order.ts`, geometry in `edit/revcloud.ts` and `edit/draw-order.ts`. A cloud is a closed (or, freehand ended early, open) LWPOLYLINE whose every segment has AutoCAD's bulge 0.520567 (≈110°), bulging out unless reversed, with `RevcloudProps` xdata (1070 0, 1040 arc length) under an APPID the first cloud adds: what ezdxf's `revcloud.is_revcloud` asks. Arcs vary between the minimum and maximum arc length (≤ 3 × minimum) by a fixed pseudo-random sequence; Calligraphy widens each arc from 0 to a tenth of the arc length; the first cloud's arc length is 1/40 of the view diagonal (two figures), 0.5 × DIMSCALE without a view. Rectangular, Polygonal, Freehand (a `trace` point prompt the canvas answers as the cursor moves, closing back at the start) and Object (circle, ellipse, polyline, spline, replaced in place with its properties; `single` selection prompts). WIPEOUT by points or from a closed polyline (arcs by chords; Erase polyline), framed as AutoCAD 2018 frames one (square of the larger side, unit pixel space, flags 7); Frames ON/OFF through WIPEOUTVARIABLES (a new one under the root dictionary, `DocumentMeta.rootDictionary`). DRAWORDER Above/Under/Front/Back, TEXTTOFRONT Text/Dimensions/Leaders/All and HATCHTOBACK write the block's SORTENTSTABLE by handing out the sort handles in use in the new order (unmapped objects' entries untouched; a block's first table in its record's extension dictionary). Gates: `packages/engine/test/commands/{revcloud,draw-order}.test.ts`, corpus gate `packages/testkit/src/export/export-markup-commands.corpus.test.ts` (every readable file from R14 saves as AC1032 DXF and reopens with the clouds, wipeout, WIPEOUTFRAME and every layout's draw order; ezdxf audit `markup` saves), Playwright `tests/e2e/ui/markup.spec.ts`. - [x] (#233) **M2-09** MLEADER with styles (read existing styles; create with current style), add/remove leader, text/block content. **Acceptance:** MLEADER written is opened by acad-ts and renders identically. *Done (#233):* MLEADER (MLD) draws a multileader with the current style (CMLEADERSTYLE, else Standard, else the first; AutoCAD's Standard, imperial or metric by MEASUREMENT, is added when the drawing has none): arrowhead first, leader landing first or content first, and Options (Leader type Straight/sPline/None, leader lAnding, Content type Block/Mtext/None, Maxpoints, First and Second angle), which it remembers per drawing. Text is typed in the in-place MTEXT editor (or on the command line, lines joined); block content asks for the style's block or a named one and its attribute values. Geometry follows AutoCAD's: the leader ends at the landing point, the landing runs the dogleg length, and text attaches a landing gap beyond it by the style's left/right (or top/bottom) attachment; block content by its extents. MLEADEREDIT (MLE, AIMLEADEREDITADD, AIMLEADEREDITREMOVE) adds leaders, a new branch with its own landing on the content's other side (the text's width from the render worker's new `textBox` view request), and removes them, keeping at least one. `export-mleader-commands.corpus.test.ts` runs a session on every readable corpus file, saves it as AC1032 DXF, reads it back with acad-ts and compares every multileader's drawn parts; ezdxf audits the saves (`mleader`). Leftovers in M2-25.

The renderer draws a dimension without a stored block from the engine. *Part 2 (#237):* ordinate dimensions (jogged leader, text along it), jogged radius (LARGE_RADIAL_DIMENSION: displaced centre, DIMJOGANG jog) and arc length (ARC_DIMENSION: parallel or radial extension lines, DIMARCSYM symbol) dimensions; deviations and limits (DIMTOL, DIMLIM with DIMTP, DIMTM, DIMTFAC, DIMTDEC, DIMTZIN), alternate units (DIMALT with DIMALTF, DIMALTU, DIMALTD, DIMALTZ, DIMALTRND, DIMAPOST, DIMALTTD, DIMALTTZ; `[]` in an override); DIMJUST 3/4. The engine worker has no fonts, so the texts are measured by the canvas (view request `textWidths`); the dimension engine reaches the engine as `EngineSession`'s `services`. - [x] (#238) **M2-13** Save/Save As as AC1032 DXF (in place via File System Access for a `.dxf`; a drawing opened from a DWG or an older DXF goes to Save As `.dxf`, never overwriting the original; download fallback), dirty state, close prompts. **Acceptance:** DXF round-trip diff = 0 after a markup session on every corpus file (edited entities excluded from diff by handle list), and every saved file passes the ezdxf audit (M1-105). (blocked-by M1-105; DEC-047 replaced the M0-07 AUDIT dependency.) *From M2-08 (#231):* the save checks a drawing's objects against the version it was read from (`triyaToAcad`'s `Resolver.version`), so Save As AC1032 of a drawing older than R14 refuses a WIPEOUT, a draw order table and, before R11, a revision cloud's xdata; check them against AC1032 instead (`export-markup-commands.corpus.test.ts` holds the 8 pre-R14 corpus files to that refusal until then). *From M2-09 (#233):* the same check refuses a multileader style (so the multileaders using it) on a drawing older than R2007 that had none; `export-mleader-commands.corpus.test.ts` holds those 11 corpus files to that refusal. *Done (#238):* the app keeps each drawing's bytes and its engine change log; Save hands both to the I/O worker, which reads the bytes again, replays the log onto that read (`applyToReplica`, the render replica's own replay) and writes AC1032 DXF from the retained original (`saveSession`, `saveDxf`), so saving twice writes what saving once does. `triyaToAcad` takes the version it writes (`TriyaToAcadOptions.version`) and checks every change against AC1032, which flips both refusals above: every readable corpus file now saves its markup and multileader sessions. Save writes in place over a `.dxf` read as AC1032 opened through File System Access (asking write permission once); anything else (a DWG, an older DXF, a dropped or picked file without a handle) goes to Save As, whose picker offers only "AutoCAD 2018 DXF (*.dxf)" named after the drawing, and later saves write there. Without File System Access Save downloads the `.dxf`. Ctrl+S, Ctrl+Shift+S, the title bar's Save and Save As buttons, and QSAVE / SAVE / SAVEAS / CLOSE on the command line (through the view host, `op: 'file'`). A tab shows a dot while it has unsaved changes (xref loads do not count); closing it, CLOSE, or leaving the page asks first ("Save changes to <name>?", Yes / No / Cancel, Yes focused, Esc cancels). Two fixes the gate found: MLEADER in a drawing with no text style (R1, R2.5) says so instead of making a Standard style the save refuses, and a save refuses a new dictionary entry (a multileader style) whose name matches an entry it keeps as read, rather than replacing an R2007 STANDARD style kept as a proxy (the R13/R14 binary DXFs: the rest of their session saves). Acceptance: `save-session.corpus.test.ts` runs LINE, REVCLOUD, WIPEOUT, TEXT, MLEADER, a MOVE and an ERASE on every readable corpus file and diffs the save against the unedited save with the touched handles (and what they own) left out: zero differences on all 110, each save audited by ezdxf as the file's `session` save. Recent drawings (`recent.ts`): files opened or saved through a file handle, kept in IndexedDB `triya-recent`, newest first, nine at most, listed on the start screen and in the Ctrl+K palette ("Open recent: …"); reopening asks for read access again. Playwright: `tests/e2e/ui/recovery.spec.ts` kills the tab's renderer and recovers. *Part 2 (#242):* cross-document copy and paste through one Clipboard the app keeps for every open drawing (`edit/clipboard.ts`, `commands/modify/clipboard.ts`): COPYCLIP (Ctrl+C), CUTCLIP (Ctrl+X, one undo step), COPYBASE (Ctrl+Shift+C), PASTECLIP (Ctrl+V), PASTEBLOCK (Ctrl+Shift+V) and PASTEORIG. `copyToClipboard` takes the objects in drawing order with every layer, linetype, text style, dimension style, multileader and table style, application id and block they reach (by reference, xdata 1003/1005, DIMVARs), as plain data; `pasteObjects` matches each record by name in the target (AutoCAD's "duplicate definitions ignored"), adds the rest, gives anonymous blocks fresh names, allocates every new object and record a handle above the target's `$HANDSEED`, remaps handles in references, xdata and DIMVARs, keeps hatch associativity to pasted boundaries, and moves the objects from the base point (the extents' lower-left corner, from the render worker's new `bounds` view request) to the insertion point. PASTECLIP and PASTEBLOCK drag the curves and the extents' frame with the cursor (`drag: preview`); PASTEBLOCK makes an `A$C<hex>` block on the current layer; PASTEORIG refuses objects from the same drawing. The engine's replica now counts an INSERT's attribute handles toward its seed, and the save plans and writes new blocks before dimension styles (a DIMBLK naming a pasted block took a later object's handle). A hatch's arc and ellipse edges are bounded by their sweep, not their whole circle, so the base point is the true corner. DRAWINGRECOVERY shows the recovery banner again. Acceptance: `paste.corpus.test.ts` pastes the model space of each of 111 readable corpus files into the self-authored AC1032 `traces.dxf`, saves it as the app does and reads it back with every pasted object and record under its new handle, type, owner and layer (ezdxf audits the saves, `paste`); `tests/e2e/ui/clipboard.spec.ts` copies with Ctrl+C in one tab and pastes with Ctrl+V in another. The start screen's thumbnails and the paste leftovers moved to M2-32.

- [x] (#246, #256) **M2-20** Settings: theme, canvas colours, units display, osnap settings, "Clear all local data", user font/pattern/CTB/linetype/template stores UI. **Acceptance:** clear-data test leaves OPFS/IDB empty. *From M2-02 (#224):* keep DYNMODE (`dynMode`, on by default, F12) between sessions. *From M2-14 (#240):* "Clear all local data" also deletes the IndexedDB databases `triya-recovery` (autosave snapshots) and `triya-recent` (recent drawings). *From M2-19 (#244):* "Clear all local data" also deletes the Cache Storage caches `triya-offline-*` and unregisters the service worker (`navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistrations()`), so the next visit is a first visit. *Part 1 (#246):* `apps/web/src/preferences.ts` keeps the user's settings in IndexedDB `triya-settings` (one record, anything invalid back to its default): the chrome theme (system, light, dark; `data-theme` on the root, dark chrome variables in `styles.css`), the canvas colours (model background, layout paper and surround, grid lines automatic or a colour as `GridSpec.color`, selection highlight; ACI 7 and the AutoSnap colours follow the background's luminance, `canvas/colours.ts`), the status bar's coordinates in the drawing's units (LUNITS/LUPREC through dims' `formatLinear`, `coordinates.ts`) or decimal, and what AutoCAD keeps per user: OSMODE, POLAR on and POLARANG, OTRACK, AUTOSNAPSIZE and APERTURE (new `DraftingState.markerSize`/`aperture`) and DYNMODE; ORTHO, SNAP, GRID, LWT and TPY stay per session (drawing variables in AutoCAD). Settings shows them in sections Display, Drafting, Hatch patterns, Fonts and Local data. "Clear all local data" (`local-data.ts`) asks once in place, stops autosave, unregisters the service worker, deletes every cache, every IndexedDB database (listed, or the known `triya-*` names) and every OPFS entry, then reloads; every app database closes on `versionchange` so the deletion is not blocked, a database another tab holds is reported after 5 s, and the worker never re-creates a deleted cache while it still answers its page. Left for part 2: the CTB/STB, linetype (.lin) and template stores (files kept in OPFS, listed, added, removed, validated by `parsePlotStyleTable` and a .lin reader) once M2-17/M3 use them; crosshair and transient (rubber band, preview) colours; the in-canvas editors (MTEXT ruler, AutoSnap tooltip) in the dark theme. *Part 2 (#256):* `apps/web/src/support-files.ts`: the user's plot style tables (.ctb/.stb) and linetype files (.lin), dropped on the window or added in Settings (sections Plot style tables and Linetypes), checked as they are added (`parsePlotStyleTable`, with the extension matching the table's kind as AutoCAD requires; `parseLin` on the file read as ANSI), listed with what each holds (a CTB's style count and description, an STB's style names, a .lin's linetype names), removed, and kept in OPFS (`plotstyles/`, `linetypes/`; a kept file that no longer reads is listed apart); `SupportFileStore.file(name)` finds one by file name, ignoring case, for M2-17 part 3's Plot dialog and M5-08's loader. Display gains Crosshairs, AutoSnap marker and AutoTrack vector colours, each Automatic (following the background, as before) or chosen (`interfaceColours` in `canvas/colours.ts`): the crosshairs are the canvas's cursor drawn as an SVG in their colour (no pickbox at `Command:`: swapping cursor images per prompt delayed prompts in CI), and the rubber band, the ZOOM window and a command's preview geometry draw in the crosshairs' colour (AutoCAD keeps no separate rubber band colour; our choice). The AutoSnap tooltip and the MTEXT ruler take the dark chrome's colours. Dynamic Input's keys now read the fields as last set (a ref), not the rendered state, so a key typed as a new prompt arrives is not lost to the previous prompt's fields. Moved on: the template store to M5-10 (its user templates folder: a template is only of use with the New dialog), listing a user's tables in the Plot dialog to M2-17 part 3 (already there).

Objects on locked layers are left alone and said so; a value out of range changes nothing ("Invalid input."). `EngineSession` sends a `properties` sheet while the palette is open (input `properties`/`property`). App: `PropertiesPalette.tsx` docked from the rail, Ctrl+1, PROPERTIES (PR, CH, MO) and PROPERTIESCLOSE through the view host (`palette` request); values formatted by LUNITS/LUPREC and AUNITS/AUPREC (`properties.ts`). - [x] (#249) **M2-22** PDF underlay osnaps: extract each underlay page's vector paths through pdf.js (`getOperatorList` path operators, transformed to page inches) into snap geometry for M2-04's running osnaps (END, MID, INT, NEA, CEN on arcs approximated by pdf.js curves) under `PDFOSNAP`, done in the pdf.js worker per page and cached with the page. **Acceptance:** on the M1-20 fixture, END and INT snap to page 2's grid corners and triangle vertices within 1e-6 in through its scale, rotation and clip, and snap nothing outside the clip. *Why:* split from M1-20, whose osnap part needs M2-04's osnap engine. *Done (#249):* pdf.js parses each page in its worker (`getOperatorList`); render's `underlaySnapFromOperatorList` walks the list (save/restore, `cm`, form XObjects; clip-only paths, text, images and annotations skipped) into engine's `UnderlaySnapPage` in page inches: lines, Bézier curves that are circular arcs (within 1e-3 of their radius, under a half turn) as ARCs, other Béziers as cubic SPLINEs, at most 1 M per page. The app reads a page once per file (`PdfPages`, cached with the file, read again when the file is replaced) and sends it to the render worker (`setUnderlaySnap`) the first time a canvas asks for that definition's page; osnap places it by the underlay's insertion, scale, rotation and mirroring, inside block references and through layout viewports too, and while clipping is on counts only points inside the boundary (on it too) with the aperture reaching what shows. PDFOSNAP (Settings > Drafting, "Snap to PDF underlays") is kept per user, on by default, as AutoCAD keeps it. `testkit/src/underlay/snap.test.ts` runs pdf.js on the M1-20 fixture: INT at all 64 grid corners inside the pentagon and END at the triangle's vertices within 1e-6, nothing at nine points outside; `pdf-underlay.spec.ts` snaps a LINE's first point to a grid corner in the app and nothing above the clip. Ambiguity noted: AutoCAD's help does not say whether a clipped PDF's geometry ends at the boundary; here a curve keeps its own ends and only its points inside count. The ribbon's Snap to Underlays toggle is left to M5-04's Insert > Reference work.
- [x] (#252) **M2-23** Undo in the app: register `UNDO_COMMANDS` in `builtinCommands()`, bind Ctrl+Z to U and Ctrl+Y to REDO, give the Quick Access undo/redo buttons their state and tooltips from the bus's `undo` event (UI_SPEC's undo toast with count), and record `EngineSession`'s own edits (M1-23 layer states, M1-97 layout edits) as undo steps through `UndoStack.push`, as AutoCAD undoes them. **Acceptance:** Playwright: draw two lines, Ctrl+Z removes the second, Ctrl+Y restores it; a layer freeze is undone by U. *Why:* M2-05 (#223) built the history in the engine; the bus reaches the engine worker and the command line only with M2-02 (#222). *Done (#252):* `builtinCommands()` registers UNDO, U, REDO and OOPS, so every drawing's command engine runs them. `EngineSession` makes a Layers palette toggle (LAYER) and a layout's rename, move or page setup (LAYOUT) one undo step each through `UndoStack.push` (a refused or no-op edit is none, nor is loading an xref, which AutoCAD does not undo either); U undoes them through the bus, so the replicas, the change log and autosave hear of it as a `change`. The bus's `undo` event now carries `action` (`UndoAction`: undo or redo and the steps' names). App: `apps/web/src/undo.ts` (`undoKeyCommand`, `undoButtons`, `undoToast`, per-drawing `UndoStates`), Ctrl+Z → U and Ctrl+Y (Cmd+Shift+Z on a Mac) → REDO beside the Clipboard keys (typed text keeps the browser's own undo, except on an empty command line; a running command is cancelled first, as AutoCAD's Ctrl+Z does), title-bar Undo and Redo buttons greyed out with nothing to do and titled with the step ("Undo LINE (Ctrl+Z)"), and `UndoToast.tsx` ("Undone: LINE · 3 more to undo", 2.5 s, polite live region). Each palette toggle is its own step, as AutoCAD's Layer control drop-down makes them (ours: UNDO Control Layer's combining is for the Layer Properties Manager, which does not exist yet). Associative dimensions (DIMASSOC 2) stay on the post-launch list. **Acceptance:** command tests for each; the corpus dimension commands gate still green. *Why:* M2-11 shipped the commands' main paths; these are the options and inputs it left. *Part 1 (#276):* DIMLINEAR on a selected circle (its diameter across the dimension line, horizontal or vertical by the location, by the quadrant picked inside the circle's box) and DIMALIGNED on one (pick to opposite end); DIMANGULAR's Quadrant (`DimPlacement.quadrant`: a location outside the quadrant keeps the arc in it at the location's radius and the text at the location, DXF 70 bit 128; `@triya/dims` carries the arc on past its nearer end to user-placed text outside the angle); DIMCONTINUE and DIMBASELINE from an ordinate ("Specify feature location", same origin and axis, leader ends in line) or a two-line angular dimension (`angularAsThreePoint`), in the base dimension's style and DSTYLE overrides (DIMCONTINUEMODE 1); the header's DIMVARs that differ from the current style as each new dimension's DSTYLE overrides when `$DIMSTYLE` names that style (`headerDimensionXdata`: DIMFIT/DIMUNIT skipped as obsolete, DIMDSEP compared as a character, text style/arrow block/linetype DIMVARs by name), shown in the drag too; `@triya/dims` reads a DIMDSEP override's character code. The annotative dimension styles this task listed are creation, which DEC-013 excludes: moved to M2-43 with the multileaders (needs-human #263). *Part 2 (#278):* DIMRADIUS and DIMDIAMETER on an arc keep the arc's start and end angles in the dimension's `ACAD_DSTYLE_DIMRADIAL_EXTENSION` xdata (AutoCAD's layout: 387 1, 388, 390; read as the arc's extent, an assumption AutoCAD's documentation leaves open, since every corpus file writes 0 and 0), registering the APPID when needed, and `@triya/dims` draws the arc extension line along the arc's circle from its nearer end when the chord point is past it (a DIMEXO gap, DIMEXE past the dimension line, ext line 1's properties, none with DIMSE1; a diameter's far end too where its arrowhead is); ROTATE, MIRROR and COPY carry the angles. The Mtext option's editor, and TEXTEDIT's, show the measurement itself, highlighted, in place of each `<>`, and answer `<>` for it.
- [x] (#274, #277, #280, #284, #286) **M2-32** Clipboard and start-screen leftovers (found by M2-14, #242): Recent drawings' thumbnails from the last autosave on the start screen (UI_SPEC §10); paste of raster images and PDF underlays (their definitions and files), multileaders whose blocks hold attribute definitions, and xref references; INSUNITS scaling when the two drawings' units differ (AutoCAD scales a paste); records and blocks keep their xdata, layers their viewport overrides and dimension styles their true colours when pasted; a pasted dynamic block reference stays dynamic (it becomes static now); annotative objects stay annotative; the Windows clipboard (AutoCAD's data reaches other apps). **Acceptance:** each pasted kind round-trips through `paste.corpus.test.ts`; a Playwright test for the thumbnails. *Part 1 (#274):* PASTECLIP, PASTEBLOCK and PASTEORIG scale objects pasted into a drawing in other units about the base point (`pasteScale`: the ratio of the two INSUNITS lengths, `metresPerUnit`; nothing scaled where either drawing is unitless, as INSUNITSDEFSOURCE/INSUNITSDEFTARGET are obsolete), the drag preview with them; PASTEBLOCK's block keeps the source's units and its reference takes the scale; `paste.corpus.test.ts` pastes each corpus drawing with units into a target in other units. The start screen shows each recent drawing's picture (`RecentDrawings.tsx`): a PNG of its canvas kept in `triya-recent`, taken when it is autosaved, saved, or closed with nothing unsaved. *Part 2 (#277):* images and PDF underlays come with their definitions (`Clipboard.objects`): each IMAGEDEF or PDFDEFINITION is filed in the target's ACAD_IMAGE_DICT or ACAD_PDFDEFINITIONS (`DocumentMeta.imageDictionary`/`pdfDictionary`, or a new one the save makes), one the target has under the same name is the target's ("duplicate definitions are ignored"); the save writes new and edited PDFDEFINITIONs (`image-objects.ts`); a canvas serves pages of definitions added after it opened. `paste.corpus.test.ts` reads back every pasted definition. *Part 3 (#280):* records keep their xdata (`clipRecord`), naming the pasted applications and, in 1005s, the pasted copies; the annotative property's application and a dynamic block's (GUID, true name, representation tags) stay behind with what they describe; the save writes the xdata of every record it adds (`records.ts`, applications planned first), a new TrueType style keeping its `ACAD` face name and charset bits (`text-styles.ts`). Multileaders whose content block holds attribute definitions paste naming the pasted ATTDEFs, or the target's ATTDEF of the same tag where the target has the block (a value whose tag it lacks goes). `paste.corpus.test.ts` reads back every pasted record's xdata and every such multileader's ATTDEFs. Settled without code: a layer's viewport overrides name viewports of the source's layouts, which a paste never brings (viewports are refused, and AutoCAD's deep clone drops a reference to an object not cloned); a BLOCK's own xdata is not read (its BLOCK_RECORD's is). Moved: xref paste to M5-04 (a new xref BLOCK_RECORD is XATTACH's to write), annotative objects staying so to M2-43 (DEC-013, #263), dimension style true colours to M2-53 (only a DWG stores them, DEC-047). *Part 4 (#284):* a pasted dynamic block reference stays dynamic. The reader holds what makes a block dynamic as `CodedObject`s (`map/coded.ts`): the DXF group codes an AC1032 save writes for a definition's extension dictionary entries (`ACAD_ENHANCEDBLOCK` graph and everything below it, purge preventer, `AcDbBlockTableRecordData`), a `*U` state's and a reference's `AcDbBlockRepresentation`, a tree kept only when every object it owns was written. COPYCLIP carries them with the records and references (and a definition's GUID/true name xdata, a state's AcDbBlockRepBTag), PASTECLIP renumbers them (a reference's tree only where all it names came: a definition the target has by name is the target's), and the save writes them into new extension dictionaries as unknown objects with their CLASSES entry (`export/coded-objects.ts`); a block anonymous by its DXF 70 bit alone (an R2000 DWG's dynamic block) pastes under a new `*U` name. `paste.corpus.test.ts` reads back every pasted reference as dynamic under the same name and every coded object with its codes. *Part 5 (#286):* the system clipboard. COPYCLIP, CUTCLIP and COPYBASE also write the objects to the browser's clipboard under Triya's own web custom format (`web application/x-triya-clipboard`: `clipboardData`, JSON that keeps what the structured clone keeps: undefined fields, −0, non-finite numbers, bigints, byte arrays, Maps, Sets; versioned, so another version's objects are refused with a reason) beside a PNG picture of them (their plot, `PlotSource.only`/`PlotRequest.handles`, fitted to their extents, 1024 px on its longer side, on white), which other apps paste. A paste reads the system clipboard first (`AppClipboard`, `apps/web/src/system-clipboard.ts`), so what was copied last pastes: another Triya window's objects (another drawing's, so PASTEORIG takes them), this window's own (with the drawing they came from), another app's text, which PASTECLIP pastes as multiline text with the MTEXT defaults (AutoCAD 2024 Help, "PASTECLIP"; `mtextLiteral`), or another app's picture, refused with a reason (M2-54); a copy the system clipboard did not take, a read the user did not allow, and a browser without web custom formats (Firefox, Safari) keep the window's own Clipboard. `paste.corpus.test.ts` passes every corpus copy through the system clipboard's text, which reads back equal to the structured clone; Playwright `system-clipboard.spec.ts` pastes between two windows and pastes another app's text.

Passed 8 of 8 locally and on the job's other run. Find the race in the app (not the test) and fix it. **Acceptance:** the spec passes 20 runs in a row locally (`--repeat-each 20`), and a command test that types an angle while the cursor moves gets the typed value. *Done (#281):* the CI run's lines show the angle reached ROTATE as the point `90,1000050` (179.9989°, which reads as −0.001° on a line), the 90 typed into the X field Dynamic Input still showed for the base point: its key handler read the rendered fields, which a key typed as the new prompt showed could beat. #256 had already made the keys read the fields last set and replay them for a newer prompt; this keeps that rule in `dynCurrent` (dynamic-input.ts) with unit tests, adds a Playwright test that types 9, 0 and Enter in the same task that shows the angle prompt with the cursor moving (fails 3 of 3 on the pre-#256 handler) and a bus test that a typed angle beats the cursor. `edit-commands.spec.ts` passed 68 local repeats on main (20 with 4 workers, 48 with 12) and 20 more with this change, the new test 50 (M2-52 is another flake found in those runs).
- [x] (#283, #285, #288, #292, #295, #296, #305, #307, #309, #310, #315, #317, #319, #321, #326, #328, #331, #334, #336, #339, #345, #351, #356, #359, #361, #360, #362, #363, #365, #364, #366) **M2-35** Properties palette leftovers (found by M2-21, #247): Select Color… (the Select Color dialog, true colour and colour books) and Other… for linetypes; Plot style, Thickness, Hyperlink and Material rows; INSERT Scale X/Y/Z editing (attributes follow) and dynamic block custom properties shown read-only; a dimension's other rows (#273 made Dim style, Text override and eight DIMVAR rows editable, regenerated as M2-30 part 2: arrowheads, colours, linetypes and lineweights, Fit, the rest of Primary Units, Alternate Units and Tolerances, Measurement); HATCH pattern, angle and scale editing (with `@triya/hatch`); polyline vertex navigation and Global width; MTEXT contents through the in-place editor (the `...` button); TEXT Style and Justify choices; feet-and-inch typed values (the parser is decimal only); Quick Properties on hover (UI_SPEC §4) and the status-bar toggle; edits to objects inside a running command's selection. *Part 1 (#283):* with nothing selected the palette shows "No selection" and General with the drawing's current settings (`propertySheet` `current`, `currentProps`): CECOLOR, CLAYER, CELTYPE, CELTSCALE, CELWEIGHT and CETRANSPARENCY; a change sets the variable as one undo step (`applyCurrentEdit`; a frozen layer and a true colour, which DXF's `$CECOLOR` cannot hold, are refused), and LINE, TEXT, MTEXT, dimensions, MLEADER, REVCLOUD, WIPEOUT and PASTEBLOCK's reference take all six, where they took CLAYER only. The Layers palette marks the layer a change made current. CETRANSPARENCY has no DXF HEADER variable: saved only into an existing DICTIONARYVAR, otherwise kept for the session. Other draw commands (CIRCLE, PLINE, …) take them when they arrive with `currentProps`. *Part 2 (#285):* a TEXT's and an MTEXT's Style is a choice of the drawing's text styles (a fixed-height style gives the text its height, as TEXT does when placing one; AutoCAD's help is silent on the palette) and Justify a choice of AutoCAD's justifications, the insertion point kept and the text moving about it (a TEXT measured on the canvas first, `textOutlines`, as EATTEDIT does; Aligned or Fit for a text no font measures, and an annotative text, are refused and say why); a block reference's Scale X, Y and Z are typed (zero refused), its attributes following (`rescaledReference`: a scale that changes sign mirrors them as MIRROR does, by MIRRTEXT, and the rest stretches their points, height and width factor along the block's axes); a polyline's Global width sets every segment's width (DXF 43, the vertices' own widths dropped), `*VARIES*` where one polyline's segments differ. Settled without code: BLOCK_RECORD 281 ("Scale uniformly") is not enforced on Scale X/Y/Z, as acad-ts reads an absent 281 as `canScale` true, the opposite of AutoCAD's default (0, any scale), so the engine's `scalable` cannot be trusted; associative dimensions on a scaled reference's geometry are not re-measured (the same as a stretch). Also fixed in #285 (CI, about 1 run in 10 on main): ZOOM typed as a drawing opens reached a canvas sized but still waiting for its first view (extents after the renderer's measure) and failed "There is no view to change." (`text-columns.spec.ts` "columns set right to left…"); a view request now waits for that first view (`whenViewed`). *Part 3 (#288):* a hatch's Pattern rows are edited as AutoCAD 2024's are: Pattern name is typed (the palette offers SOLID and the library's names, the bundled patterns and the user's .pat files: the engine asks the app to place the pattern, view request `hatchPattern`, as it has no pattern files; Predefined, or Custom when one of the user's files defines it; SOLID makes a solid fill, writing no angle or scale as DXF does), Angle and Scale lay the stored pattern lines out again about the hatch's origin (its first line's base point: the hatch origin is kept only in the lines, and the ANSI, ISO, Triya and user-defined patterns start their first line there), a user-defined hatch has Spacing and Double (a second family at 90°) instead of Scale, and Island detection style is a choice; a solid fill greys Angle and Scale, a gradient keeps its rows read only. *Part 4 (#292):* Color's last choice, "Select Color...", opens AutoCAD's Select Color dialog: Index Color (the ACI palette 10–249 by hue and shade, the nine standard colours and six greys, ByLayer and ByBlock, the swatch under the pointer read as "Index color" and "Red, Green, Blue"; one tab stop, the arrow keys move), True Color (HSL or RGB, each channel a number and a slider) and Color Books, the Color field taking what -COLOR takes, `r,g,b` and `BOOK$COLOR`. Triya ships no colour books (AutoCAD's .acb files are Autodesk's), so Color Books offers the books the drawing uses with the colours of each it uses (`PropertySheet.colorBooks`), as -ATTEDIT's COlorbook does; with nothing selected only Index Color is offered (CECOLOR holds no true colour in DXF, part 1). AutoCAD's help shows the palette without stating its row order: rows run full saturation bright to dark, then half saturation dark to pale. Found: a layer's colour-book colour is read as its RGB only (io maps the DBCOLOR name of objects' colours, not layers'; the acadsharp sample's `Layer_color_book`), so its book is not offered. *Part 5 (#295):* a dimension's other rows, under AutoCAD 2024's groups and labels (`packages/engine/src/properties/dimension.ts`): Lines & Arrows (Arrow 1 and 2, lineweights, Dim line 1/2 and Ext line 1/2 on or off, colours, linetypes, Dim line ext, Ext line fixed and its length), Text (Fill color, Fraction type, Text color, Text outside/inside align, Text pos hor/vert, Text style), Fit (Dim line forced, Dim line inside, Fit, Text inside, Text movement), Primary Units (Decimal separator, Dim prefix and suffix, Dim roundoff, Dim units, the four Suppress rows), Alternate Units and Tolerances (display None/Symmetrical/Deviation/Limits/Basic, limits, position, precision, Suppress rows, text height), each written as DSTYLE overrides. A row may set several DIMVARs, as AutoCAD's do: Arrow 1 sets DIMBLK1 and turns DIMSAH on with the other end kept (closed filled is handle 0), Tolerance display sets DIMTOL, DIMLIM and DIMGAP's sign (Basic frames the text; Symmetrical makes the lower limit follow the upper), Dim prefix/suffix are the halves of DIMPOST about `<>` (`[]` for DIMAPOST), the Suppress rows DIMZIN's bits; DIMDSEP is written as its character code. The colour rows hold index colours only (DSTYLE overrides are ACIs) and open Select Color on its Index Color tab. Found and fixed: `@triya/dims` read DIMALTU as DIMLUNIT, so an alternate format of Architectural (6), Fractional (7) or Windows desktop (8) drew wrongly (`altUnits` in format.ts). Arrowheads offered are the drawing's own arrowhead blocks: AutoCAD makes a missing one (_DOT, _ARCHTICK, …) when it is chosen, which needs Triya-authored arrowhead blocks. Text view direction (DIMTXTDIRECTION) and Measurement are not shown, nor an angular dimension's angle format rows (DIMAUNIT, DIMADEC, DIMAZIN). *Part 6 (#296):* General's Plot style, Hyperlink and Thickness rows and the 3D Visualization group's Material, in AutoCAD 2024's places (`packages/engine/src/properties/general.ts`): Plot style is ByColor and read only in a colour-dependent drawing ($PSTYLEMODE 1, the default) and a choice of ByLayer and ACAD_PLOTSTYLENAME's names in a named one (ByBlock is not offered: the model reads a ByBlock or dictionary-default plot style as none); Hyperlink is the URL in the object's `PE_URL` xdata (the first 1000 string, as AutoCAD and ezdxf write it), its description and named location kept, PE_URL registered when the drawing has none, and an empty value takes the link off (`PropertyRow.clearable`); Thickness (DXF 39) shows where every object has one (LINE, CIRCLE, ARC, POINT, LWPOLYLINE, a 2D POLYLINE, TEXT, ATTDEF, SOLID, TRACE; AutoCAD's help lists none), 0 leaving the group out; Material offers ByLayer and the drawing's materials (absent, or the ByLayer material, reads ByLayer), shown for mixed selections too and not in a drawing without materials. With nothing selected General keeps its six current settings. Corpus gate `export-general-properties.corpus.test.ts` (saved AC1032, read back; ezdxf audit kind `properties`). Found: a DWG's MATERIALs are not written to DXF (FILE_FIDELITY.md, "MATERIAL is still skipped"), so a material set in a DWG-opened drawing does not survive its DXF save (DEC-047 item 4: the gate skips Material on DWG sources). *Part 7 (#305):* feet-and-inch and fractional values are typed as AutoCAD reads them in the drawing's LUNITS (`parseDistance` in `packages/engine/src/commands/parse.ts`; AutoCAD 2024 Help, "UNITS (Command)"): in Engineering (3) and Architectural (4), whose unit is the inch, feet and inches (`5'6"`, `5'-6-1/2"`, `5'-6 1/2"` as the palette shows it, `5.5'`, `1-1/2'`, `6"`; a number without a mark is inches), and in Architectural and Fractional (5) a whole number and a fraction (`6-1/2`, `66 1/2`, `3/8`). They are read at distance prompts, in typed coordinates (`@1'8",0`, `10'<90`), as a direct distance, in Dynamic Input's fields (a quote or inch mark after a digit stays in the field; a quote first is still a transparent command) and in the Properties palette's distance rows (the field now starts as the value is shown, `83339'-2"`, not a decimal), the dimension rows among them; a row that is a plain number (Linetype scale, Dim scale overall) takes no feet, as AutoCAD's do not. Decimal and Scientific units read as before, so `1-1/2` there is the expression's 0.5 (ARCHITECTURE.md §5.1's `'cal`-lite), and so do real and integer prompts. Found: prompt defaults and echoes (`<10.0000>`) are still shown in decimal whatever LUNITS says (AutoCAD shows them in the drawing's units). *Part 8 (#307):* a polyline's vertex navigation, as AutoCAD 2024's Geometry group shows it for one polyline: Vertex, a spinner from 1 to the vertex count (the palette's `spin` row; the session's `property-vertex` input picks the vertex the sheet shows, back to the first for another selection), then Vertex X and Vertex Y and the Start and End segment width of the segment leaving it (a constant-width polyline's DXF 43 becomes each vertex's own when one segment's width is set, as AutoCAD does), each edit one undo step and a moved vertex keeping its bulge; row keys name their vertex (`vertex:2:x`), so an edit reaches the vertex shown. The vertex shown is marked on the canvas (AutoSnap's × at AUTOSNAPSIZE) while one of its rows has the focus. AutoCAD's help does not say what Vertex shows for several polylines; one number naming a different point on each is no use to a drafter, so several polylines show no vertex rows. Misc adds Linetype generation (Enabled/Disabled, PLINEGEN, DXF 70 bit 128, which the save already wrote). *Part 9 (#309):* one MTEXT's Contents has AutoCAD 2024's `...` button, which opens the In-Place Text Editor on it as TEXTEDIT does (`PropertyRow.editor`, the session's `property-editor`, `PromptBus.editContents`): a running command is cancelled first, the edit is one undo step, and the MTEXT stays selected with the palette showing what the editor wrote; the editor's answer is echoed as TEXTEDIT's is (`Enter text: …`), no `Command:` line. AutoCAD's help does not say what `...` does for several MTEXTs; the editor edits one object, so several show their contents without it. *Part 10 (#310):* an angular dimension's Primary Units are angular ones (`DimvarRow.for`): Angle format (DIMAUNIT: Decimal Degrees, Degrees Minutes Seconds, Gradians, Radians, the Dimension Style Manager's names), Suppress leading and trailing zeros (DIMAZIN's bits) and Precision (DIMADEC, −1 shown as DIMDEC's value) replace the linear units, scale, roundoff and DIMZIN rows, and it has no Alternate Units or alternate tolerance rows (the dimension engine does not convert angles); AutoCAD's help does not list them, so they follow ActiveX AcadDimAngular's properties. A selection of linear and angular dimensions shows the rows both have, and an edit of a row for the other kind is refused. Text adds Measurement after Text rotation: read only, what the dimension measures before DIMLFAC (a length in the drawing's units, an angle in its angular units), worked out by `@triya/dims` `measuredValue`, which the app hands the engine as `EngineServices.measureDimension`. *Part 11 (#315):* a multileader whose content is text has AutoCAD 2024's Text group (`packages/engine/src/properties/multileader.ts`): Contents (read only; one multileader's has the `...` button, which opens the In-Place Text Editor as TEXTEDIT does, `PromptBus.editContents`), Text style (a choice of the drawing's text styles; a fixed-height style keeps the multileader's height, which is its MLEADERSTYLE's times its overall scale), Justify (Left, Center, Right: the column of the context data's attachment point, DXF 171, and the entity's text alignment, 175; ActiveX's TextJustify is an AcAttachmentPoint whose row the multileader's attachment overrides) and Text height. The text stays on its landing: the side of its box facing the landing (left edge right of a landing, right edge left of one, middle above or below the leader or between landings on both sides) is kept, so the insertion point moves by widths the canvas measures (`textWidths`; a defined width when the text wraps) and by the change in the attachment level a new height makes; text style and justification set their DXF 90 override bits (11 and 14, as ezdxf names them). An annotative multileader's Text edits are refused (each scale keeps its own placement, M2-43), as is one whose text the canvas cannot measure. Block content and mixed text and block multileaders show no Text rows. Corpus gate: `export-mleader-commands.corpus.test.ts` sets Justify, Text height and a text style on its first multileader and reads them back. *Part 12 (#317):* a multileader has AutoCAD 2024's Leaders group, before Text, for text and block content alike (`packages/engine/src/properties/multileader-leaders.ts`): Leader type (Straight, Spline, None: DXF 170), Leader color (the Select Color dialog's Index and True Color tabs: a colour-book name is saved only for an object's own colour), Leader linetype (the drawing's linetype records, ByLayer and ByBlock by name; none draws as ByBlock), Leader lineweight, Arrowhead (Closed filled and the drawing's arrowhead blocks, as a dimension's Arrow 1/2: `arrowheadOptions`; Closed filled is refused where the multileader's style names an arrowhead block, which a multileader without one draws), Arrowhead size (drawn at the overall scale, context 140), Horz landing (the dogleg, 291; turning it on turns the landing, 290, on too) and Landing distance (DXF 41 and every branch's 40). Each edit flags its DXF 90 override bit (the ODA specification's, as ezdxf names them: 0–3, 4 and 6, 7, 8, 9). The text stays put: a landing distance keeps where each landing ends and moves where the leader lines meet it (the connection point), which AutoCAD's help does not describe. An annotative multileader refuses an arrowhead size or landing distance (each scale keeps its own). A leader line's own overrides (LEADER_LINE 93) keep drawing. *Part 13 (#319):* a hatch's gradient has its own rows in place of a pattern's (`gradientRows` in `packages/engine/src/properties/sheet.ts`; AutoCAD's help does not list the palette's, so they follow ActiveX AcadHatch's GradientName, GradientColor1/2, GradientAngle and GradientCentered): Gradient name (DXF 470's nine names), Color 1 and Color 2 (Select Color's Index and True Color tabs: a gradient's colours are DXF 63/421, so ByLayer, ByBlock and colour-book names are refused), Gradient tint for a one-colour gradient in place of Color 2 (DXF 462, 0 shade to 1 tint, as GFCLRLUM), Gradient angle (460) and Centered (461: Yes 0, No 1, as GFSHIFT); a pattern hatch has Origin X and Y (AcadHatch.Origin: the first pattern line's base point, every family moved with it); Associative is a choice, No letting the hatch go of its boundary objects as HATCHEDIT does (each takes the hatch off its reactors; `dissociateHatch` in `edit/edit.ts`), Yes refused for a hatch that is not associative (it no longer names the objects; AutoCAD's help does not say what the palette's Yes does). A mixed selection shows each row for the hatches it edits. Corpus gate `export-hatch-properties.corpus.test.ts` (saved AC1032, ezdxf audit kind `hatch`, read back). *Part 14 (#321):* a text multileader's Text group goes on after Text height (`multiLeaderTextRowChange` in `packages/engine/src/properties/multileader.ts`; AutoCAD's help does not list the rows, so they follow ActiveX AcadMLeader's TextRotation, TextLineSpacingFactor, TextLineSpacingDistance, TextLineSpacingStyle, TextBackgroundFill and TextFrameDisplay, with MTEXT's palette labels): Text rotation (the text turned about its insertion point, as an MTEXT's Rotation row turns it), Line space factor (0.25 to 4), Line space distance (the factor times 5/3 of the text height, AutoCAD's single spacing; each multileader takes the factor its own height gives, and a distance one of them cannot take changes none), Line space style (At least, Exactly), Background mask (Yes turns it on in the drawing window colour with a 1.5 border, the Background Mask dialog's defaults, and keeps a mask already on; No turns it off) and Text frame (DXF 292, flagged as overriding the style with ENABLE_FRAME_TEXT, bit 17). An annotative multileader keeps rotation, spacing and mask per scale in its context data, so it refuses them (M2-43) and takes the frame. Direction (DXF 172) is not shown: the text layout draws no top-to-bottom flow. Corpus gate: `export-mleader-commands.corpus.test.ts` turns its first multileader's text 15°, spaces it 1.5 Exactly and turns its mask and frame on, and reads them back. *Part 15 (#326):* a multileader whose content is a block has a Block group in Text's place (`packages/engine/src/properties/multileader-block.ts`; AutoCAD's help does not list the rows, so they follow ActiveX AcadMLeader's ContentBlockName and BlockScale and the context data's block colour and rotation, labelled as MLEADERSTYLE's Block options): Source block (the drawing's named blocks, not anonymous, a layout's or an xref's; the new block's attributes take their definitions' values, as MLEADER offers them), Block color (Select Color's Index and True Color tabs), Block scale (the block's own, what it is drawn at over the overall scale; a mirrored block stays mirrored) and Block rotation, each flagged with its DXF 90 bit (BLOCK_CONTENT_ID 19, _COLOR 20, _SCALE 21, _ROTATION 22). The side of the block's box facing the landing stays where it was, as MLEADER attaches block content by its extents (the box of the turned extents, as `blockAnchor` places one), or the insertion point where the style connects by it or the block has no bounds the engine knows (text only). An annotative multileader refuses them (M2-43). M2-66 closed in the same PR. Corpus gate: `export-mleader-commands.corpus.test.ts` colours, scales and turns its `_TagCircle` multileader and gives it the detail callout, and reads them and their bits back. *Part 16 (#328):* Quick Properties (AutoCAD 2024 Help, "Quick Properties Palette", "QPMODE", "Quick Properties Tab (Drafting Settings Dialog Box)"; UI_SPEC §4's "on hover" was AutoCAD's rollover tooltips, so it now reads as AutoCAD's palette, shown for a selection): QPMODE (−2 to 2, initially −1: off, a negative value keeping the mode the QP button turns back on) and QPLOCATION (0 by the cursor, 1 static) are kept per user with the drafting settings and set by SETVAR; the status bar's QP button, Ctrl+Shift+P and the Ctrl+K palette's "Quick Properties on/off" turn it. Objects picked at `Command:` show it 50 px above and to the right of where the pick ended (the Drafting Settings tab's quadrant and distance, whose defaults Help does not state), or top left of the drawing area until its title bar drags it (static), kept inside the area; collapsed to three rows until the pointer or focus is in it. Its rows (`packages/engine/src/properties/quick.ts`, `quickPropertySheet`; the session's `properties` input takes `quick`) are Color, Layer and Linetype for every object and a few per type (`QUICK_PROPERTIES`, Triya's own: AutoCAD's are in its CUI file); several types show the three until the object list narrows them; QPMODE 2 shows it only where a selected type has its own rows. Edits are the Properties palette's (one undo step). It does not show while a command runs or the Properties palette is open; × hides it until the selection changes. *Part 17 (#331):* the command line shows lengths and angles in the drawing's units, as AutoCAD's RTOS and ANGTOS write them (AutoLISP Reference, "rtos", "angtos", "About String Conversions"; `packages/engine/src/commands/units.ts`, `drawingUnits`, `formatDistance`, `formatAngle`): a prompt's default (`<1'-6">`, `<0'-0 3/16">`, `<329d30'>`), the echo of an answer a front-end picks or sends, and the lengths TEXT and MTEXT (Text height) and REVCLOUD (arc lengths) report. LUNITS 1 scientific (`1.7500E+01`), 2 decimal, 3 engineering (`1'-5.50"`), 4 architectural (`1'-5 1/2"`), 5 fractional (`17 1/2`) at LUPREC; UNITMODE 1 as typed (`1'5-1/2"`, `17-1/2`). Both zero parts are kept (`0'-10"`, `2'-0"`), as the status bar and the Properties palette show lengths: Help says DIMZIN sets RTOS's zeros and does not say which its prompts use. Angles by AUNITS at AUPREC: decimal degrees without a sign, degrees/minutes/seconds (AUPREC 0 degrees, 1–2 minutes, 3–4 seconds, then decimals of a second), grads, radians and surveyor's bearings (`N 45d E`, a cardinal direction alone, no spaces under UNITMODE 1), taken into 0 to 2π first as ANGTOS does (`<-30>` is now `<330>`) and without ANGBASE, as before. Reals and integers stay plain numbers (SCALE's `<1.0000>`), and a whole decimal distance echoes as typed. Every form but a bearing reads back where it is typed (`parseDistance`, `parseAngleValue`, tested); the unit formatting is the engine's, ready for M3-07's inquiry commands. *Part 18 (#334):* surveyor's bearings are typed (`parseBearing` in `packages/engine/src/commands/parse.ts`; AutoCAD 2024 Help, "UNITS (Command)", Surveyor's Units): N or S, the angle from that axis (degrees/minutes/seconds or decimal degrees, 0 to 90), then E or W (`N45d30'E`; the palette's `N 45d30' E` with spaces), or a cardinal direction alone (`N`, `E`, `S`, `W`), in either case, at angle prompts, as a polar coordinate's angle (`@100<N45dE`) and in the Properties palette's angle rows, whatever AUNITS says (as `45d`, `1.2r` and `50g` are). A bearing names a direction from north, so ANGBASE and ANGDIR do not turn it (Help is silent; north-east is what a drafter means by N 45d E), and a default shown as a bearing reads back when typed. Keywords still win (a prompt offering `Exit` takes `E` as it). The palette shows an AUNITS 4 angle as a bearing (ANGTOS's form, the command line's), and its field starts as shown where it started in decimal degrees. *Part 19 (#336):* a layer's colour-book colour is read and kept (found by part 4): AutoCAD writes it as the LAYER's 430, `BOOK$COLOR` beside the 420 true colour, with no DBCOLOR (ACadSharp's R2004–R2018 samples' Layer_color_book, DIC COLOR GUIDE(R)$DIC 7). acad-ts dropped 430 on read and never wrote it, so every DXF save lost the name (patch 0181); io maps it to the layer's `bookName`/`colorName` and writes it back, taken off with a plain colour, where a layer's colour-book colour was refused. Select Color's Color Books now offers the books layers use too, and an object given a colour only a layer holds gets that colour's DBCOLOR in ACAD_COLOR at the save, numbered above every handle given out, as AutoCAD adds one. The Layers palette's Color column shows the colour's name (`DIC 7`) where it showed the RGB. Corpus gate `layer-book-colors.corpus.test.ts` (every ASCII DXF's LAYER 430 mapped, the binary saves alike, the AC1032 save writing them back). Not done: a DWG layer's colour name (DEC-047). *Part 20 (#339):* Dynamic Input's fields show the cursor's values in the drawing's units, as the command line words its defaults (RTOS and ANGTOS, the engine's `formatDistance`/`formatAngle`): X and Y, Distance by LUNITS/LUPREC (`2'-6 1/4"`), Angle by AUNITS/AUPREC (`45d0'`, `100.0g`, `N 45d E`), a SCALE factor as a plain number. Each prompt the bus emits carries the drawing's units and angle convention (`BusEvent` `prompt` `units`, `angles`), so a change by SETVAR or UNITS shows at the next prompt. The angle shown is the one that, typed, goes where the cursor is: ANGBASE and ANGDIR turn typed angles, so they turn the shown one back (a bearing is a world direction, part 18). AutoCAD's defaults (AUPREC 0) now show whole degrees where the fields showed four places. An untyped field beside a typed one still sends what it shows, which the engine reads back in every LUNITS (tested for all 25 LUNITS×AUNITS pairs). Playwright `command-line-dyn.spec.ts` sets LUNITS 4, AUNITS 1 and AUPREC 2 at `Command:` and reads the fields. Found M2-73 (the status bar keeps the units the drawing opened with). *Part 21 (#345):* rollover tooltips and QUICKPROPERTIES (AutoCAD 2024 Help, "ROLLOVERTIPS (System Variable)", "QUICKPROPERTIES (Command)"). At `Command:` the cursor resting on an object (400 ms, within 3 px: Help gives no delay) shows a tooltip below and to its right, the object's type name and its Color, Layer and Linetype (`rolloverSheet` in `packages/engine/src/properties/quick.ts`, the session's `rollover` input; AutoCAD lists the rows in the CUI's Rollover Tooltips, whose General entry is those three, and Triya ships no CUI, so every type shows them); it goes when the cursor moves off, a button is pressed, the wheel rolls or a command starts, and none shows while a command runs (Dynamic Input's tooltips are the cursor's then). ROLLOVERTIPS (0 or 1, initially 1) is kept per user with the drafting settings, set by SETVAR or Settings' "Show rollover tooltips". QUICKPROPERTIES takes the objects selected, or asks "Select objects", leaves them selected (`CommandContext.select`) and shows Quick Properties for them whatever QPMODE says (view request `palette` `quick`), by the cursor where they were picked, until the selection changes; nothing picked shows nothing (Help is silent). *Left:* the rest of the list above (Other… linetypes, dynamic block custom properties, Text view direction (DIMTXTDIRECTION, which the dimension engine does not draw yet), a multileader's other rows (Misc, Text's Direction once the text layout draws a top-to-bottom flow, and a block multileader's Attachment, the block connection type: the MULTILEADER's own DXF 176 is not mapped, so the style's is used), Quick Properties' leftovers (the Drafting Settings dialog's Quick Properties tab with its quadrant, distance and row count (M3-06), its Customize button (no CUI)), edits inside a running command's selection), SETVAR's distance variables in them (its table has no distance kind), and Select Color in the Layers palette and EATTEDIT's Properties tab (M5-05 and M2-35). *Part 22 (#351):* AutoCAD's arrowheads the drawing lacks are authored when chosen (AutoCAD 2024 Help, "Lines and Arrows Tab (Modify Dimension Style Dialog Box)": AutoCAD adds an arrowhead's `_NAME` block the first time it is used). A dimension's Arrow 1 and Arrow 2 and a multileader's Arrowhead (in the palette and Quick Properties) offer closed filled and all nineteen of AutoCAD's arrowheads in AutoCAD's order, then a user arrow the objects use; one the drawing has is its block (matched without case, `_ArchTick`), one it lacks is offered by its block name and, when chosen, added as that block before the edit, in the same undo step (`addArrowheadBlock` in `packages/engine/src/edit/arrowhead-blocks.ts`): based at the tip, drawn for a size of 1 with its back towards −X, every part on layer 0, ByBlock, with handles above `$HANDSEED`. The shapes AutoCAD stores in drawings we read are kept (`_ARCHTICK` a 0.15-wide stroke, `_CLOSEDBLANK` a sixth of the size either side, `_BOXBLANK` a unit square and a tail); Help gives the rest no sizes, so our reading (ezdxf's MIT arrows draw the same) is a filled dot 1 across, open arms back to the size at their angle, an equilateral datum triangle, two quarter arcs for the integral, and the small dots half the dot's size (ezdxf draws them as large; AutoCAD's list shows them smaller). Arrowheads whose dimension line stops at their back carry it on to the tip. Corpus gate: `export-mleader-commands.corpus.test.ts` gives the first multileader Closed blank on every readable file, authoring `_CLOSEDBLANK` where the drawing lacks it, and the block reads back from the AC1032 save with its parts; Playwright `dimension-arrowheads.spec.ts`. *Part 23 (#356):* SETVAR's lengths are shown and typed in the drawing's units, as AutoCAD's prompts for them are (RTOS; `formatDistance`, `parseDistance`): a `distance` kind for the header's lengths (TEXTSIZE, TRACEWID, PLINEWID, FILLETRAD, CHAMFERA/B/C, ELEVATION, PELEVATION, THICKNESS, SKETCHINC and the DIMVAR distances DIMTXT, DIMASZ, DIMEXO, DIMEXE, DIMDLI, DIMDLE, DIMTSZ, DIMCEN, DIMGAP, DIMRND, DIMTP, DIMTM, DIMFXL), the DICTIONARYVAR CENTEREXE (still written as AutoCAD's six-place text) and the read-only VIEWSIZE; a point's coordinates (INSBASE, LIMMIN, VIEWCTR, …) likewise, SCREENSIZE staying in pixels. Factors (LTSCALE, DIMSCALE, DIMLFAC), PDSIZE (negative is a percentage) and DIMALTRND (alternate units) stay plain numbers. Text that is no length is refused with "Requires numeric distance." (AutoCAD's distance prompts add "or two points"; SETVAR here takes no picked points). EATTEDIT's Properties tab's Color list ends with Select Color..., opening the Select Color dialog over the editor with the colour books the drawing uses (`colorBooks` moved to `packages/engine/src/properties/color-books.ts` so commands offer them; the eattedit request's `colorBooks`); Esc closes Select Color only. Playwright `attributes.spec.ts`. *Left:* Other… linetypes, dynamic block custom properties, Text view direction, a multileader's Misc, Direction and block Attachment rows, Quick Properties' Drafting Settings tab (M3-06), edits inside a running command's selection, and SETVAR's angles (ANGBASE) in AUNITS. *Part 24 (#359):* SETVAR's angles are shown and typed in the drawing's angular units, as AutoCAD's prompts for them are (ANGTOS; `formatAngle`, `parseAngleValue`): ANGBASE, CHAMFERD, SNAPANG and DIMJOGANG, which the drawing keeps in radians (`ANGLE_VARIABLES` in `packages/engine/src/commands/sysvars.ts`). ANGBASE 90 under Surveyor's units reads `<N>` and takes `N 45d30' W`, `0.5r`, `50g` or `45d30'`; a plain number is decimal degrees whatever AUNITS says, as at any angle prompt; a negative angle shows as its turn (`270.0`), as ANGTOS writes it. Typed values are the variable's own, counter-clockwise from world +X: ANGBASE and ANGDIR turn angles typed at a command's prompts, not these (Help is silent on SETVAR ANGBASE under ANGDIR 1). DIMJOGANG takes 5 to 90 degrees (the Modify Dimension Style dialog's Jog angle range), where it showed and took radians as a plain number; text that is no angle is refused with "Requires valid numeric angle." (AutoCAD adds "or second point"; SETVAR takes no picks). *Part 26 (#361):* a dimension's Text view direction row (DIMTXTDIRECTION, after Text rotation as AutoCAD 2024 lists it: Left-to-Right or Right-to-Left, written as a DSTYLE override of 294), and the dimension engine draws it: AutoCAD 2024 Help calls 1 a "Right-to-Left reading style" and shows no picture, so the text is turned half a turn about its middle to read from the other side of the dimension line (upside down on a horizontal dimension), staying where the placement rules put it, the common drafter reading (CADTutor topic 40964 pairs it with text below the line for a rotated viewport) (`viewed` in `packages/dims/src/geometry.ts`, every dimension type, user-rotated text too). *Part 25 (#360):* a multileader's Misc rows, last as for a block reference (AutoCAD's help does not list a multileader's rows; these follow ActiveX AcadMLeader's StyleName and ScaleFactor): Multileader style and Annotative shown, Overall scale (DXF 45, flag bit 24) set. The context data states sizes as drawn, so an overall scale change multiplies the text height, wrap width and box height, the landing gap, the drawn arrowhead size, each landing's length and the block's scale by the change (the entity's own sizes, DXF 41 and 42, are before the scale and stay); the arrowheads stay where they point and the content grows about its anchor (context DXF 10), each landing keeping its place against the content, so the point where the leader lines meet it moves (AutoCAD's help does not say where it grows from; a similarity about one point keeps wrapped text on its lines with no measuring). A block multileader's Attachment (Center extents or Insertion point, the MLEADERSTYLE dialog's names; ActiveX BlockConnectionType) is the multileader's own DXF 176, now mapped and saved (`MultiLeaderGeometry.blockConnection`, absent meaning its style's; MLEADER states its style's), flagged by bit 23: the point that met the landing stays (the facing side's middle by extents, the base point by insertion point), and the Block rows' edits keep the block by its own attachment instead of its style's. An annotative multileader refuses both (M2-43). Corpus gate: `export-mleader-commands.corpus.test.ts` sets an overall scale and Insertion point and reads them and their bits back. *Left:* Other… linetypes (with the LINETYPE command's Load dialog), dynamic block custom properties, a multileader's Direction row and another Multileader style chosen in the palette (its values re-applied to what the multileader does not override), Quick Properties' Drafting Settings tab (M3-06), and edits inside a running command's selection. *Part 27 (#362):* an MTEXT's and a multileader's Text have a Direction row after Justify, where AutoCAD 2024's MTEXT palette lists it: Left to right, Top to bottom or By style (ActiveX AcDrawingDirection's acLeftToRight, acTopToBottom and acByStyle; MTEXT DXF 72, the multileader context data's MTEXT DXF 172; a 0, 2 or 4 AutoCAD does not write reads as Left to right). The text stays where it is and is drawn down its columns only in a font with vertical glyphs, as M1-110 draws a vertical MTEXT (AutoCAD offers vertical text for no other font), so the bundled Hershey fonts draw it left to right still. An annotative multileader refuses it (each scale keeps its own context data); an annotative MTEXT takes it, 72 being the entity's own. MLEADERSTYLE has no direction, so no override bit is set. Help does not list the palette's option labels; they follow the ActiveX names (noted in the PR). *Left:* Other… linetypes (with the LINETYPE command's Load dialog), dynamic block custom properties, another Multileader style chosen in the palette (its values re-applied to what the multileader does not override), Quick Properties' Drafting Settings tab (M3-06), and edits inside a running command's selection. *Part 28 (#363):* a multileader's Multileader style (Misc) is a choice of the drawing's styles, as ActiveX AcadMLeader.StyleName sets it: the new style's values go to every property the multileader does not override, and what it overrides (DXF 90's bits) keeps its own value and flag (`packages/engine/src/properties/multileader-style.ts`). The style's sizes are drawn at the style's overall scale where the multileader does not override it, grown about the content's anchor as the Overall scale row grows it (an annotative style gives its scale to a plain multileader, as MLEADER does until M2-43); leader type, colour, linetype, lineweight, landing, dogleg, arrowhead and its size, landing distance and gap; text style, colour, height, alignment, frame, angle type and top/bottom attachments; block, colour, scale, rotation and attachment. The content stays where it is, as the Text and Block rows keep it: the text's side facing the landing (measured by the canvas where its width matters), the block's facing side or base point; each landing keeps its place against the content, so a new landing gap or distance moves where the leader lines meet it. What cannot be re-applied in place is kept and flagged as the multileader's own, as MLEADER flags a setting that differs from its style: the content type (text stays text), the attachment direction, a left or right attachment that anchors the text by another row of its lines (the text's height in lines is the canvas's), and a source block the drawing lacks or the style does not name. AutoCAD's help does not list what a changed style re-applies; the list follows the ODA override bits (noted in the PR). Choosing the style it has changes nothing; an annotative multileader refuses (M2-43). Corpus gate: `export-mleader-commands.corpus.test.ts` gives the boxed note another style on the 54 drawings with a second style and reads its style and flags back. *Left:* Other… linetypes (with the LINETYPE command's Load dialog), dynamic block custom properties, Quick Properties' Drafting Settings tab (M3-06), and edits inside a running command's selection. *Part 30 (#365):* while a command's "Select objects" prompt runs, the palette shows what it has selected so far (as it has since M2-21), and an edit now changes those objects inside the command (`PromptBus.setProperty`), which goes on prompting with its selection; before, the edit cancelled the command first and so changed the drawing's current settings instead of what the palette showed. With nothing selected yet the edit sets the current setting and the command goes on too. The edit joins the undo step the command is building, as a transparent command's edits do (AutoCAD groups what is done while a command runs into its undo group), so one U takes back the edit and the command and the change sets are undone in the order made; a command cancelled afterwards keeps it as its step. A dimension the edit restyles is redrawn at once. Any other prompt is still cancelled first (^C^C), and the `...` In-Place Text Editor, a command of its own, cancels the command but opens on the MTEXT the prompt selected, which stays selected. AutoCAD 2024's Help does not say what a palette edit does mid-command; the common expectation, that an edit changes what the palette shows, is the one encoded (noted in the PR). *Part 29 (#364):* a dynamic block reference's custom properties, read-only in a Custom section after Misc (`properties/dynamic.ts`): each parameter whose Show properties is on, by its label (linear and polar distance, polar and rotation angle, XY's X and Y distances, a point's X and Y, flip state, visibility state, lookup value), with the value AutoCAD saved with the reference (its AcDbBlockRepresentation's ACAD_ENHANCEDBLOCKDATA XRECORD for the parameter's node, read from the samples since no reference documents it) or, for a reference it saved none for, the definition's own; `*VARIES*` across references to one dynamic block, none across different blocks. AutoCAD's Help does not say what a rotation's Angle is measured from: the base angle's line, which is +X in every sample. Corpus gate: `dynamic-properties.corpus.test.ts` checks every reference of the acadsharp dynamic-block samples against values read off their DXF by hand, and their DWG saves against the DXF. *Closed (#366):* the owner moved the last two leftovers to later tasks on 2026-11-15: Other… in the Linetype list (with the Linetype Manager's Load dialog it opens) to M5-08, and Quick Properties' tab in the Drafting Settings dialog (quadrant, distance, rows) to M3-06, each added to that task's acceptance. M2-35 is complete.

- [x] (#290, #291) **M2-38** PDF underlay plot leftovers (found by M2-16 part 5, #253): an encrypted PDF (the Standard security handler with an empty user password, RC4 and AES, which most "protected" drawings use) plots its boundary only, as `PdfSource` refuses it, while pdf.js shows it on screen: decrypt strings and streams while copying; PDF layers (AutoCAD's ULAYERS: the underlay's optional content groups turned off) are neither on screen nor in a plot (copied pages show their default visibility); the page's annotations are not plotted (AutoCAD's help is silent; check what it plots); the Plot dialog does not say which underlays plotted without their file. **Acceptance:** an encrypted test PDF (written by a test, no file committed) plots as the unencrypted one; screen and plot honour the underlay's saved layer settings. *Part 1 (#290):* an encrypted PDF that opens without a password plots its page: `PdfSource` reads the Standard security handler with the empty user password (`security.ts`: V1/V2 RC4 40–128-bit, V4 crypt filters RC4 or AESV2, V5 AES-256 in revision 5 and 6, a stream's own /Crypt filter, /EncryptMetadata false) and hands back every object's strings and streams decrypted (not the cross-reference stream nor the Encrypt dictionary; an object stream's objects through the stream), also when the cross-reference is damaged and rebuilt by scanning; MD5, SHA-256/384/512, RC4 and AES-CBC are `crypto.ts`'s own (synchronous, Node and worker alike), checked against Node's. One that needs a password, or another security handler, still plots its boundary, the reason in the `PdfReadError`. Test files are written by `packages/testkit/src/pdf/encrypt.ts` with Node's crypto (no file committed), read by pdf.js first, and each plots byte for byte as the plain one (`encrypted.test.ts`). *Part 2 (#291):* the PDF layers an underlay turns off are hidden on screen and in the plot. ULAYERS keeps them as the strings of the underlay's `AdeskUnderlayLayerOverrideData` xdata (the application AutoCAD registers in every drawing; its layout is undocumented, and every 1000 string read as a layer name, a DGN underlay's `???DGN_Placeholder???` skipped, is how other open readers take it), mapped to `UnderlayGeometry.layersOff` and saved as passthrough. On screen the page is asked for with those layers (`UnderlayRequest.layersOff`), drawn by pdf.js with their optional content groups hidden, and kept under `underlayKey` apart from the plain page's; the plot lists the copied groups in its /OCProperties, off where the PDF shows them off by default (copied pages showed every group before) or the underlay turns them off by name, an underlay with layers off getting copies of its own. Test drawing `fixtures/underlay/pdf-underlay-layers.{dxf,pdf}` (one page, three layers, attached twice); pdf.js and Poppler both read the plot's layers as written. Closes M2-38; the annotations, the Plot dialog note and ULAYERS itself moved to M2-56.

Outlines stay where the PDF plot draws them (fsType forbids embedding, Convert to geometry) and where a glyph cannot be selected by its character (.notdef, one character shown by two glyphs, controls and invisible format characters, above the BMP), and in the SVG a PNG or a clipboard picture rasterises. Tests: `packages/testkit/src/pdf/svg-text.test.ts` (opentype.js reads the embedded fonts), `apps/web/test/{plot,system-clipboard}.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/fidelity/svg.spec.ts` (pages 6–7 against the PDF goldens, and placed in a page: fonts loaded, every character at its `x`). *Part 4 (#301):* the Publish dialog's Publish to is PDF, SVG or PNG; an SVG or PNG publish writes a file a sheet (`publishSheetFiles` in `apps/web/src/publish.ts`, each sheet with its page setup to the plotter chosen, named "<drawing>-<layout>.svg"), downloaded as itself when one sheet published and as one ZIP named after the drawing when more did (`publishedDownload`, `zipFiles` in `packages/pdf/src/zip.ts`: PNGs stored, SVGs deflated, repeated names numbered), since a page cannot write a folder in every browser and several downloads at once are blocked or asked about; Export Data's All layouts is offered for SVG and PNG too, a file a layout in one ZIP (the File name becomes `<drawing>.zip`, `exportExtension`).
