- [x] (#329) **M2-51** PDF text copies wrongly where characters share a glyph (found by M2-33, #279): the plot's embedded fonts give each glyph one CID and the ToUnicode CMap maps it to the first character it was shown for (`EmbeddedFont.cid` in `packages/pdf/src/fonts.ts`), so text whose characters the font lacks (all drawn with .notdef) or that share a glyph (space and no-break space) copies and searches as the first such character: `TEXT` in a font without T, E and X reads `TTTT`. Give a character that maps to an already-used glyph its own CID (a CIDToGIDMap stream instead of Identity, or an ActualText span). **Acceptance:** a pdf writer unit test where .notdef and a shared glyph copy as the characters typed; plot-sheet.pdf text unchanged otherwise. *Why:* found by the offline plot test, which can only assert the text's shape until this lands. *Done (#329):* a glyph's first character keeps code = CID; each further character gets a code above every CID of the font (TrueType: its glyph count; CFF: its highest CID + 1), mapped back to the glyph's CID by an embedded encoding CMap (one `cidrange` per high byte below that code, a `cidchar` per further character) that replaces Identity-H only when needed, so the subset and plot-sheet.pdf are byte-identical; ToUnicode maps codes. Chosen over a CIDToGIDMap stream because a CIDFontType0 (CFF) has none, and over ActualText because pdf.js ignores it when copying. A font whose CIDs reach 0xFFFF has no free code: its further characters copy as the glyph's first. Tests: `truetype.test.ts` and `cff.test.ts` (plain and CID-keyed) read the text back with pdf.js; the offline plot test now expects `TEXT` eight times.

A production build had no long task over 100 ms before the fix. `DrawingFile.bytes` is now non-enumerable (`KeptFile` in save.ts); on the dev server no long task over 100 ms remains on open or tab switch, the switch shows in ~50 ms, and health.spec.ts:74 keeps the default timeout and checks no long task reaches 200 ms on the switch.
- [x] (#314) **M2-64** CI for three threads at once (owner's ask, 2026-11-14): M2-58 sized CI for two task threads. A PR run asks for 10 runners at once (lint and types, three unit, two fidelity and two UI shards, offline; perf too on hot PRs and on `main`) and the account runs at most 20 jobs at a time, so with three threads every merge (a push to `main` plus an update-branch run on each other open PR, all at once) asks for 31 or more and a third of them queue; the unit shards had also drifted apart (5.5 against 9 minutes) and `acad-ts-patches-2.corpus.test.ts` had passed the 300 s per-file limit. Fit three concurrent PRs under the job limit without running fewer tests on a PR or weakening a gate, keeping the two required check names. **Acceptance:** at most 8 runners at once per PR run; a push to `main` whose tree already passed both required checks on its PR does not run the suites again (perf still runs); the committed timings split the unit suite evenly; no Vitest file over 300 s; `tests/ci/workflows.test.ts` pins it; before→after runner use and wall time recorded on the PR. *Why:* with three threads, queued jobs hold every thread's merge (DEC-055). *Done (#314):* the fidelity and UI suites share three shards (`fidelity · ui · shard i/3`, the fidelity diffs kept before the UI run empties `test-results/`), so a PR run asks for 8 runners instead of 10; `detect hot-path changes` outputs the run's tree, a PR run whose two required checks passed uploads `ci-passed-<tree>` (`record the tree that passed`), and a push to `main` that finds its tree among this repository's unexpired ones skips every piece and both gathering jobs and runs only `performance budgets`, noting the run that passed it (a failed lookup runs everything). `acad-ts-patches-2.corpus.test.ts` (319 s) is now parts 2 and 3; `pnpm test:timings` reads CI's absolute file names relative to the runner's checkout and averages with the committed timings, as a file runs faster on a lighter shard. This PR's run: 12.5 min end to end beside two other threads' runs, no job queued, about 51 runner minutes (before: 11–12 min, 10 runners, about 51 runner minutes; a `main` run 55 runner minutes on 11 runners).

- [x] (#350) **M2-67** Flaky CI start of a Playwright shard (seen once by M2-35 part 13, #319): a `fidelity · ui` shard failed before its first test when Vite's dependency optimiser renamed `node_modules/.vite/deps_temp_*` onto `deps` and got `ENOTEMPTY` (two optimiser runs racing on the same cache, as the dev server re-optimises on first request). Pre-bundle the dependencies before the web server starts (`optimizeDeps` entries/`vite optimize`, or a `cacheDir` per run) so no run renames onto a directory another run filled. **Acceptance:** the Playwright web server starts with the dependencies already optimised (no `new dependencies optimized` / re-optimise in its log on a cold cache); `pnpm test:fidelity` and `pnpm test:ui` green. *Why:* a shard that fails at start costs a full CI rerun with three threads sharing runners. *Done (#350):* Playwright's global setup (`tests/e2e/global-setup.ts`) pre-bundles the dependencies of every test dev server config (the app's, the render harness's, the io harness's) once per run, before any worker starts a server, each into its own cache (`apps/web/node_modules/.vite-e2e`, `node_modules/.vite-e2e-render`, `node_modules/.vite-e2e-io`, so the render and io harnesses no longer share the repository's `node_modules/.vite` under different config hashes, and a test run never rewrites `pnpm dev`'s cache). It uses Vite's `optimizeDeps` (deprecated as a manual call, still the one call that scans and commits up front) with `force`, so the cache always holds what today's imports need. New `tests/e2e/ui/deps-prebundled.spec.ts`: a server opening a DWG and a PDF-underlay DXF with its PDF logs no optimiser message and leaves the cache's `_metadata.json` untouched; it fails on a cold cache without the setup, and with a stale config hash ("Re-optimizing dependencies because vite config has changed").

A layout with no such block left still maps none and is kept as read (no space to show).

AutoCAD keeps a view's category as DXF 300 of the `ADSK_XREC_VTRVIEWINFO` XRECORD in the VIEW's extension dictionary (`<None>` when it has none; sample_AC1032_ascii.dxf's `view_custom`); Triya maps neither, so read it into the view record, write it back on the existing XRECORD by handle, and make the extension dictionary and XRECORD for a view that has none. **Acceptance:** a corpus test reads `view_custom`'s `<None>`; a Playwright test gives a view a category in the View Manager, saves, and the reopened drawing (and ezdxf) lists it under that category. *Why:* the last of the View Manager's General rows a 2D drafter uses. *Done (#355):* `ViewRecord.category` (absent for `<None>`), read from DXF 300 of the VIEW's `ADSK_XREC_VTRVIEWINFO` XRECORD (map/tables.ts) and written back by handle into that group alone, or into a new XRECORD (300 only) in the view's extension dictionary, made when it has none, numbered above every engine handle (export/view-records.ts; AutoCAD's other groups there, 302 layer snapshot, 330 and 294, belong to features Triya lacks and are kept as read); a view taken back to none keeps its record saying `<None>`. Engine edit `view-category` and New View's `category` on the `view` edit (each a VIEW undo step; 255 characters at most, `<None>` or nothing for none). View Manager: Category row and New View's View category, fields offering `<None>` and the drawing's categories (`viewCategories`). Corpus gate `export-view-categories.corpus.test.ts` (every readable file; ezdxf reads every category back in the audit's new `categories` save, audit.py `READ_BACK`); Playwright `view-categories.spec.ts`. AutoCAD's categories from a sheet set are out of scope (PRD non-goal).
- [x] **M2-71** Dynamic Input type-ahead (moved from M2-47, #327): M2-47 part 3 (#323) holds a space typed ahead of a prompt at the command line until the prompt it reaches, but Dynamic Input's tooltip still decides a space at the key, so `-VIEW` `S` `Two words` typed fast into the tooltip ends the name at the space. Hold the tooltip's keys the same way (`holdsKey`/`replayAhead`), replaying them into the fields of the prompt they reach (a relative second point stays relative). **Acceptance:** Playwright: the M2-47 type-ahead test typed into Dynamic Input instead of the command line saves `Two words`, and `LINE` `5,5` `10,0` typed at once into the tooltip draws the line from 5,5 to 15,5 (the second point relative, as Dynamic Input reads it). *Why:* AutoCAD buffers type-ahead in both. *Done (#343):* every key typed while the engine has not read the last input is held, at the command line and in the tooltip alike (`holdsKey`, which now holds letters and Enter too: only the prompt a key reaches says which fields it goes into), and replayed into Dynamic Input's fields when the tooltip shows the prompt reached (`dynAhead`), at the command line otherwise. Dynamic Input read `10,0` at a next point as an absolute point (a comma made the Distance field's text raw); a comma after a number now switches to X and Y relative to the last point (AutoCAD's DYNPICOORDS 0), which the acceptance's line needs. Dynamic Input's relative points now count from the rubber band's base, which its fields measure from (`submit` `relativeTo`, bus `#relativeBase`), not from the last point given: COPY's `0,20` then `0,-20` copy up and down from the base point, as AutoCAD's tooltip reads them. Five UI specs that typed absolute next points at the cursor now type them with `#` (AutoCAD's absolute prefix under Dynamic Input). Esc is held behind held keys and numbered like a submit (session `cancel` with `seq` answers with `read`), so it cancels after them and a second Esc cancels at once.

When a save names pasted pictures, offer to write their PNGs beside the DXF (File System Access: the folder the DXF is saved in, asked once; a download otherwise), and let the References palette save a kept picture's file. **Acceptance:** Playwright: Save As of a drawing with a pasted picture writes the PNG under the IMAGEDEF's file name beside the DXF; the References palette saves a kept picture. *Why:* markup sent to someone else should show the screenshot it was drawn on.
- [x] (#342) **M2-73** The status bar's coordinates follow units changed while the drawing is open (found by M2-35 part 20, #339): `coordinateText` reads LUNITS/LUPREC from the header the drawing opened with, so after SETVAR LUNITS or UNITS the status bar keeps the old units while Dynamic Input and the command line show the new ones. The engine should tell the app the header's units when they change (as each prompt now carries them), and the status bar use them. **Acceptance:** Playwright: SETVAR LUNITS 4 at `Command:` and the status bar shows feet and inches without reopening. *Why:* one value shown two ways on one screen reads as a bug.

## M3 — Full drafting command set and precision

**Exit criteria:** every tier-1 and tier-2 Draw/Modify/Selection/Inquiry command in `COMMANDS.md` is done with tests; full grips; full selection; full precision input.

- [x] (#368, #373, #375, #376, #378, #385) **M3-01** LINE (all options), PLINE (arc/width/halfwidth/close/undo/length), CIRCLE (2P/3P/TTR/TTT), ARC (all 11 methods incl. continue), RECTANG (C/E/F/T/W/A/D/R), POLYGON, ELLIPSE (+arc), XLINE/RAY (all), POINT + PDMODE/PDSIZE, DONUT, SPLINE (fit/CV, knots), DIVIDE/MEASURE (+block). **Acceptance:** per-command scripted tests. *From M2-02 (#222):* LINE's prompt loop (Undo, Close, Enter continuing from the last line) exists; left for LINE here: continuing from the last arc (with ARC), tangent/perpendicular starts through osnaps (M2-04), and drawing with CECOLOR/CELTYPE/CELWEIGHT (new lines take CLAYER and ByLayer properties). *Part 1 (#368):* CIRCLE (Center Radius/Diameter, 3P, 2P, Ttr) and ARC (all eleven methods and Continue), dragged while their last value is asked (`geom/construct.ts`, `commands/draw/sketch.ts`); LINE and ARC continue tangent from each other's last end; LINE is done (osnap starts #226, current properties M2-35). *Part 2 (#373):* the deferred TANgent and PERpendicular object snaps (asked for by the owner, 2026-11-15): where a prompt solves them later (`PointPrompt.deferred`), TAN and PER offer AutoCAD's Deferred Tangent and Deferred Perpendicular (marker with an ellipsis, tooltip), the click carrying the object and the pick to the command (`geom/deferred.ts`); LINE's first point takes them (a line tangent to two circles, tangent to one and perpendicular to another, perpendicular to two, or to a point; "Line does not exist." otherwise), and CIRCLE 3P's points take deferred TAN on lines, circles and arcs, mixed with points: Tan, Tan, Tan is CIRCLE ↵ 3P ↵ and TAN with a pick three times (the ribbon's button with M7-01); typed TAN/PER/NEA overrides ask "to" and keep the drag. *Part 3 (#375):* PLINE (line and arc segments in one LWPOLYLINE growing as drawn: Arc with Angle, CEnter, CLose, Direction, Radius and Second pt; Width and Halfwidth with PLINEWID; Length; Undo; Close; PLINEGEN), RECTANG (Chamfer, Elevation, Fillet, Thickness and Width kept per drawing; Area counting the corners, Dimensions, Rotation with Pick points) and POLYGON (sides kept, Inscribed or Circumscribed with a typed radius levelling the bottom edge, Edge), dragged with the cursor (`geom/shapes.ts`, sketch shapes `pline`, `rectangle`, `polygon`, `polygonEdge`). ARRAYEDIT changes counts, spacings, angles, rotation, method, alignment and polar/path base points; COPY makes an array of its own, ERASE takes its record. *Part 2 (#386):* ARRAYEDIT Source opens an item's objects in its place (the item left out of the array) until ARRAYCLOSE saves them into its block or puts them back, every item following each command's edits and objects drawn meanwhile joining them; REPlace puts other objects (kept in the drawing) in picked items or in place of the source objects, saved as 92/93/341 groups after the record's (same version, DEC-059); RESet takes replaced items back; lines, rays, xlines, ellipses, splines, points, solids, traces, 3D faces and polylines without arcs or widths carried by the scale; texts and attribute definitions whose baseline stays square to their height take the stretch in their height and width factor, and nested references whose axes stay square stay references, scaled; An arc's grips in Dynamic Input: its ends measure the chord from the other end (Resulting Dimension, Length Change, Absolute Angle, Angle Change) and the Arc Radius that chord makes with the chord height STRETCH keeps, its middle the Arc Radius of the arc through both ends; a typed radius sets the end along the cursor's direction, or the middle on the cursor's side, the short or the long way round as the cursor is nearer. Typed input in a viewport's own UCS needs a viewport made current (MSPACE): moved to M6-02.
- [x] (#425, #426) **M3-16** Edit-command leftovers (added by M3-12 part 7, #424, so M3-12 could close): PEDIT vertex tangents saved (DXF 50) and the 3D polyline's own Edit vertex prompts; CHPROP's COlorbook (colour-book colours the drawing already holds); MATCHPROP's Viewport, Table and Center object settings and its paintbrush cursor; SPLINEDIT's tangent on a closed spline (stored, the curve stays periodic), the fit tolerance's approximation (M1-65) and the control frame shown while editing vertices; OVERKILL with Do not break polylines off (polyline segments deleted as duplicates of lines), polyline arc segments against arcs, partial overlaps with polyline segments. **Acceptance:** per-item tests, as M3-02's. *Part 1 (#425):* OVERKILL compares polyline straight and arc segments with lines, arcs, circles and other polylines' segments: a segment lying within another object is deleted by breaking its polyline (the later drawn of two the same span; none with Do not break polylines, nor a wide segment unless widths are ignored; `edit/overkill.ts` `breakPolyline`), and lines and arcs partly on polyline segments are cut back to what lies off them; CHPROP and CHANGE COlorbook from the colour books of the colours the drawing holds; MATCHPROP Viewport (on/off, display locking, scale, snap and grid on/off, UCS icon; no shade plot, which Triya does not keep) and the paintbrush cursor (`SelectionPrompt.cursor`). MATCHPROP Table moved to M4-05 and Center object to M4-08. Left: PEDIT tangents and 3D Edit vertex prompts, the SPLINEDIT items. *Part 2 (#426):* PEDIT's Edit vertex Tangent stores the tangent on the vertex (`PolylineVertex.tangent`), saved as VERTEX flag 2 and DXF 50 (an angle in degrees, which acad-ts wrote as the radians it held until patch 0184), so Fit uses it then and in any later PEDIT; a lightweight polyline given one becomes a heavyweight 2D polyline, as Fit makes it; ROTATE, MIRROR and Reverse turn it with the polyline. A 3D polyline's Edit vertex offers AutoCAD's Next, Previous, Break, Insert, Move, Regen, Straighten and eXit ("3D Polyline Selection (PEDIT)"), and its vertices carry their heights (`z`, DEC-062): Move keeps a vertex's, Insert takes the height of the segment under the point, Spline curve fits them and Decurve takes them back, Break and Straighten keep them, and a save writes each from the model rather than by index. SPLINEDIT: a closed spline's tangent shapes the curve at its seam (C1 along it, the rest as before); a fit tolerance above 0 makes SPLINE, SPLINEDIT's toLerance and the other fit edits approximate (least squares with knots by averaging, Piegl and Tiller §9.4.1, the ends and their tangents kept, the fewest control points within the tolerance; AutoCAD documents no algorithm, so M1-65's check against AutoCAD stays post-launch); Edit vertex shows the control frame. Every corpus 2D polyline curve-fit with a tangent and every 3D polyline spline-fit or given a vertex, saved and reopened (`export-pedit.corpus.test.ts`).
- [x] (#427) **M3-17** JOIN of 3D polylines (added by M3-16 part 2, #426): M3-12 part 2 left them out because their vertices' heights were kept by index in the file; since DEC-062 the model carries them, so JOIN can join 3D polylines whose ends meet (in plan and in height) into one, as AutoCAD 2024's JOIN joins 3D polylines with each other. **Acceptance:** per-case tests; corpus 3D polylines joined to a copy of themselves set end to end, saved and reopened with their heights.
- [x] (#428) **M3-18** JOIN of other objects to 3D polylines (added by M3-17, #427): AutoCAD 2024's JOIN joins any linear or curved object to a source 3D polyline, and several noncoplanar linear objects at once into a 3D polyline. M3-17 joins 3D polylines only with each other: a sloped LINE's end heights are not in the model (DEC-039 keeps its plan view and writes its Z back from the original), a curve joined to a 3D polyline makes a 3D spline, which the model does not have, and a spline-fit 3D polyline would have to be decurved first. Needs an ADR like DEC-062 for line heights. **Acceptance:** per-case tests; corpus lines and 3D polylines joined, saved and reopened with their heights. *Done (#428), DEC-063:* a sloped LINE carries its end heights in the model (`heights`, read and written; a level one keeps `elevation`), and BREAK, TRIM, EXTEND, LENGTHEN, FILLET and CHAMFER give an end they move along the line the line's height there, REVERSE swaps them, EXPLODE of a 3D polyline makes lines from vertex to vertex with their heights and LIST shows them (`edit/line-heights.ts`). JOIN with a source 3D polyline takes lines and straight open 2D polylines meeting it in plan and height; several objects at once make a 3D polyline of each chain through a 3D polyline or sloped line; collinear sloped lines on one 3D line make one line; a spline-fit 3D polyline joins as the vertices it draws. Curves joined to a source 3D polyline make a spline only when every height is 0; elsewhere they would make a 3D spline, which PRD non-goal 1 leaves out, so they are discarded from the operation (DEC-063). PEDIT Join takes no curves. Vertical lines (no plan view) still cannot be joined.

## M4 — Annotation at AutoCAD depth

**Exit criteria:** DIMSTYLE/TEXTSTYLE/MLEADERSTYLE/TABLESTYLE managers at parity; all dimension commands; hatch creation; tables editing; centerlines.

- [x] (#429) **M4-01** Dimension engine complete: ordinate, arc length, jogged, jog line, tolerances/limits, alternate units, `DIMTMOVE`/`DIMATFIT`/`DIMTIX`/`DIMSOXD`, user arrow blocks, `DIMTXSTY`, text override, annotative scale; a DIMENSION stored without a `*D` block is drawn and bounded (spatial index, extents) from the block the engine generates (left by M1-35). **Acceptance:** regenerated vs stored match ≥ 99 % on corpus dims. *Done (#429):* most of the list came with M2-10 (#235, #237, #239), M2-30 (#271, #273, #275) and M2-31 (#276, #278): ordinate, arc length and jogged radius dimensions, DIMTOL/DIMLIM, alternate units, the fit DIMVARs, arrowhead blocks, DIMTXSTY, text overrides, and blockless dimensions drawn and bounded. This task added the jog line (`ACAD_DSTYLE_DIMJAG_POSITION` xdata, 389 and the point, read by the engine's `annotation/linear-jog.ts`; our reading of AutoCAD's layout, no corpus file holds one): `@triya/dims` breaks the dimension line where the point falls on it and draws a zigzag whose vertices are the Jog Height Factor (the style's `ACAD_DSTYLE_DIMJAG` 388, 1.5 when absent) × the text height apart, and MOVE, ROTATE, SCALE, MIRROR and COPY carry the point (`carriedDimensionXdata`); an annotative dimension (one with representations) draws at its default representation's annotation scale in place of DIMSCALE (`annotationScaleOf`; the SCALEs are now mapped with their handles), DEC-013/DEC-058 still keeping Triya from making one; `dims-health.corpus.test.ts` asks for ≥ 99 % of the counted dimensions, and all 228 match (DEC-050's 33 legacy ones still counted apart). Making jog lines (DIMJOGLINE) is M4-02's.

Remove), each one undo step, drawn by the dimension engine and kept by an AC1032 save on every readable corpus file (testkit `dims-commands.corpus.test.ts`, 17 dimensions per file). *Part 2 (#436):* DIMEDIT (DED: Home, New with `<>` for the measurement, Rotate, Oblique on linear and aligned dimensions), DIMTEDIT (DIMTED: the text dragged to a new location and kept there; Left/Right/Center as a DIMJUST override with the text home, Left and Right on linear, radial and diameter dimensions only; Home; Angle), DIMOVERRIDE (DOV: DIMVAR names and values until Enter, each checked, filed in the DSTYLE xdata; Clear overrides), DIMSPACE (base dimension, dimensions to space, a value or Auto at twice the base's text height; linear and aligned moved across, angular and arc length taken in or out about the base's centre) and QDIM (Continuous, Staggered, Baseline, Ordinate, Radius, Diameter, datumPoint, Edit, seTtings on the ends and centres of the geometry selected; not associative, as DIMLINEAR's), each one undo step; the corpus gate makes 18 dimensions per file. *Part 3 (#437):* DIMREASSOCIATE (DRE) re-picks each selected dimension's definition points with AutoCAD's prompts by kind (<next> keeps a point; Select object, Select arc, the lines of a 2-line angle, the circle or arc of a radial dimension, an ordinate's feature, an arc length dimension's arc), measured afresh; Triya's dimensions are DIMASSOC 1, so it does not follow the object afterwards, and a re-picked dimension lets go of any association it was read with. A merged cell an insertion falls inside grows, the title across every column stays across, and a merged cell losing rows keeps its text. Each action is one undo step; the edited table saves and reads back (`table-cells.corpus.test.ts`). Rows are inserted and deleted among the data rows only, because Triya draws the title and header rows by their place. *Part 4 (#441):* a press dragged across cells selects them; the cell menu's Background Fill, Alignment (its own menu of the nine alignments) and Borders (all, outside, inside or one side of the selection, a colour and lineweight) and a Text Height option (rows growing to fit) format every cell selected; SCALE scales a table's text heights and cell margins with it (as overrides, so the table draws the same when edited later); a table's grips size it (the corner across from the insertion point its width, the one below its height, the opposite one both, its columns or rows in proportion) and a grip atop each line between columns moves that line, the table keeping its width, while the grid is shown as it would be; rows keep one line of text and columns their margins. Each saves and reads back (`table-cells.corpus.test.ts`). *Part 5 (#445):* TABLESTYLE (TS) opens AutoCAD's Table Style dialog: the styles (all or in use) with the current one, a preview drawn by the table engine and the description; Set Current (CTABLESTYLE, where the drawing keeps it), New... (Create New Table Style: name and Start With, then New Table Style), Modify..., Delete (not Standard, the current style or one in use) and the list's shortcut menu with Rename. New and Modify Table Style set the table direction and, for the Data, Header and Title cell styles, the fill colour, alignment, margins (the style's, one pair as the file holds it), text style, height and colour, and the borders' lineweight, colour and which show (All, Outside, Inside, one side, No Borders); a modified style's tables are drawn again in it at once, rows growing to hold their text. A drawing without table styles lists AutoCAD's Standard, added by the first action. Insert Table's launcher opens the dialog and comes back on the style made current there. MATCHPROP's Table gives destination tables the source's style and draws them again. Everything saves and reads back on every readable corpus file (`table-style-manager.corpus.test.ts`; an R2010+ DWG's own styles lose their alignments and fills in the AC1032 DXF save, as `table-command.corpus.test.ts` found, DEC-047). *Part 6 (#447):* the cells selected show a grip in the middle of each edge of the selection (`tableCellGrips`); a click on one asks for the point it goes to and sizes the selection's column or row at that edge, no smaller than its margins or one line of text, the table growing or shrinking with it (the far edge stays; before a left or top edge the rest of the table, its insertion point too, moves along); Ctrl held with a table's column grip sizes the column before it alone and the table with it, in place of Copy, dragged so while Ctrl is held; and TABLECELL's Paste (Ctrl+V while cells are selected, M4-13) pastes a spreadsheet's cells into the cells from the first one selected. A new hatch goes behind its boundary (the objects its loops lie along, HPDRAWORDER's initial "send beHind boundary"), or where -HATCH DRaw order says (do Not assign, send to Back, bring to Front, bring in front of bounDary), each separate hatch behind its own; `edit/draw-order.ts` now moves objects the change adds too. hatch COlor (a number, a name, ByLayer, ByBlock, Truecolor, COlorbook, or "." the current colour), LAyer (a layer the drawing has, or one the hatch makes, as HPLAYER does) and Transparency (HPTRANSPARENCY, kept in the drawing as SETVAR keeps it). Advanced's Boundary set (New: only the objects selected bound hatches; Everything: what the view shows) and Retain boundary (`edit/hatch-retain.ts`: each picked loop kept as a closed polyline on the current layer, a whole circle as two half circles, a loop with elliptical or spline edges edge by edge since Triya cannot make regions). draW boundary hatches a boundary drawn point by point with PLINE's arcs (AREA's `nextPoints`), each further one an island, keeping the polylines when asked; remove Boundaries takes islands back out of the picked areas (an area's outer boundary stays) and objects out of the selected ones; HATCH offers remove Boundaries once an area is found. The gradient prompts' wording is ours (AutoCAD's help gives none). Saved and read back on every corpus drawing. *Part 4 (#454):* HATCHEDIT (HE) and -HATCHEDIT on the command line with AutoCAD 2024's options, each changing the hatch picked as one undo step: DIsassociate (an associative hatch from a file lets go of its boundary), Style, Properties (a new pattern placed by the library; the same pattern's stored lines turned, scaled and moved, so a file's pattern the library lacks can still change), DRaw order (back, front, or behind or above the objects the hatch's loops lie along, `edit/hatch-separate.ts`), ADd boundaries (pick points or objects, as HATCH finds areas), Remove boundaries (the loops the objects selected lie along; one is always kept), recreate Boundary (each loop as a polyline on the current layer; Region and Reassociate refused), ASsociate refused (DEC-015), separate Hatches (HPSEPARATE on an existing hatch: one hatch per area, an area being an outer loop and the islands directly in it as the island style fills them, each copy just above the original), Origin, LAyer, COlor (background, or a gradient's colours) and Transparency. The Hatch Creation contextual tab is M7-02's (Triya has no ribbon until M7-01; M7-02 lists the Hatch contextual tab). *Part 5 (#460):* the Hatch and Gradient dialog (HATCH's seTtings, and HATCH and GRADIENT at once while HPDLGMODE, the app's per-user variable at AutoCAD's initial 2, says so: Triya has no ribbon) and the Hatch Edit dialog (HATCHEDIT, and a double click on a hatch), `commands/draw/hatch-dialog.ts` and app `HatchDialog.tsx`, with AutoCAD 2024's layout: the Hatch tab (Type Predefined, User defined or Custom; Pattern with the Hatch Pattern Palette of swatches; hatch and background colour; Angle, Scale, Double, Spacing; Hatch origin with Click to set new origin, Default to boundary extents (HPORIGINMODE) and Store as default origin), the Gradient tab (one or two colours, Shade/Tint, the nine gradients, Centered, Angle), Boundaries (Add: Pick points and Select objects close the dialog to pick and open it again; Remove boundaries; Recreate boundary in Hatch Edit; View Selections), Options (Create separate hatches, Draw order, Layer, Transparency), Inherit Properties (with Inherit options' source hatch origin), Preview (Enter accepts the hatch, Esc goes back) and More options (Islands, Retain boundaries, Boundary set New, Gap tolerance). Hatch Edit's OK makes every change in one undo step. Annotative (DEC-013, DEC-058) and Associative (DEC-015) show unchecked and unavailable. The Hatch Creation and Hatch Editor contextual tabs are M7-02's; the Properties palette's hatch Background color row is M4-18.

(TABLECELL DataFormat) opens AutoCAD's Table Cell Format dialog on the first cell selected (Data type: Angle, Currency, Date, Decimal Number, General, Percentage, Point, Text, Whole Number; Preview; Format, Precision, Symbol and Append symbol, the date format and its Examples, as the type offers them); OK gives every text cell selected the type (one undo step, rows growing to fit): a value of the type's kind is kept and shown again, else the cell's text is read as the type, text that does not read staying text with the format kept; text typed into a cell is read by its data format (`12.5` in a Currency cell is `$12.50`). (Angle, Currency, Decimal Number, Percentage, Point): AutoCAD's Additional Format dialog with Current value, Conversion factor (`%ct8[f]`, applied to what is shown, the value kept), Additional text (`%ps`, greyed for Currency and Percentage, whose symbol it is), Number separators (`%ds`, `%th`) and Zero suppression (Leading, Trailing, `%zs` 4 / 8); text typed into such a cell is read with its prefix, suffix and separators taken off; saved and read back on A35 (`table-cells.corpus.test.ts`). AutoCAD also keeps the picked table's structure and contents in the style as a template for new tables (Remove Table drops it); a DXF table style has no place Triya writes for it, so Remove Table is greyed. What is left (title and header rows inserted or deleted, other cell styles for a new table's first rows, cell styles of the drafter's own on rows and cells, border linetypes and double lines, locking, table breaks) needs each row's and cell's cell style, lock and the table's break settings saved, which an AC1032 DXF states only in the table's AutoCAD 2008 data (the TABLECONTENT object its `ACAD_ROUNDTRIP_2008_TABLE_ENTITY` record names) that Triya writes back raw and never for a new table: M4-28 first.

Properties lists marks as Center Mark or Centerline with Position X/Y, a mark's Rotation, Cross size and Cross gap (a length or a factor with `x`), Show extensions, Left/Right/Top/Bottom extension, a centerline's Start and End extension, and Associative (read-only); edits redraw the mark as CENTERRESET does. CENTERLINE on polyline segments moved to M4-29 (needs-human #482).
- [ ] **M4-25** `tests/e2e/ui/undo-views.spec.ts` (undo of zoom and pan) flakes (found by M4-18, #473: failed CI shard 3/3 once and passed on rerun; fails in the sandbox on `main`'s code too). **Acceptance:** the spec passes 20 times in a row with `--repeat-each=20` at the CI worker count, with the cause found and fixed rather than the wait lengthened. *Why:* an untracked flake in a required check costs every PR a rerun.
- [ ] **M4-26** Type-ahead into the MTEXT editor and TEXTEDIT (found by M4-20, #475): keys typed while MTEXT's in-place editor opens after the opposite corner, or while TEXTEDIT's line editor opens on a picked TEXT, are still held by the command line and replayed there, as TEXT's were before M4-20. **Acceptance:** a Playwright test types the contents ahead of each editor (`typeAhead` in `tests/e2e/ui/text-editor.spec.ts`) and finds them in it. *Why:* M4-20 took the held keys for TEXT's line editor placing new lines only; MTEXT's Enter is a paragraph break, so its keys need the editor's own replay (`cellAhead`'s, M4-05 part 7, is close).
- [ ] **M4-27** `tests/e2e/ui/user-fonts.spec.ts:47` ("a dropped TrueType file draws the style that names it, and stays after a reload") flakes (found by M4-20, #475: failed CI shard 3/3 once; locally 2 of 10 fail with two workers on `main`'s code): after Settings' Remove Arial.TTF the canvas keeps drawing the filled font (ink 133540482, below 16089273 expected) for the 15 s the spec polls. **Acceptance:** the spec passes 20 times in a row with `--repeat-each=20` at the CI worker count, with the cause found (a removed font still cached by a renderer, or the reload's font arriving after the removal) rather than the wait lengthened. *Why:* an untracked flake in a required check.
- [x] (#483) **M4-28** Write an ACAD_TABLE's AutoCAD 2008 table data in DXF saves (found by M4-16 part 4, #479): AutoCAD's AC1032 DXF keeps what the legacy ACAD_TABLE codes cannot state (each row's, column's and cell's cell style, cell locks, border linetypes and double lines, table breaks) in a TABLECONTENT object named by the table's `ACAD_ROUNDTRIP_2008_TABLE_ENTITY` XRECORD (acadsharp `sample_AC1032_ascii.dxf`, table A35); acad-ts reads and writes it raw (patch 0070), so Triya cannot read those values from a DXF, an edited table keeps the copy as read (DEC-040: Triya edits tables through the ACAD_TABLE entity), and a new table has none. Map the TABLECONTENT (rows, columns, cells, cell styles, contents) into acad-ts's table fields on DXF read and write it from them, for edited and new tables. *Why:* M4-16's title/header rows, row cell styles, locking, border linetypes and breaks all need it. **Acceptance:** every corpus DXF table round-trips with zero structural diff; a -TABLE table saved as DXF carries a TABLECONTENT whose rows name their `_TITLE`/`_HEADER`/`_DATA` cell styles, read back by acad-ts and by ezdxf's audit. *Done (#483):* `@triya/io` reads a TABLECONTENT's codes into acad-ts's table content classes (columns, rows, cells, contents and attributes, custom data, cell styles with their borders, merged ranges, the style; map/table-content.ts) and writes them back, code for code on every corpus TABLECONTENT (`table-content.corpus.test.ts`, 14 files; one from a DXF before R2007 with its values framed as R2007 frames them); a new table (TABLE, -TABLE, COPY, PASTECLIP) saved as DXF gets the `ACAD_XREC_ROUNDTRIP` XRECORD in its extension dictionary, laid out as AutoCAD's, and a TABLECONTENT holding the content a DWG from R2010 states for it (rows on the table style's `_TITLE`/`_HEADER`/`_DATA` cell styles, taken from its CELLSTYLEMAP in a DXF; cell formats in cell styles; merges as ranges), read back by acad-ts and passing ezdxf's audit on every table save (DEC-067; acad-ts 0185 exports the object class). No TABLEGEOMETRY is written for a new table (DEC-067 item 3). A table the drawing came with keeps its TABLECONTENT as read: reading it into the table on open and writing it back from the edited table is M4-30.
- [ ] **M4-29** CENTERLINE on polyline straight segments (left by M4-24, #481): AutoCAD 2024's CENTERLINE also takes a polyline's straight segments, and the centerline follows the segment. **Acceptance:** engine command tests and a DXF save that reads the association back. *Blocked:* the segment's index needs a place in the `TRIYA_CENTERMARK` record of DEC-066 (CLAUDE.md stop condition 5), needs-human #482; build only the option the owner picks. *Why:* the M4-08 roadmap text's last item.
- [ ] **M4-30** Read a DXF table's AutoCAD 2008 data on open and write it back from the edited table (left by M4-28, #483): the TABLECONTENT reader of M4-28 runs on open, so each row's, column's and cell's cell style id, cell locks, custom data, border linetypes and double lines of a DXF table reach the table model as they do from an R2010+ DWG, and an edited table's TABLECONTENT is written again from the edited table (rows and columns inserted or deleted by their origins, cell values and formats), keeping what Triya does not model as read; the stale `ACAD_ROUNDTRIP_2008_CELL_CHECKSUM` custom data of an edited cell is left out (its checksum is not published). *Why:* M4-16's title/header rows, row cell styles, locking and border linetypes must read back from Triya's own DXF saves and survive an edit of a table the drawing came with. **Acceptance:** every corpus DXF table round-trips with zero structural diff; an edited corpus table (A35) saved as DXF reads back with its TABLECONTENT matching the edit and passes ezdxf's audit.

**Exit criteria:** block authoring, attribute management, xref/image management, layer manager with states/filters/VP overrides, linetype loading, purge, templates.

- [ ] **M5-01** BLOCK/-BLOCK/WBLOCK dialogs, INSERT dialog (file/drawing block list, scale/rotation/explode/repeat, unit scaling), base point, nested insert. **Acceptance:** block scenario tests.
- [ ] **M5-02** BEDIT block editor (in-place edit of definition with attribute defs), REFEDIT/REFCLOSE for blocks and xrefs (xref as save-to-file), ATTSYNC. **Acceptance:** redefinition propagates to all inserts; round-trip clean.
- [ ] **M5-03** ATTDEF dialog, BATTMAN, ATTDISP, attribute flags; EATTEDIT complete. **Acceptance:** attribute matrix tests.
- [ ] **M5-04** XREF manager palette: attach/overlay/detach/reload/unload/bind (bind/insert), path display, nested tree, XCLIP command, image attach (IMAGEATTACH, IMAGECLIP, IMAGEADJUST, IMAGEFRAME), PDF underlay attach (PDFATTACH, scale calibration, clip). *From M2-22 (#249):* Insert > Reference's "Snap to Underlays" toggle (PDFOSNAP, now in Settings > Drafting only). *From M2-24 part 2 (#260):* IMAGEFRAME 2 ("displayed but not plotted"): acad-ts reads RASTERVARIABLES 70 as on/off (`isDisplayFrameShown`), so 2 reads as on, is saved as 1 and plots; keep the number as patch 0176 does for WIPEOUTVARIABLES and leave image frames off the plot (`imageFrames(model, 'plot')`). *From M2-32 part 3 (#280):* PASTECLIP of an xref reference (refused now: the save cannot write a new xref BLOCK_RECORD and its path, which attach brings), as AutoCAD pastes one attached. **Acceptance:** bind produces a self-contained DXF that round-trips and passes the ezdxf audit (DEC-047).
- [ ] **M5-05** Layer Properties Manager complete: inline edit, multi-select, search, columns, VP freeze/overrides, transparency, plot, description, new/delete/merge, current. **Acceptance:** Playwright on 200-layer corpus file; performance < 100 ms filter. *Added from M2-36 (#287):* the manager's changes are one undo step while UNDO Control Layer is on (the setting is kept already).
- [ ] **M5-06** Layer tool row commands (LAYISO w/ fade, LAYUNISO, LAYOFF/ON, LAYFRZ/THW, LAYLCK/ULK, LAYMCUR, LAYMCH, LAYWALK, LAYERP, LAYDEL, LAYMRG, LAYCUR). **Acceptance:** command tests.
- [ ] **M5-07** Layer states (save/restore/edit/export/import `.las`) and filters (property/group/invert) stored in AutoCAD dictionaries. **Acceptance:** round-trip of states/filters through acad-ts. *From M2-47 (#327):* the View Manager's Update Layers and the New View dialog's "Save layer snapshot with view", AutoCAD's layer state `ACAD_VIEWS_<view name>` named by DXF 302 of the view's `ADSK_XREC_VTRVIEWINFO` XRECORD, restored with the view.
- [ ] **M5-08** LINETYPE dialog + loader (user `.lin` drop, bundled set), LWEIGHT dialog, `LTSCALE` commands. **Acceptance:** complex linetype load test; Playwright: Other… in the Properties palette's Linetype list loads a linetype from a user .lin and applies it to the selection (from M2-35). *From M2-20 part 2 (#256):* the user's .lin files are already kept (`linetypes` in `apps/web/src/support-files.ts`, OPFS `linetypes/`, dropped or added in Settings > Linetypes); the loader reads them with `SupportFileStore.file`. *From M2-35 (#366):* the Properties palette's Linetype list (Quick Properties' too, and the current linetype with nothing selected) ends with AutoCAD's "Other…", which opens the Linetype Manager to load a linetype (bundled or from the user's .lin files) and, once loaded, applies it to the selection or makes it current (CELTYPE); until then the list offers only the drawing's linetypes. *From M4-08 part 3:* CENTERMARK and CENTERLINE load CENTERLTYPE (CENTER2) from the bundled set when the drawing lacks it (today the mark is drawn ByLayer and says so).

The public samples' PDFUNDERLAY names `..\pdf-definition.pdf`, which is not shipped, so M1-20's acceptance ("underlay corpus file renders") cannot be met and the page placement, clip and adjustment rules AutoCAD leaves undocumented cannot be checked (needs-human, issue #103). (blocks M1-20) *(Deferred post-launch by DEC-047; only if users ask.)*
- [-] **M1-74** acad-ts: read and write the DWG `LARGE_RADIAL_DIMENSION` object as `DimensionRadialLarge` (patch 0107 reads it from DXF only). **Acceptance:** a DWG sample with a jogged radius dimension maps like the same drawing saved as DXF, and its DWG round trip stays clean. *Why:* found by M1-34; the corpus has no DWG with one, so the layout cannot be checked yet, and DWG ones stay unmapped (kept raw). *(Deferred post-launch by DEC-047; only if users ask.)*
- [-] **M1-78** Bundled stand-ins for AutoCAD's ASCII-mapped Greek and symbol fonts (`greeks`, `greekc`, `syastro`, `symath`, `symap`, `symeteo`, `symusic`): Hershey has Greek Simplex and Complex and many symbol glyphs to draw them with, but which key each sits on in AutoCAD's fonts is only shown in AutoCAD's font sample charts, which are pictures. Needs those key layouts verified, from the owner's AutoCAD (a TEXT of every printable ASCII character in each font, plotted with the M1-26 reference plots), before the catalog maps them; then `SHX_EQUIVALENTS` serves each name. **Acceptance:** each stand-in's layout matches its reference plot within tolerance, and `bundled-fonts.test.ts` checks every printable ASCII key draws. (blocked-by M1-26) *Why:* split from M1-59 (#125): Hershey's own mapped Greek files use an alphabetical layout that may not match AutoCAD's, and guessing a symbol font's layout would draw the wrong symbol without any warning. *(Deferred post-launch by DEC-047; only if users ask.)*
- [-] **M1-81** `AcadToTriya` table border overrides: the table-wide border colour, lineweight and visibility overrides (DXF 94–96 and the values after them, DWG R2004/R2007 per row type and grid line, R2010+ the table and row cell styles' borders) and the DXF cell edge visibility codes (289/285/286/288), which acad-ts does not read. **Acceptance:** they compare equal in `entities.corpus.test.ts` on a corpus table that states them, and DWG matches its DXF save. (blocked-by M1-26: no corpus file states a table border override or a hidden cell edge, so the DXF value order and the visibility convention cannot be checked) *Why:* split from M1-77, whose corpus sample states only margins and text heights; acad-ts still writes 94–96 back as read (DEC-043). *(Deferred post-launch by DEC-047; only if users ask.)*
- [-] **M1-99** Table style user cell styles: map an R2010+ table style's user cell styles (DWG ids 101+, and the same in a DXF CELLSTYLEMAP) and each named cell style's own margins and merge flags into `TableStyleRecord`, and write them. **Acceptance:** a sample with a user cell style maps alike from its DWG and DXF saves and its edits save on both. (blocked-by M1-26: no public corpus file has a user cell style) *Why:* split from M1-96, whose record holds the three rows only. *(Deferred post-launch by DEC-047; only if users ask.)*
- [-] **M1-101** DWG MLINESTYLE element linetypes before R2018: an element names its linetype by an index (0x7FFF ByLayer, 0x7FFE ByBlock), which acad-ts reads and writes (patch 0049) as the linetype's place in its own table, ByLayer and ByBlock counted, while a DWG's LTYPE_CONTROL lists them apart; check the index base on an AutoCAD-saved R2013 DWG whose MLINE style has an element on a named linetype (and its DXF save), fix acad-ts if it differs, and lift the save's refusal of such an element (M1-75 part 31). **Acceptance:** the sample's style maps alike from DWG and DXF, and an edited element linetype saves to an R2013 DWG and reads back in AutoCAD. (blocked-by M1-26: no public DWG before R2018 has an MLINE style element on a named linetype) *Why:* found by M1-75 part 31; saving such an element now could name the wrong linetype in AutoCAD. *(Deferred post-launch by DEC-047; only if users ask.)*
- [-] **M1-102** Annotative representations beyond TEXT: map and write the context data of annotative MTEXT (`ACDB_MTEXTOBJECTCONTEXTDATA_CLASS`), dimensions (`ACDB_ALDIMOBJECTCONTEXTDATA_CLASS` and the other dimension types), block references (`ACDB_BLKREFOBJECTCONTEXTDATA_CLASS`), hatches, leaders, MULTILEADERs and multiline attributes, so edits of those entities keep every scale in step as M1-75 part 37 does for TEXT (acad-ts types only block reference, MTEXT attribute and MULTILEADER context data, partly). **Acceptance:** `annotative-sheet.dwg` (`TESTING.md` §2.1) maps alike from its DWG and DXF saves, MOVE and grip edits of each annotative entity save and read back, and AutoCAD shows each scale where Triya put it. (blocked-by M1-26: no public corpus file has an annotative MTEXT, dimension, block reference, hatch, leader or MULTILEADER with a representation; ACadSharp's samples hold only annotative TEXT) *Why:* split from M1-75 part 37, which refuses shape changes of these entities until their representations are written. *(Deferred post-launch by DEC-047; only if users ask.)*
- [-] **M2-53** Dimension style true colours on paste (found by M2-32 part 3, #280): PASTECLIP drops a DIMSTYLE's DIMCLRD/DIMCLRE/DIMCLRT/DIMTFILLCLR true colours (`clipRecord`), keeping the ACI AutoCAD derives for each, because only an R2004+ DWG stores them (`trueColorProblems` in `dim-styles.ts`) and a save is DXF only. **Acceptance:** with DWG save, a style pasted from a DWG keeps its true colours through the save. *(Post-launch by DEC-047: needs DWG save.)*
